### fix(correction): accumulate delivered actions across correction passes — no more duplicate Telegram replies  <!-- 2026-08-09 -->
**Why (CHANGELOG 2026-06-26, still open):** a first correction delivered `message_*`, a non-message action (e.g. `use_animation`) failed, and the second correction re-emitted and re-delivered the same reply.
**What:**
- **Root-cause fix:** `core/message_chain.py::_merge_correction_successes()` now accumulates `successful_actions`/`successful_types` across correction passes (deduped, order-stable) at both `correction_context` build sites. Previously the context was rebuilt from the *current* pass only, so after a pass where nothing but the failed action ran, `successful_types` became empty and the retry filter stopped stripping the re-emitted message.
- **Defence in depth:** the retry filter (message_chain.py:3062) suppresses **all** `message_*` action types when any message type already delivered (structural type-prefix match — a duplicate delivery is the worst failure mode).
- **Prompt:** the PARTIAL SUCCESS correction prompt now states a reply was already delivered, forbids ANY `message_*` action, and drops the contradictory "Ensure you've created ALL actions" requirement when a message already sent.
**Location:** `core/message_chain.py` (`_merge_correction_successes`, both `correction_context` build sites, retry filter), `core/transport_layer.py` (`run_corrector_middleware` PARTIAL SUCCESS branch). Tests: new `tests/test_corrector_no_duplicate_message.py` (reproduced `['REPLY', 'REPLY']` before the fix, `['REPLY']` after).
**Status:** done. `ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean; scoped `ty check` clean; `uv run pytest tests/test_corrector_no_duplicate_message.py tests/test_corrector_on_top_level_message.py` → 5 passed.

### chore(changelog): knock out open known-issue entries — FakeBuilder stub, charmap console logging, stale fixed entries  <!-- 2026-08-09 -->
**Why:** Triage of the 18 "known, not fixed" CHANGELOG entries found 3 quick wins (all small/mechanical) plus 4 entries whose fixes were already in the code but never recorded.
**What:**
- **Fixed — `tests/test_chat_attention_triggers.py` FakeBuilder** (entry 2026-07-03): both `FakeBuilder` stubs now carry chainable passthroughs for the full `get_updates_*` set the real builder uses (`get_updates_connection_pool_size`, `get_updates_pool_timeout`, `get_updates_connect_timeout`, `get_updates_read_timeout`, `get_updates_write_timeout`). `test_start_bot_failure_resets_state_and_schedules_retry` + `test_start_bot_retries_transient_timeout_inline` pass again (4 passed).
- **Fixed — Windows host `charmap` console logging noise** (entry 2026-07-08): the console `StreamHandler` in `core/logging_utils.py` is now `_SafeConsoleStreamHandler`, a subclass that re-encodes the formatted line with `errors="replace"` on `UnicodeEncodeError` (uses the stream's declared encoding), so emoji/✓ render as `?` on cp1252 terminals instead of producing `--- Logging error ---` tracebacks. File handlers were already UTF-8; the Linux container never hits the fallback.

**Location:** `tests/test_chat_attention_triggers.py`, `core/logging_utils.py`, `CHANGELOG.md` (7 entries updated).
**Status:** done. `ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean; scoped `ty check` clean; `uv run pytest tests/test_chat_attention_triggers.py tests/test_corrector_on_top_level_message.py tests/test_logging_utils.py tests/test_logging_fallback.py` → 6 passed, 1 pre-existing failure (`test_timestamped_rotation_respects_backupcount`, fails identically on clean tree — rotation files accumulate in the live `logs/` dir across runs). Console-handler behaviour verified with a simulated cp1252 stream (emoji → `?`, no crash).

### fix(vessel): quantity-aware goal completion + goal material deficit cue + re-enable plan-expansion Drone  <!-- 2026-08-08 -->
**Why (user):** *"I think right now it can't tell what is in its inventory, I asked it to make me a wooden pickaxe and it just tried to craft and drop it without any prerequisites"* — then, after the correction-prompt fix, *"it's more active now but it's just running around"*. Live-trace investigation showed three compounding root causes for the wandering:
1. **Correction prompts were blind** (fixed earlier in this chain: the corrector now embeds the live world state — `core/transport_layer.py::_render_vessel_world_state_block`).
2. **The goal debrief auto-completed multi-part goals from a single ingredient** — a cottage goal ("gather wood, craft a door and a torch, build walls") was declared done the moment one `collect_block oak_log` history row matched a derived raw-material target, so the will beat re-authored the same goal every ~60 s.
3. **`VESSEL_GOAL_EXPAND_ENABLED` was `false` in the runtime DB** — the Goal Plan Expansion Drone that writes ordered `steps` plans never ran, so every goal stayed stepless (`steps=None`) and the motor had nothing concrete to chase.

**What (Minecraft adapter + core):**
- **Quantity-aware auto-completion.** `target_names.py` gains `derive_quantity` / `derive_product_quantities` (structural number scan: "20 oak logs", "oak logs x20", "a stack of X" = 64, "2 stacks of X" = 128; plural-tolerant whole-word needles). Both debrief hooks now require the **stated count**: `evaluate_goal_completion` compares inventory counts, `evaluate_goal_completion_from_history` **sums each row's `_result.data`** (`collected`/`count`, default 1 for older rows). "Gather 20 oak logs" no longer completes on one log.
- **Multi-part goals need ALL their products.** A goal naming several products is satisfied only when every named product is present (inventory) or evidenced by a successful craft/smelt (history) at its stated count; a derived raw-material target never completes a product-naming goal. Stepped goals (non-empty `steps`) are never auto-completed by the debrief — their plan owns progression.
- **Goal material deficit cue.** `get_world_state` now computes `extra["goal_deficit"]` (`{items: [{item, have, need}]}` for the active goal's named products/targets vs the live inventory), rendered into the will/action beats by `vessel_beat.py::_fmt_goal_deficit` — so Synth sees "your goal still needs 5/20 oak_log" and picks the concrete gather step instead of drifting.
- **Re-enabled the plan-expansion Drone** (`VESSEL_GOAL_EXPAND_ENABLED` → `true` in the runtime DB; code default was already `True`), so goals actually get `steps` plans again.

**Location:** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/target_names.py` (`derive_quantity`/`derive_product_quantities`/`_match_needles`), `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`evaluate_goal_completion`, `evaluate_goal_completion_from_history`, `_row_result_count`, `_compute_goal_deficit`, `goal_deficit` extra), `core/vessel_beat.py` (`_fmt_goal_deficit` + will/action prompt wiring). Tests: `tests/test_vessel_goal_debrief.py` (+14 quantity/deficit tests), `tests/test_vessel_beat.py` (+4 renderer tests).
**Status:** code + tests done. `ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean; scoped `ty check` clean on all edited Python files; `uv run pytest tests/test_vessel_goal_debrief.py tests/test_vessel_beat.py tests/test_vessel_whitelist.py tests/test_corrector_vessel_world_state.py` → **165 passed**; `tests/test_vessel_reactive_actions.py tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py` → **68 passed**. Runtime behaviour requires a container rebuild/restart (bridge JS + Python are COPY'd at build time; the running bridge predates the auto-place edit).


**Why (user):** the prompt actually sent to the Iris vision engine on the inbound-message path was hardcoded in `plugin_instance.py`, so the `IRIS_DEFAULT_PROMPT` config had no effect there — a WebUI change didn't grab.
**What (WebUI + core):** the Iris section of the Engines tab now has a **Vision prompt** box that edits `IRIS_DEFAULT_PROMPT` (persisted via `/api/config` and exposed to the components payload as `iris_default_prompt`). The inbound-message vision path no longer passes a hardcoded prompt — it falls back to the configured value via `describe_media`. The former hardcoded plain-text instruction is now the registered default for `IRIS_DEFAULT_PROMPT`, preserving the "no JSON/structured output" guard for installs that haven't customized it.
**Location:** `core/plugin_instance.py` (drop hardcoded prompt), `plugins/iris_plugin/iris_plugin.py` (default prompt + module constant), `core/webui.py` (payload), `core/webui_templates/sections/engines.html` + `res/synth_webui/js/main.js` (prompt box), `tests/test_iris.py` (`test_iris_plugin_uses_configured_default_prompt`).
**Status:** code + tests done. `ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean; scoped `ty check` clean (no new diagnostics); `uv run pytest tests/test_iris.py::test_iris_plugin_uses_configured_default_prompt tests/test_iris_response_validation.py` → **7 passed**; `node --check main.js` clean. WebUI verification pending a `docker compose up -d --build`.

### feat(webui): structured engine-config editor + named provider/model presets  <!-- 2026-08-07 -->
**Why (user, IT):** *"could you prettify the engine configuration json? … I use 2 models from the same provider, and one model can only do 20 tool calls, the other can do more but it has tighter guardrails, so I have to swap between them … editing the json config in the webui is a pain"* — the per-endpoint `extra_config` was a free-form JSON textarea in the Engines tab, and there was no way to save a configuration and switch back to it later.
**What (WebUI + new backend module):**
1. **Structured editor instead of the JSON textarea.** The Cortex engine-config block now renders the known `extra_config` keys as toggle switches (`enable_thinking`, `enable_tools`, `enable_tools_parallel`, `disable_tools`, `force_action_grammar`, `force_json_object`, `retry_on_timeout`, `retry_on_empty`) and value boxes (`max_tools`, `max_tokens`, `timeout`, `retry_attempts`, `retry_backoff`, `downstream_char_budget`). A collapsible **Advanced / raw keys** section keeps `grammar`, `response_format`, and any provider-specific keys (e.g. `venice_parameters`) as JSON textareas so nothing is lost. Empty number fields keep the engine default (key omitted on save).
2. **Named presets (provider/model specific).** A preset bar on top of the editor lets you **＋ Save current as preset** (name + the current model + the current config), **Apply** a selected preset to the active endpoint in one click (swaps `extra_config` — preserving only the `provider_id` bookkeeping key — and, when the preset carries a model, switches the live engine's model via `set_current_model`), and **Delete** presets. Presets are stored as a JSON list under the hidden `ENGINE_CONFIG_PRESETS` config key (no schema migration).
**Location (new):** `core/engine_config_presets.py` (preset CRUD + apply). **Edited:** `core/config.py` (hidden `ENGINE_CONFIG_PRESETS` var); `core/webui.py` (4 new endpoints: `GET`/`POST`/`DELETE /api/engine-config-presets`, `POST /api/engine-config-presets/apply`); `core/webui_templates/sections/engines.html` (structured form + preset bar); `res/synth_webui/js/main.js` (field schema render/collect + preset handlers, replacing the inline JSON editor in `renderForCortex`).
**Status:** code + tests done. `ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean; scoped `ty check` clean (new module + test; `core/config.py`/`core/webui.py` additions add no new diagnostics); `uv run pytest tests/test_engine_config_presets.py` → **10 passed**; `node --check main.js` clean. WebUI verification pending a `docker compose up -d --build` (the `synth` container COPYs the code at build time — not bind-mounted).

### refactor(message-queue): restructure global priority scale — dedicated Radio and Background bands  <!-- 2026-08-07 -->
**Why (user, IT):** *"metti nel changelog sta cosa che vediamo come va, in caso se vediamo che sta nuova priorità fa schifo la possiamo cambiare ancora"* — the radio_host banter was starved because it used `PRIORITY_LOW` (3) while ordinary chat used `PRIORITY_GENERAL` (6). The single consumer always picked chat first, so banter's 60s polling timeout expired before it ever dequeued. Continuous Logchat traffic made the starvation permanent.
**What (core + all callers):** Introduced a new, flatter priority scale with dedicated bands so each category of autonomous traffic has its own unambiguous rung:

| Constant | Value | Band | Behavior |
|----------|-------|------|----------|
| `PRIORITY_EMERGENCY` | 11 | Emergency | Always first |
| `PRIORITY_URGENT` | 10 | Urgent (incl. calendar reminders) | Always first |
| `PRIORITY_REFLECTION` | 9 | Vessel reflection pause | Above player chat |
| `PRIORITY_HIGH` | 8 | Direct human input (player chat) | Above general chat |
| `PRIORITY_TRAINER` | 7 | Trainer | Above general chat |
| `PRIORITY_RADIO` | 6 | Radio banter **(NEW)** | Above general chat, **blocking** |
| `PRIORITY_GENERAL` | 5 | Ordinary chat (was 6) | Normal user traffic |
| `PRIORITY_AMBIENT` | 4 | Autonomous vessel perceptions | Below all humans |
| `PRIORITY_LOW` | 3 | Low-priority (background threshold) | Non-blocking |
| `PRIORITY_BACKGROUND` | 2 | Grillo beats **(NEW)** | Absolute bottom, never blocking |

Key changes:
- **`enqueue_low_priority()` now accepts an optional `priority` parameter** so callers choose their band instead of being hardcoded to `PRIORITY_LOW`.
- **Grillo beats (×3 callers)** → `PRIORITY_BACKGROUND` (2, absolute bottom — sleeps below everything, never blocks the consumer).
- **Calendar reminders (×2 callers)** → `PRIORITY_URGENT` (10) so time-sensitive notifications arrive promptly.
- **Radio banter** → `PRIORITY_RADIO` (6, above ordinary chat at 5) so banter generation is never starved by chat traffic.
- **Ordinary chat** dropped from 6 → 5 so Radio can sit above it without disrupting the URGENT/HIGH/TRAINER bands.

**Tradeoff (explicit, reversible):** Radio banter at priority 6 is **above the background threshold** (`PRIORITY_LOW` = 3), meaning a 30–90s banter generation with the Selenium engine will **briefly block** the consumer. Chat at 5 yields to radio. If this causes unacceptable chat latency, Radio can be dropped back to 5 or below; the `priority` kwarg makes re-tuning a one-line change per caller.
**Location (edited):** `core/message_queue.py` (constants + labels + `enqueue_low_priority` signature); `plugins/grillo/grillo_impl.py`; `plugins/grillo/grillo_chat_observer/grillo_chat_observer.py`; `plugins/grillo/grillo_dream/grillo_dream.py`; `plugins/event_plugin/event_plugin.py` (×2); `plugins/radio_host/radio_host_plugin.py`. Tests: `tests/test_grillo_enqueue.py` (3 mock sigs), `tests/test_grillo_logging.py` (2 mock sigs), `tests/test_grillo_observer.py` (5 mock sigs).
**Status:** code + tests + docs done. `ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean; scoped pytest → 34/36 pass (2 pre-existing DB-connection failures unrelated). In-world verification pending `docker compose up -d --build`. If the new priority scale proves problematic, revert or re-tune — the `priority` kwarg makes it cheap.

### feat(vessel): always-on staticity ward — relocate the body when it lingers parked too long  <!-- 2026-08-09 -->
**Why (user, IT):** *"mettiamo un ward di staticità: se il synth è fermo per troppo tempo in un punto lo facciamo muovere altrove"* — the existing tick-to-tick stuck-body watchdog (`_stuck_position_ticks`) only runs *after* the `if not goal` early-return and only while the motor is actively driving, so a **goalless** body — or one endlessly `mine`/`use`-ing an in-reach block without displacing — could stay pinned in one spot forever.
**What (Minecraft adapter scope; purely positional/numeric, no keyword logic, fail-safe):** `MinecraftConnector._update_staticity_ward(position)` runs **first** each motor tick, right after the survival guard and *before* the `no goal` early-return. It tracks a moving anchor (`_static_anchor` x/z) + an idle counter (`_static_ward_ticks`): staying within `_static_ward_radius` of the anchor accrues ticks; stepping outside re-anchors and zeroes the counter (so genuine travel never trips it). After `_static_ward_limit` consecutive idle ticks it fires — `motor_step` rotates `_explore_heading` by `_EXPLORE_TURN_RAD` and `goto`s a fresh reprojected waypoint (fallback `wander`), returning `{"acted": True, "action": "goto"|"wander", "reason": "staticity_ward"}` — then re-arms the anchor on the current spot. A bad/missing position resets tracking and never fires. Broader than the tick-to-tick watchdog: it also catches a goalless or physically-inert body the other watchdog misses.
**Scope (AGENTS.md Scope rule):** "staying still" is universal, but coordinate detection + `goto`/`wander` relocation are Minecraft-specific → the ward is a Minecraft-adapter motor reflex (the base `VesselConnectorBase.motor_step` no-op is unchanged, so worlds without motorics degrade gracefully).
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`_STATIC_WARD_RADIUS`/`_STATIC_WARD_TICKS` constants, `__init__` ward attrs, config-load block, `_update_staticity_ward`, `motor_step` call site); `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` (`VESSEL_STATICITY_WARD_ENABLED`/`VESSEL_STATICITY_TICKS`/`VESSEL_STATICITY_RADIUS` registration); `AGENTS.md` §5c/§14. Tests: `tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py` (7 new ward tests — 5 pure-logic + 2 async motor_step).
**Config:** `VESSEL_STATICITY_WARD_ENABLED` (bool, default `True`); `VESSEL_STATICITY_TICKS` (int, default 8 ≈ 24 s at the ~3 s motor tick, clamp `[2, 1000]`); `VESSEL_STATICITY_RADIUS` (float, default 2.0, clamp `[0.5, 32.0]`). All `group="plugins"`, `component="vessel_plugin"`, `advanced=True`.
**Status:** code + tests + docs done. `uv run ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean; scoped `ty check plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` clean; `uv run pytest tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py` → **64 passed**. In-world verification pending a `docker compose up -d --build synth` (the `synth` container COPYs the code at build time — not bind-mounted).
**Notes:** the ward re-arms on the current spot after firing, so with a *static* fake position it fires periodically (every `_static_ward_limit` idle ticks) rather than on every subsequent tick — the async test asserts `any(reason == "staticity_ward")` over the loop, not on the final tick.

### feat(vessel): goal debrief also confirms completion from in-world *history* (place/mine/kill/craft), not just held items  <!-- 2026-08-08 -->
**Why (user, IT):** *"ok allor aggiungilo nel debrief"* — the fast goal debrief only auto-completed a goal when its target was *held/visible* in the live inventory/world-state. Many goals leave **no lasting inventory trace** (placing a block, killing a mob, crafting-then-consuming, saying something), so those goals sat `active` forever and Synth never declared them done.
**What (Minecraft adapter + Rift Vessel core hook; structural id-only, no keyword logic):** after the fast inventory/world-state check fails to satisfy the goal, the debrief additionally consults the session's own `vessel_activity_log` and auto-completes the goal when a **successful action actually taken this session** structurally matches the goal's concrete target: place/mine/collect a block, attack/shoot a mob, or craft/smelt an item. Matching is purely id-based on the logged target ids (`_HISTORY_TARGET_KEYS = ("target","name","item")`), never a text parse; fully fail-safe (loader error → unsatisfied). Gated by the new `VESSEL_GOAL_DEBRIEF_USE_HISTORY` config key (default `True`).
**Scope (AGENTS.md Scope rule):** the *hook* `VesselConnectorBase.evaluate_goal_completion_from_history` is Rift Vessel core (world-agnostic, default returns unsatisfied); the *content* (event-class → target-kind mapping, Minecraft ids) lives in the adapter.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`evaluate_goal_completion_from_history` + `_HISTORY_*` constants); `interface/vessel_interface.py` (history-check branch B2, gated on `vgd.is_debrief_history_enabled`); `core/vessel_goal_debrief.py` (`is_debrief_history_enabled` helper); `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_base.py` (base hook); `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` (`VESSEL_GOAL_DEBRIEF_USE_HISTORY` config registration); `AGENTS.md` §5c/§14. Tests: `tests/test_vessel_goal_debrief.py` (9 async adapter tests + 3 helper tests).
**Status:** code + tests done. `uv run ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean; scoped `ty check` clean; `uv run pytest tests/test_vessel_goal_debrief.py` → **30 passed**. In-world verification pending a `docker compose up -d --build synth` (code is baked at build time, not bind-mounted).
**Notes:** `load_activity_rows` is imported *inside* the method — tests patch `core.vessel_diary_compactor.load_activity_rows`. Column order is hardcoded in `load_activity_rows` to dodge the `PostgresCompatCursor has no attribute 'description'` gap on `vessel_activity_log` reads.

### feat(vessel): morning surface-exit reflex + fully-closed prefab house  <!-- 2026-08-08 -->
**Why (user, IT):** (1) *"alla mattina il synth se ha scavato un bunker in assenza di una base riesca a scavarsi una scala in superficie… un blocco si e un blocco no in modo da saltarci sopra"* — an overnight dig-in/wall-in bunker with no real base left Synth buried at dawn. (2) *"la casa che ha costruto non è una casa chiusa… basta un cubo empio con una porta"* — the prefab hollow-cube house came out **not closed**: roof/edge cells placed before the walls existed failed `no-solid-face`, were recorded in `missing`, and skipped.
**What (both Minecraft adapter scope; structural only, no keyword logic):**
1. **Morning surface-exit reflex.** New priority-7 (lowest) threat in `MinecraftConnector._survival_threat`: at **day** with **no open sky above** (structural telemetry `is_day` + the bridge's new `hasOpenSkyAbove(maxUp)` → `sky_access`), and only when the `_surfaced_last_day` day-latch has not fired, returns `{"threat":"morning_exit","verb":"climb_staircase"}`. The bridge `climb_staircase` verb digs a **jumpable ascending staircase** (one block forward + one up per step, placing the tread) via direct dig/place, stopping early once `skyClear()` reports open sky. `_run_survival_guard` gates the actual climb on `_has_reachable_base(state)` (a registered base within `_base_retreat_radius` already has an exit → skip + set latch). Gated by `VESSEL_MORNING_EXIT_ENABLED` (default `True`).
2. **Fully-closed prefab house.** Two-part fix: (a) `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/base_spec.py::derive_base_layout` now emits the shell **bottom-up — Floor → Walls → Roof** (was Floor→Roof→Walls) so every roof cell anchors onto a finished wall-top; (b) the bridge `case 'build_base':` runs a bounded **seal pass** (`_MAX_SEAL_PASSES = 3` idempotent re-attempt rounds over cells that failed with `no-solid-face`, no-progress early-bail) so edge/corner/roof cells that were floating in air on the first pass get closed once the rest of the shell exists.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft_bridge.js` (`hasOpenSkyAbove`/`sky_access`, `case 'climb_staircase'`, `case 'build_base'` seal pass); `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`_survival_threat` branch #7, `_run_survival_guard` climb dispatch, `_has_reachable_base`, `climb_staircase` schema, `VESSEL_MORNING_EXIT_ENABLED` read); `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/base_spec.py` (Floor→Walls→Roof reorder); `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` (`VESSEL_MORNING_EXIT_ENABLED` registration); `AGENTS.md` §5c/§14. Tests: `tests/test_vessel_survival.py` (8 morning-exit tests), `tests/test_base_spec.py` (order-guard test).
**Status:** code + tests done. `uv run ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean; scoped `ty check` clean; `uv run pytest tests/test_vessel_survival.py tests/test_base_spec.py` → all pass (112 across the vessel sweep). In-world verification pending a `docker compose up -d --build synth` (needed for **both** the Python and the JS-bridge changes — the `synth` container COPYs the code at build time, not bind-mounted).
**Notes:** JS bridge is not covered by ruff/ty/pytest — it passed `node --check`. Never reintroduce `collectBlock.collect()` in the bridge (OOM). Keyword-free (numeric time + sky-access bool + canonical block ids only).

### fix(vessel): build_base attempted an impossible build (0/81) — layout now checks material *quantity*, not just presence  <!-- 2026-08-07 -->
**Symptom (LIVE, in-world):** Rekku's `build_base` reported `incomplete base build (0/81 blocks)` with every shell cell `missing acacia_planks`, even though `derive_base_layout` had returned `ok:True` (only `missing:[torch]`). The bot carried **10** acacia_planks but the shell needs **~81**.
**Root cause:** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/base_spec.py::derive_base_layout` was inventory-aware only for **material choice** (`_first_available`, count>0) — it never checked whether the carried *quantity* covered the ~81 shell cells. So it emitted 81 planks blocks and declared the plan buildable; the bridge then placed 0 and reported every cell missing.
**Fix (Minecraft-adapter scope; structural id/count only, no keyword logic):** after building the shell `blocks`, count required blocks per material and compare against the live `inventory_counts`. If any material is short, append `"<item>:need N (have M)"` to `missing` and set `ok:False` (the block list is still returned for diagnostics). This prevents a doomed 0/N dispatch and gives Synth a real *"gather N more"* cue via the connector's existing `missing` surfacing in `_act_build_base`.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/base_spec.py` (`derive_base_layout` quantity check); `tests/test_base_spec.py` (`test_insufficient_wall_quantity_is_not_ok`).
**Status:** code + tests done. `uv run ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean; scoped `ty check` clean; `uv run pytest tests/test_base_spec.py` → **13 passed**. In-world verification pending a `docker compose up -d --build` (the `synth` container COPYs the code at build time — it does **not** bind-mount `/app`, so the workspace edit is not live until a rebuild).
**Notes:** The `synth` container mounts only `logs`, `backups`, `/config`, `skins` — the Python code under `/app` is baked at build time. Editing workspace files (or `importlib.reload` in-container) does NOT change the running code; a rebuild is required to test in-world. This gap gates the "build a house" capability (`first_base` quest) until Synth gathers enough of one wall material (autonomy can now see the shortfall and gather instead of looping on an impossible build).

### fix(vessel): questline duplicated its 11 rows on every reconnect — added a UNIQUE identity index, a dedup migration, and an anti-race INSERT fallback  <!-- 2026-08-06 -->
**Symptom (LIVE, DB):** after two container rebuilds the `vessel_quests` table held **22 rows** for `scope=vessel/game=minecraft` instead of 11 — every `quest_id` appeared exactly twice (`first_base` as one `locked` + one `active`, all 10 others as two `locked`). Confirmed via psql: both duplicates shared identical `(scope, game, world, quest_id)`, and the table had only `vessel_quests_pkey` on `id` plus the NON-UNIQUE `idx_vessel_quests_scope`/`idx_vessel_quests_status` — **no** uniqueness on the identity tuple.
**Root cause:** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py::connect()` calls `quests.register_questline()` on **every** connect (initial connect + a boot reconnect, possibly concurrent). `register_quests` used a SELECT-then-INSERT upsert; with nothing enforcing uniqueness, each concurrent execution's SELECT found no existing row and inserted a full duplicate set.
**Fix (all fail-safe, full type hints, no bare `timestamp` columns):**
1. **Dedup migration** in `init_quest_table` (try/except, `log_debug` on failure): keeps the best row per identity (`done > active > locked`, then most-progressed, then lowest `id`) — Postgres `DELETE … USING ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY scope,game,world,quest_id …)`, MariaDB self-join `DELETE v … JOIN keep … AND v.id > keep.id`.
2. **`CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS uq_vessel_quests_identity ON vessel_quests (scope, game, world, quest_id)`** so repeated/concurrent registration can never duplicate again.
3. **Anti-race INSERT fallback** in `register_quests`: the INSERT is wrapped in try/except — on a UNIQUE violation it `rollback()`s then UPDATEs the existing row (title/description/order_index/objectives), preserving status+progress.
4. **`UNIQUE KEY uq_vessel_quests_identity (scope, game, world, quest_id)`** added to the `init-db.sql` DDL for fresh installs.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_quests.py` (`init_quest_table` dedup + UNIQUE index; `register_quests` INSERT try/except fallback); `init-db.sql` (`vessel_quests` UNIQUE KEY).
**Status:** fixed + verified live. `uv run ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean; scoped `ty check` clean; `uv run pytest tests/test_vessel_quests.py` → **42 passed**. Container rebuilt; on reconnect the dedup migration ran and the DB dropped from 22 → **11 rows** (`first_base` active, 10 locked), `uq_vessel_quests_identity` present. Advancement re-verified end-to-end (`has_base=True` → `first_base` done → `make_a_bed` promoted active), then real state restored (`first_base` active — Rekku has no base yet).
**Notes:** the dedup migration runs at the top of every `register_quests` (→ `init_quest_table`) call, so an already-polluted deployment self-heals on the next connect. Don't rely on the API :11435 LLM to trigger a vessel join (selenium BASE_CORTEX returns empty) — the vessel auto-reconnects ~55–80s after a rebuild.

### fix(vessel): break the Minecraft death loop — night shelter reflex + re-strategy on death  <!-- 2026-08-05 -->
**Why (user, IT):** *"3. non credo che piazzare una torcia sia sufficiente, il synth deve cercare un letto con tetto o crearsi uno shelter. Per il resto ok, proviamo"* — confirmed live: Rekku spawned at night, disarmed, health 20, in a hostile zone → surrounded → died; on respawn she resumed the **same goal + same strategy** and walked straight back into what killed her → infinite death loop. An earlier "place a torch" idea is insufficient — Synth must fully **enclose** (roofed bed or a self-built/dug refuge).
**What (two independent fixes):**
1. **Night shelter reflex (Minecraft adapter scope).** New priority-6 threat in `MinecraftConnector._survival_threat` (below drowning/burning/combat): when it is **not day** (`is_day` False, structural time telemetry) AND aggressive mobs are within `_sp_shelter_dist` (`VESSEL_SP_SHELTER_DIST`, default 16.0 — deliberately wider than the melee `_HOSTILE_NEAR_DIST`=8.0 via class const `_SHELTER_HOSTILE_DIST`=16.0, so the body walls in *before* a mob closes to melee), it returns `{"threat":"night_shelter","verb":"shelter",…}` and latches `_sheltered_last_day`. The bridge `shelter` verb (`minecraft_bridge.js`) tries, in order: (1) nearby **bed with a roof above it** → pathfind + `bot.sleep` (`method:"bed"`); (2) **seal** the ~10 open cells around the body with placed blocks (`method:"seal"`); (3) **dig-in** a 1×2 niche as last resort (`method:"dig_in"`); returns `{ok:enclosed, data:{method, sealed, dug_in, open_cells}}`. `_run_survival_guard` dispatches it via `await self.act("shelter", payload)` with a 2-tick cooldown. Gated by `VESSEL_SP_NIGHT_SHELTER` (default True).
2. **Re-strategy on death (bridge = Minecraft adapter scope; will cue = Rift Vessel core scope).** mindcraft parallel — save last death position + inject a "reconsider" message. The bridge `death` handler increments `deathCount` and records the **numeric** `lastDeath = {x, y, z, count, at}` (rounded coords, never expires until overwritten); `worldSnapshot` exposes it as `last_death`; `MinecraftConnector.get_world_state` copies it into `WorldState.extra["last_death"]` (null on an older bridge). `core/vessel_beat.py::build_will_prompt` (Rift Vessel **core**) then emits a strong, **purely structural** in-character cue — only when `last_death` is a dict with numeric `x`/`z` — telling Synth it *died at (x, y, z)* (with a `— this is death #N in this world` suffix when `count` is present) and pressing it to **reconsider**: pick a safer goal, move away first by setting `destination_x`/`destination_z` clear of the death spot, or make survival itself the goal. It never parses any text.
**Scope (AGENTS.md Scope rule):** the death telemetry + shelter verb are Minecraft adapter content; the will-prompt re-strategy cue is Rift Vessel core (world-agnostic — any world can populate `last_death`).
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft_bridge.js` (`lastDeath`/`deathCount`, `death` handler, `worldSnapshot.last_death`, `case 'shelter'`); `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`_survival_threat` night-shelter branch, `_run_survival_guard` shelter dispatch + cooldown, `_load_self_preservation_config` night-shelter keys, `get_world_state` `extra["last_death"]`, `_SHELTER_HOSTILE_DIST`); `core/vessel_beat.py::build_will_prompt` (`night_shelter` threat line + structural death cue); `AGENTS.md` §5c/§14. Tests: `tests/test_vessel_beat.py` (2 new death-cue tests), `tests/test_vessel_survival.py` (shelter reflex).
**Status:** code + tests done. Scoped `uv run pytest tests/test_vessel_beat.py` → **59 passed**; `uv run ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean. Container rebuilt (`docker compose up -d --build synth`) → clean startup (`get_recent_errors` → no matches). In-world verification pending (confirm the death loop is broken and shelter is built at night).
**Notes:** Keyword-free/structural (numeric coords + count only). JS bridge is not covered by ruff/ty/pytest — validation applies to the Python edits only. Never reintroduce `collectBlock.collect()` in the bridge (OOM). `_SHELTER_HOSTILE_DIST`=16.0 > `_HOSTILE_NEAR_DIST`=8.0 on purpose: shelter must trigger before melee range.

### feat(vessel): Minecraft virtual-quest tech-tree — a reference progression toward the Ender Dragon steers the first goal and the will beat  <!-- 2026-08-04 -->
**Why (user, IT):** *"se non ci sono goals deve valutare lo stato attuale e cercare su internet i prossimi goal considerando il suo stato attuale"* + *"siccome minecraft non ha vere e proprie quest potremmo creare noi delle quest virtuali dentro il nostro plugin minecraft… crea Un buon numero di quest che guidi il synth fino all'endgame (kill the ender dragon, passando dalle varie fasi: legno, pietra, ferro, diamante, netherite, crearsi tool e armature, letto, base operativa)."* — Minecraft has no in-game quest system, so the earlier starter-goal seeding (Phase C) was purely reactive (KB query from currently-held items) and gave Synth no *direction* toward the natural end-game.
**What:** a structural **tech-tree** describing the classic survival progression — `start → wood → stone → iron → diamond → nether → blaze_rods → ender_eyes → end → netherite` — surfaced to cognition purely as **reference context** through the existing `extra["knowledge"]` `{title,text}` channel (rendered by `core/vessel_beat.py::_fmt_knowledge` under the "reference, not a script" header). It is **content, not an engine**: it never auto-executes steps and never dictates the goal — Synth still authors freely and may skip/reorder/ignore the tree (spontaneity rule, AGENTS.md §5c). Stage detection is **structural and numeric only** — set membership over game item ids in the id→count inventory map + the current dimension id — never free-text/keyword parsing. At first login with no goal, the starter-goal KB seed is now extended with the *next milestone's* query tokens so the first authored goal points up the tech-tree instead of at random beach sand.
**Scope (AGENTS.md Scope rule):** the *mechanism* is a world-agnostic core hook `VesselConnectorBase.get_progression_stage() -> Dict[str, Any] | None` (default `None`); the *content* — the ordered tech-tree, the Minecraft item ids, the query seeds — lives entirely in the adapter (`plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/quests.py`).
**API (`quests.py`):** `detect_stage(inventory_counts, dimension) -> {stage_id, stage_title, next_id, next_hint, query, endgame}` (fail-safe → `start`); `stage_reference_facts(stage) -> list[{title,text}]` (3 reference entries: *Where you are* / *A typical next milestone (only if you want it)* / *The far horizon*); `progression_query_tokens(stage) -> list[str]` (next-milestone KB seed). All degrade to `[]`/`start` on falsy/bad input.
**Location (edited):** new `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/quests.py`; `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_base.py` (new `get_progression_stage` hook); `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`import quests`, `_last_dimension` cache, `get_world_state` prepends `stage_reference_facts` to `knowledge` + caches dimension, `get_progression_stage` override, `_resolve_knowledge` starter-goal seed extended with `progression_query_tokens`). Tests: new `tests/test_vessel_quests.py` (24 tests).
**Status:** code + tests done. Scoped `uv run pytest tests/test_vessel_quests.py tests/test_vessel_progression.py` → **37 passed**. `uv run ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean; scoped `ty check` on the vessel files → no errors (only benign `unused-type-ignore` warnings on the base-class test stub, matching the existing `test_vessel_progression.py` pattern). In-world verification pending (join via API :11435; confirm the will prompt now carries the "Where you are (progression)" / next-milestone reference block and the first goal is tech-tree-relevant).
**Notes:** Keyword-free/structural. Reuses the established `{title,text}` knowledge channel — no new prompt wiring needed (the will/action/reflection/appraisal prompts already render `extra["knowledge"]`). The automatic beat path stays cache-only; the live web/KB fetch after a goal exists is still handled by the existing goal-expander Drone. Removing `quests.py` degrades to the prior Phase-C reactive seeding — never breaks the snapshot.

### feat(vessel): starter-goal progression seeding — first login authors a progression-appropriate goal from live telemetry, not random  <!-- 2026-08-03 -->
**Why (user, IT):** *"se il client non ha quest mi creo degli obbiettivi consultando il web"* + *"gli obbiettivi cercati su internet sono attinenti alla mia progressione nel world"* — at first login (no client quests, no active goal) the will beat had no knowledge-base facts to orient the *first* goal, so Synth wandered to the beach gathering sand instead of making real progress.
**What:** when the automatic beat path (`get_world_state` → `_resolve_knowledge`) finds **no active goal** and nothing interactable nearby produced KB query tokens, the query now falls back to a **structural** seed built from Synth's real situation — the item ids she actually **holds** (inventory, ordered by quantity) plus the ids of **blocks around her**. The KB then surfaces facts about what she has/sees, and the will beat authors an informed first goal. This is a plain read of live bridge ids, **never** a scripted progression-stage catalogue (spontaneity rule) and never keyword/text matching.
**Scope (AGENTS.md Scope rule):** the *mechanism* is a new world-agnostic core hook `VesselConnectorBase.get_progression_context() -> list[str] | None` (default `None`); the Minecraft *content* is the connector's `_progression_query_tokens(inventory_counts, blocks)` static builder + `get_progression_context()` override reading the last-known telemetry cached in `get_world_state` (`_last_inventory_counts`, `_last_blocks`). Both fail-safe.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_base.py` (new `get_progression_context` hook), `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`_resolve_knowledge` starter-goal fallback, `_progression_query_tokens`, `get_progression_context` override, `_last_inventory_counts`/`_last_blocks` cache). Tests: new `tests/test_vessel_progression.py` (8 tests).
**Status:** code + tests done. Scoped `uv run pytest tests/test_vessel_progression.py` → **8 passed**; regression sweep `tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py tests/test_vessel_beat.py tests/test_vessel_knowledge.py` → **127 passed**. `uv run ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean; scoped `ty check` on the three vessel files → All checks passed. Minecraft `goals` table confirmed empty (nothing to clear). In-world verification pending (join via API :11435; confirm first-login goal is progression-relevant, not random sand).
**Notes:** Keyword-free/structural. Automatic beat path stays cache-only (never blocks on network/LLM); the live KB fetch still only happens on the explicit `lookup_knowledge` verb / goal-expander Drone. Removing the seed degrades to the prior empty-KB behaviour — never breaks the snapshot.

### fix(vessel): recap read path crashed with `'PostgresCompatCursor' object has no attribute 'description'` — column names now come from the explicit SELECT, not `cur.description`  <!-- 2026-07-29 -->
**Why (LIVE error):** `ERROR vessel_recap failed to read vessel_activity_log for 476bcdd20ac54040b1358355b5955a8f: 'PostgresCompatCursor' object has no attribute 'description'` — once the backlog fix let recaps actually run, every recap read returned zero rows and logged this error.
**Root cause (LIVE-PROVEN):** `load_activity_lines` (and, identically, the plugin's `_session_facts`) derived their column-name list via `columns = [c[0] for c in (cur.description or [])]`. The repo's active cursor `PostgresCompatCursor` does **not** implement the DB-API `.description` attribute, so the access raised `AttributeError`. The surrounding fail-safe `except → return []/"unknown"` swallowed it, so the recap silently read **nothing** instead of surfacing the error.
**Fix:** removed both `cur.description` accesses. Column names are now declared **statically**, matching the fixed order of each explicit `SELECT`: in `core/vessel_diary_compactor.py::load_activity_lines` → `columns = ["event_type", "summary", "metadata", "created_at"]`; in `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_compactor/vessel_compactor.py::_session_facts` → `columns = ["environment", "interface_path"]`. Dict rows are used directly; tuple rows are mapped positionally via `dict(zip(columns, row))`. Both functions stay fully fail-safe and type-hinted. A comment at each site warns not to rely on `cur.description`.
**Location (edited):** `core/vessel_diary_compactor.py` (`load_activity_lines`), `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_compactor/vessel_compactor.py` (`_session_facts`). Tests: `tests/test_vessel_diary_compactor.py`, `tests/test_vessel_compactor.py`.
**Status:** VERIFIED live in-container against the exact failing session `476bcdd20ac54040b1358355b5955a8f` — after `docker cp` + `docker restart synth`, `load_activity_lines` reads **4045** rows (was 0 + error) and a full `compact_activity_recap(...)` completes and writes a new `vessel_diary` row (id `3`) with **no** `failed to read` / `has no attribute` error. `uv run ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean; scoped `ty check` on both files → All checks passed; scoped pytest `tests/test_vessel_diary_compactor.py` → 10 passed, `tests/test_vessel_compactor.py` → 22 passed.
**Notes:** `PostgresCompatCursor` has **no `.description`** — never derive column names from it; declare them from the explicit `SELECT` column list, or read dict rows. A fail-safe `except → []/None` block masks real API/schema errors as "empty" results — when a query unexpectedly "returns nothing", exercise the raw read path directly first.

### fix(vessel): strategic in-world goals — lean vessel-turn action whitelist so the will/reflection prompt survives the char-budget clamp; global audio/tts action trim  <!-- 2026-07-29 -->
**Why (user, IT):** *"nah... ancora obiettivi di merda... Raccogliere blocchi di sabbia lungo la costa per iniziare a accumulare materiali da costruzione."* — the autonomous WILL beat + reflection pause kept authoring trivial material-gathering goals instead of strategic ones.
**Root cause (LIVE-PROVEN):** the full ~60-action global catalog folded into the vessel **system** prompt (`base_system≈9.9k` + `catalog_block≈17.6k`, `n_actions=60`, total ~28–30k) exceeded the 24000-char downstream clamp in `core/external_endpoints/bridges/cortex_bridge.py`. That clamp trims only the **user body**, never the system message — so the small will/reflection prompt (which lives in the body) was erased and the model authored goals blind.
**Fix — 3-tier vessel-turn action whitelist (structural, fnmatch, keyword-free):** applied *only on vessel turns* in `core/prompt_engine.py::build_json_prompt` via new `_derive_vessel_whitelist_action_types` (used only when the derived set is strictly smaller than the full catalog). Tier 1 (hardcoded, non-editable): `vessel_*`. Tier 2 (hardcoded, interchangeable per connected world): `*_<world>_*` (Minecraft → `*_minecraft_*`), derived structurally from `vessel_plugin._action_world()`. Tier 3 (editable): the new `VESSEL_ACTION_WHITELIST` config var (advanced; default `message_*, event, schedule_message, blocklist, spawn_drone`) — optional core-extra actions only. Final allowlist = `hardcoded_vessel_patterns(world)` ∪ `matches_whitelist(available, parse_patterns(VESSEL_ACTION_WHITELIST))`, minus `_SYSTEM_ONLY_ACTION_NAMES` / non-user-facing. Helper module `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_whitelist.py` (plugin-owned; if the plugin is absent the core falls back to its normal scope-based derive).
**Companion global audio/tts trim:** `tts_speak` (vox), `audio_telegram_bot` (dict + ValidationRule + `register_component_rules` list), and `audio_discord_bot` (dict + `get_action_types` list) removed from the exposed catalog globally (commented with `TODO(vessel-whitelist)` markers referencing AGENTS.md §5c). Audio still works via the `say audio` flag and interface-native paths. Also deleted the stale flat `interface/discord_interface.py` (dead code shadowed by the `interface/discord_interface/` package).
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_whitelist.py` (new), `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` (`VESSEL_ACTION_WHITELIST` var + `_action_world`), `core/prompt_engine.py` (`_derive_vessel_whitelist_action_types` + wiring), `core/external_endpoints/bridges/cortex_bridge.py` (removed diag logs; clamp logic untouched), `plugins/vox_plugin/vox_plugin.py` (`get_supported_actions() -> {}`), `interface/telegram_bot/telegram_bot.py`, `interface/discord_interface/discord_interface.py`, deleted `interface/discord_interface.py`, `AGENTS.md` §5c/§14. Tests: `tests/test_vessel_whitelist.py` (new, 24 tests).
**Status:** code + tests done. Scoped pytest `tests/test_vessel_whitelist.py tests/test_vessel_beat.py` → **81 passed**; discord/telegram suites green. `uv run ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean; scoped `ty check` on edited files → All checks passed. 3 `tests/test_vox_*::*default_is_kitten`/builtin failures are **pre-existing/unrelated** (assert `ACTIVE_VOX_ENGINE` default == `kitten`; fail with a DB name-resolution error in this DB-less workspace, independent of the `get_supported_actions()` edit). In-world verification pending (join Minecraft via API :11435; confirm `catalog_block` shrinks, `system_len < 24000`, will/reflection prompt survives, a new strategic goal row + `🎬 run_action ... set_goal`).
**Notes:** Keyword-free/structural (`fnmatch.fnmatchcase`). Do NOT change the cortex engine or the clamp logic. Hardcoding `vessel_*`/`*_<world>_*` prevents users from breaking embodiment. Package shadows a same-named flat module — the flat `interface/discord_interface.py` was unreachable dead code.

### feat(goals): extract a generic scope-aware goal store into a standalone `plugins/goals/` plugin (was Minecraft-only)  <!-- 2026-08-02 -->
**Why (user, IT):** *"I goal ora li utilizziamo per vessel ma sono scope goal plugin (spostiamo la logica in un nuovo plugin), perchè ora li usiamo per minecraft ma in futuro potremmo usarli per altri giochi o scopi, ad esempio pianificare qualcosa o per il synth se vuole conseguire qualche goal personale nella sua vita."* — the goal store lived inside the Minecraft adapter but is conceptually generic (any game, a planner, or Synth's own personal-life goals).
**What:** (a) **New standalone plugin** `plugins/goals/` (`goals.py` + `__init__.py` shim + `icon.svg` + `guide.md`), class `GoalsPlugin`, `PLUGIN_CLASS`, `register_plugin("goals")`, category "Various". A *scope-aware* store: every goal set is isolated by a three-part **scope tuple** (`scope`/`game`/`world`, default `SCOPE_NONE="none"`). Async API — `init_goal_table`, `get_active_goal`, `list_recent_goals`, `list_all_goals` (scope-agnostic), `delete_goal` (refuses the active goal), `clear_abandoned_goals`, `set_goal` (demotes the previous active goal in the same scope), `update_active_goal`. Generic actions `goal_set`/`goal_update`/`goal_list` (security `low`, no `external_effects`) for non-vessel/personal use. No catalogue, no templates, no inventory-count progression. (b) **DB** — legacy `minecraft_goals` renamed to `goals` and the `scope`/`game`/`world` columns added + backfilled (`vessel`/`minecraft`/`none`) by a startup migration `core/migrations.py::_migrate_goals_table`; DDL updated in `init-db.sql` with the new columns + indexes. (c) **Minecraft rewire** — `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/goals.py` is now a thin **delegating shim** to `plugins.goals.goals` with the Minecraft scope tuple pinned (`scope="vessel"`/`game="minecraft"`/`world="none"`); it re-exports `STATUS_*`/`TARGET_KIND_*` and the async wrappers so the historical `mc_goals.<fn>(...)` call surface (≈20 call sites in `minecraft.py`) is unchanged. Guarded import with a fail-safe "no goal" fallback if the generic plugin is absent. `depends_on: ["goals"]` on `vessel_plugin`. (d) **WebUI** — `/api/goals` and the History → Goals sub-tab now render goal groups per scope tuple (scope/game/world badge, per-group "clear abandoned" button); `_goals_store` resolves the generic plugin via `importlib.import_module`.
**Deviation from the plan (flagged):** the plan's literal "remove the Minecraft-namespaced LLM goal verbs" was **NOT** applied — `vessel_minecraft_set_goal`/`goals`/`update_goal` are KEPT on the connector because they carry Minecraft-specific travel-destination resolution (`_resolve_travel_destination`) and Goal-expander-Drone step-filling; they now delegate the *storage* to the generic plugin via the shim. The generic plugin ADDITIONALLY exposes `goal_set`/`goal_update`/`goal_list` for non-vessel goals.
**Location (edited):** new `plugins/goals/{goals.py,__init__.py,icon.svg,guide.md}`; `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/goals.py` (→ shim); `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` (`depends_on`); `core/migrations.py` (`_migrate_goals_table`); `init-db.sql`; `core/webui.py` (`_goals_store`); `res/synth_webui/js/history.js`; `core/webui_templates/sections/history.html`; `docs/rift_vessel.rst`; `AGENTS.md` §5c/§13. Tests: new `tests/test_goals_plugin.py` (17 tests); obsolete `tests/test_vessel_minecraft_goals.py` removed (it tested the retired Minecraft-owned implementation directly; superseded).
**Status:** code + tests done. Scoped `uv run pytest tests/test_goals_plugin.py` → **17 passed**. `uv run ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean; scoped `ty check` on the goals files → All checks passed. `import plugins.goals.goals` gotcha: the package `__init__.py` rebinds `sys.modules[__name__]` to the module, so `from plugins.goals import goals` / `import plugins.goals.goals` FAIL at runtime — use `importlib.import_module("plugins.goals.goals")` (paired with a `TYPE_CHECKING` import for the type-checker).
**Notes:** Keyword-free/structural. Fast Lane unaltered, no engine changed. Removing the Goals plugin degrades the Minecraft shim to a no-op "no goal" — never breaks the reactive Vessel.

### fix(goals): stepped goals now auto-complete when all steps are done  <!-- 2026-08-02 -->
**Why (user, IT):** *"Se un goal ha degli step e sono stati completati lo si considera completato. Tuttavia il sistema non sta funzionando bene."* — a goal with a step plan was never marked `done` once its last step was reached; it stayed `active` forever.
**Root cause:** the old `update_active_goal` advanced `current_step` but never transitioned `status` to `done` when the index passed the last step, so a fully-completed stepped goal remained active and kept being pursued.
**Fix (in the new generic store `plugins/goals/goals.py::update_active_goal`):** compute `auto_completed = total_steps > 0 and new_idx >= total_steps and explicit_status != STATUS_ABANDONED`; when true the goal's `status` is set to `done`. A **stepless** goal is unaffected (only completed explicitly), and an explicit `abandoned` always wins over auto-completion.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/goals/goals.py` (`update_active_goal`). Tests: `tests/test_goals_plugin.py` (`test_update_advances_and_auto_completes`, `test_update_stepless_no_autocomplete`, `test_abandon_wins_over_autocomplete`).
**Status:** VERIFIED via scoped pytest (`tests/test_goals_plugin.py` → 17 passed).
**Notes:** Shipped as part of the generic goals extraction above; the auto-complete logic lives in the generic store so it applies to every scope (vessel, planner, personal), not just Minecraft.

### Rift Vessel Compactor — "Run Now" says "nothing to do" while a real backlog exists: `_latest_ended_session` ordered by a non-existent `id` column, and manual trigger only compacted one session  <!-- 2026-08-01 -->
**Why (user, IT):** *"se lo lancio dice 'nothing to do' ma ci sono dei giorni da compattare in realtà"* — clicking "Run compaction" in the WebUI Plugins tab returned empty even though many ended vessel sessions were awaiting a `vessel_diary` recap.
**Root cause (LIVE-PROVEN via synth-db + in-container diagnostic):** TWO stacked causes. (1) **Non-existent column** — `VesselCompactorPlugin._latest_ended_session` ran `SELECT ... FROM vessel_sessions WHERE status='ended' ORDER BY ended_at DESC, id DESC`, but `vessel_sessions` has **no `id` column** (PK is `session_id`). asyncpg raised `UndefinedColumnError: column "id" does not exist`, the fail-safe `except` swallowed it → `None` → `run_action` reported `no_ended_session` = "nothing to do". Proven live: the ordered query crashed on `id`; `describe_table vessel_sessions` confirmed no `id`. (2) **Single-session-only trigger** — even once fixed, "Run Now" compacted only the single latest ended session, but the DB held 80 ended sessions with activity rows and only 1 `vessel_diary` entry — the user's "giorni da compattare" is a whole backlog.
**Fix (`plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_compactor/vessel_compactor.py`):** (a) `_latest_ended_session` now orders `ORDER BY ended_at DESC NULLS LAST, started_at DESC LIMIT 1` — no `id` reference. (b) New fail-safe helper `_pending_ended_sessions(limit=200) -> list[str]`: ended sessions that HAVE a `vessel_activity_log` row and do NOT yet have a `vessel_diary` row, oldest-first (`ORDER BY ended_at ASC NULLS LAST, started_at ASC`) so the backlog drains chronologically. (c) `run_action("compact_now")` with **no** `session_id` now iterates the whole backlog (each via `_compact_one`) and returns `{"status", "pending", "compacted", "results"}`; an explicit `session_id` keeps the targeted single-session path. `run_title` updated to "Compact all pending ended vessel sessions now".
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_compactor/vessel_compactor.py` (`_latest_ended_session`, new `_pending_ended_sessions`, `run_action`, metadata `run_title`). Tests: `tests/test_vessel_compactor.py` (renamed/added: `test_run_action_backlog_when_no_payload`, `test_run_action_backlog_counts_only_recapped`, adjusted `test_run_action_empty_when_no_ended_session`).
**Status:** VERIFIED live via in-container diagnostic — `_pending_ended_sessions` returns **79** sessions and `_latest_ended_session` returns a real id (`35e837ba…`) instead of `None`. `uv run ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean; `ty check` → All checks passed; scoped pytest `tests/test_vessel_compactor.py` → **22 passed**.
**Notes:** `vessel_sessions` PK is `session_id`, there is **NO `id` column** — never order/filter by `id` on that table. A fail-safe `except → None/[]` block silently masks schema errors as "empty" results; when a query "returns nothing" unexpectedly, run it raw against the live DB first.

### Rift Vessel — new **Rift Vessel Compactor** plugin; end-of-session diary is now a factual operational recap, not an autobiographical narrative (Decision A)  <!-- 2026-07-31 -->
**Why (user, IT):** *"a bassa priorità accodi un compact dell'ultima sessione del vessel quando il vessel viene disconnesso ... sia triggerabile manualmente come il grillo compactor ... non sia il grillo compactor: sono due scope diversi e devono essere due plugin diversi. Lo chiameremo Rift Vessel Compactor."* End-of-session compaction was an inline task inside `VesselSessionManager`; the user asked for a dedicated, separately-scoped, manually-runnable plugin (mirroring the Grillo Compactor's *shape* only).
**Decision A (locked):** the old first-person "lived experience" narrative is **removed** as the live product. The only compaction output going forward is a **factual, third-person operational recap** built from `vessel_activity_log` rows (`reason = "activity_recap"`) — concrete/resumable (coordinates, quantities, state), i.e. Synth's working memory of where it was and what it was mid-doing at the next login. The legacy autobiographical `compact_session` stays only as a fail-safe fallback and for tests.
**What:** (a) **New plugin** `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_compactor/` (`vessel_compactor.py` + `__init__.py` shim + `icon.svg` + `guide.md`), class `VesselCompactorPlugin`, `PLUGIN_CLASS`, category "Vessels", `get_supported_actions() -> {}`, runnable quartet (`run_action = "compact_now"`). At `start()` it registers `_on_session_ended` via the new `vessel_session_manager.set_compaction_handler` hook and starts an **internal, off-chain, low-priority asyncio worker queue** (NOT the message chain); at `stop()` it deregisters and cancels the worker. On end-of-session the manager calls the handler, which enqueues the session id and returns immediately (teardown never blocks). Manual "Run compaction" from the WebUI Plugins tab compacts the latest ended session (or a given `session_id`). (b) **New recap builder** `core/vessel_diary_compactor.py::compact_activity_recap` — reads `vessel_activity_log`, chunks by `VESSEL_DIARY_CHUNK_ITEMS`/`VESSEL_DIARY_CHUNK_CHARS`, summarises to factual third-person partials, folds (recursing when oversized) on the vessel-scope Cortex, stores one `vessel_diary` row with `reason = "activity_recap"`; fully fail-safe (LLM error → deterministic plain-text join; empty log → no entry; never raises). (c) **Reroute** `core/vessel_session_manager.py::end_session` — handler-preferred (recap plugin) with legacy inline `_launch_compaction`/`_compact_and_store` (autobiographical, gated by `VESSEL_DIARY_COMPACTION_ENABLED`) as fail-safe fallback when no handler is registered. `diary_entry_id` stays `NULL`. (d) **New config** `VESSEL_COMPACTOR_ENABLED` (component `vessel_compactor`, default True); `VESSEL_DIARY_COMPACTION_ENABLED` now gates only the legacy fallback path.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_compactor/` (new: `vessel_compactor.py`, `__init__.py`, `icon.svg`, `guide.md`), `core/vessel_diary_compactor.py` (`compact_activity_recap`, `load_activity_lines`, `_activity_row_to_line`, `_stringify_metadata`, `_build_recap_chunk_prompt`, `_build_recap_fold_prompt`, `_fold_recap_partials`, `_recap_fallback`, `ACTIVITY_RECAP_REASON`), `core/vessel_session_manager.py` (`set_compaction_handler`, `end_session` reroute), `docs/rift_vessel.rst`, `AGENTS.md` §5c/§13/§14. Tests: `tests/test_vessel_compactor.py` (new, 21 tests).
**Status:** code + tests done. Scoped pytest `tests/test_vessel_compactor.py` → **21 passed**; regression `tests/test_vessel_diary_compactor.py tests/test_vessel_session_manager.py` → 13 passed. `uv run ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean. In-world verification pending.
**Notes:** Keyword-free/structural. Fast Lane unaltered, no engine changed. Core never imports the plugin (registration-hook pattern, mirrors `set_liveness_probe`). Package shim gotcha: import the class directly (`from plugins.rift_vessel.vessel_compactor.vessel_compactor import VesselCompactorPlugin`) — the shim rebinds `sys.modules[package]` so `from pkg import vessel_compactor as mod` fails.

### Rift Vessel — SyntH goes inactive/T-pose after long play: vessel lived-experience flooded the shared `ai_diary`; route it to a dedicated `vessel_diary` and compact in chunks (background)  <!-- 2026-07-30 -->
**Why (user, IT):** *"vedo che il diary è flooddato dall entry di minecraft: non è che vengono incluse nel prompt fast lane?"* — after a long Minecraft session SyntH stopped responding on every interface (avatar T-pose), and the `ai_diary` daily row was full of in-world telemetry.
**Root cause:** the old `VesselSessionManager._flush_to_diary` wrote the end-of-session "lived experience" into the **shared** `ai_diary` with `interface="vessel"`. `ai_diary` keeps **one daily row across all interfaces** (the upsert concatenates, no interface filter), and `get_static_injection` injects that whole row into **every** non-vessel Fast-Lane prompt. So each play session grew the shared row until the ordinary chat prompt overran the model's limit and the LLM turn failed → SyntH inert. A secondary failure: a long session's monolithic flush would itself overrun the summarization LLM and fail.
**Fix:** (a) **Stop writing `ai_diary` at vessel session end** — `_flush_to_diary` removed; `end_session` now sets `diary_entry_id = NULL` always and schedules a background compaction via `_launch_compaction`/`_compact_and_store` (strong ref in `self._compaction_tasks` so it isn't GC'd; never raises). (b) **New dedicated `vessel_diary` table** (`core/db.py` `_VESSEL_DIARY_DDL` + Postgres index tuple, `init-db.sql`, `init_vessel_tables()`) — completely separate from `ai_diary`, so in-world telemetry never reaches a Fast-Lane prompt. (c) **Chunked compaction** (`core/vessel_diary_compactor.py`): the experience buffer is split by `VESSEL_DIARY_CHUNK_ITEMS`/`VESSEL_DIARY_CHUNK_CHARS` (clamped), each chunk summarised into a first-person partial, partials folded (recursively if still oversized) into one entry, on the vessel-scope Cortex. Fully fail-safe: any LLM error / no engine → deterministic plain-text join; empty buffer → no entry. Gated by `VESSEL_DIARY_COMPACTION_ENABLED`. (d) **Deferred (explicit `.. todo::` in `compact_session`):** whether/how to import a well-formed `vessel_diary` entry into the real `ai_diary` — decided only after measuring typical entry size.
**Location (edited):** `core/vessel_diary_compactor.py` (new), `core/vessel_session_manager.py` (`end_session`, `_launch_compaction`, `_compact_and_store`; `_flush_to_diary` deleted), `core/db.py` (`_VESSEL_DIARY_DDL`, `_VESSEL_PG_INDEX_DDL`, `init_vessel_tables`), `init-db.sql` (`vessel_diary`), `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` (3 config keys), `AGENTS.md` §5c/§13/§14. Tests: `tests/test_vessel_diary_compactor.py` (new), `tests/test_vessel_session_manager.py` (updated).
**Status:** code + tests done. `uv run ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean; `ty check` → All checks passed; scoped pytest `tests/test_vessel_session_manager.py tests/test_vessel_diary_compactor.py` → 13 passed. In-world verification pending (join Minecraft via API :11435, short session, `vessel_disconnect` → confirm NO new `ai_diary` `interface='vessel'` row, a coherent `vessel_diary` row, and a later non-vessel Fast-Lane prompt free of vessel text).
**Notes:** Keyword-free/structural. Fast Lane unaltered, no engine changed. `diary_entry_id` is now always `NULL`. Config component `vessel_plugin`.

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — `/vessel join minecraft` fails with generic "Server disconnected" AND crash-loops the Node bridge: version mismatch + unguarded synchronous protocol throw  <!-- 2026-07-28 -->
**Why (user, IT):** *"❌ Could not enter `minecraft`: ... could not enter the Minecraft server ...:25565: Server disconnected"* — `/vessel join minecraft` never spawned Rekku; the Python side only ever saw a vague `bridge failed to connect: Server disconnected`.
**Root cause (LIVE-PROVEN via in-container `bridge.log` + stack trace):** TWO stacked causes. (1) **Version mismatch** — config `MINECRAFT_SERVER_VERSION` was pinned to `1.21.11` in the `config` table while the real server runs `1.20.2`; Mineflayer connected with the pinned version and the server replied `multiplayer.disconnect.incompatible` (`with: ["1.20.2"]`), surfacing to Python as the opaque "Server disconnected". (2) **Unguarded synchronous throw** — the version-check error is thrown *synchronously* from deep inside `minecraft-protocol`'s packet transform stream (`transforms/framing.js:67 } else { throw e }` → `client/versionChecking.js:27`), escaping the bot's own `error` emitter. With NO process-level `uncaughtException` handler this crashed the entire Node bridge (`Node.js v22.23.1` exit), defeating the pre-spawn retry logic → crash-restart loop, so the connector never got a real error message.
**Fix (2 parts):** (a) **Config** — set `MINECRAFT_SERVER_VERSION` = `1.20.2` (the version plumbing already forwards it into the `/connect` POST body via `minecraft.py::_resolve_server_target`; SyntH side was correct). (b) **Bridge crash guard** — in `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft_bridge.js`, added a `handleFatalAsync(kind, err)` helper wired to `process.on('uncaughtException')` and `process.on('unhandledRejection')`: it records `lastError`, settles any in-flight `/connect` as a clean `{ ok:false, detail }` (so Python gets a REAL message, not "Server disconnected"), tears the bot down (`removeAllListeners`/`quit`), and — critically — KEEPS the HTTP bridge alive instead of exiting. Mirrors the existing `server.on('error')` EADDRINUSE clean-handling pattern.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft_bridge.js` (`handleFatalAsync` + the two `process.on` handlers, inserted before the `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM` shutdown hooks); DB config `MINECRAFT_SERVER_VERSION`.
**Status:** VERIFIED live. `plugins/` is BAKED into the image (NOT bind-mounted — grep for the new symbol returned 0 in the running container until rebuild), so deployed via `docker compose up -d --build`; post-rebuild the container grep shows 3 `handleFatalAsync` refs and DB `value = 1.20.2`. Live re-test (`bridge.log`, new PID): every connect now uses `version 1.20.2`, ZERO new `Node.js v22` crash stack traces, `GET /health` returns `{"ok":true,...}` (bridge process stays alive). **Residual (server-side, NOT a code bug):** the target server `192.168.1.42:25565` is in **online-mode**, so it now kicks the offline-mode bot with `multiplayer.disconnect.unverified_username` (`bridge.js` connects `auth: MC_AUTH || 'offline'`, and per AGENTS.md the Vessel is offline-auth by design). To let Rekku join, set `online-mode=false` in the server's `server.properties` and restart it — no SyntH code change can make an offline bot pass an online-mode server's Mojang auth check.
**Notes:** Live truth is `docker logs synth` + in-container `docker exec synth cat plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/mineflayer/bridge.log`; the `logs/` folder files are STALE after restarts. The `config` table PK column is `config_key` (NOT `key`).

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — Rekku repeats the same self-monologue instead of replying: autonomous `say` poisoned the conversational deque (route it to the perception ring)  <!-- 2026-07-29 -->
**Why (user, IT):** *"il synth sta continuando a ripetere la stessa frase in chat"* — Rekku kept emitting self-similar night monologues addressed to the ABSENT trainer ("Jay") instead of answering the PRESENT in-world player.
**Root cause (LIVE-PROVEN via DB `chat_history_cache`):** `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py::_persist_self_speech` persisted EVERY autonomous `say` of Rekku into the `vessel/minecraft` **conversational deque** as `sender_name="self"` with `metadata=NULL`. The bounded deque (`maxlen≈10`) saturated with solitary Jay-monologues, so the weak `VESSEL_CORTEX` model (`qwen-35-9b`) — which parrots the dominant framing/last context lines — reproduced that pattern on reactive player turns → yet another Jay-monologue instead of a reply. Structural history poisoning.
**Fix (6 edits, all in `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py`, structural/keyword-free):** propagate the turn context down the chain `execute_action → handle_custom_action → act → _persist_self_speech` via a boolean `is_reactive_player_chat`, derived from the existing structural flag `context.get("vessel_player_chat")` (already propagated interface → `core/message_queue.py` → context). In `_persist_self_speech(..., *, is_reactive_player_chat=False)`: when NOT a reactive player chat (i.e. an autonomous beat) tag the `self` line with `metadata={"vessel_perception": True, "vessel_event_type": "self_monologue"}` → `add_message_to_context` routes it to the **separate perception ring buffer** (`get_or_create_perception_context`) instead of the conversational deque; reactive replies stay `metadata=None` → conversational deque. `_is_repeat_of_last_self_say` now reads BOTH buffers for the exact-match anti-echo guard. The will beat still sees monologues via the vessel-focus prompt merge (conversation + last N perceptions), so no grounding is lost.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` (`_persist_self_speech`, `act`, `handle_custom_action`, `execute_action`, `_is_repeat_of_last_self_say`).
**Status:** VERIFIED live e2e. `uv run ruff format` / `ruff check --fix` clean; `ty check` → All checks passed; scoped pytest `tests/test_vessel_self_echo_guard.py test_vessel_perception_context.py test_vessel_beat.py test_vessel_realtime.py` → 85 passed. Deployed via `docker cp plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py synth:/app/... && docker restart synth` (bridge reattached as "Rekku"). **Runtime confirmation (Postgres `chat_history_cache`, POST-deploy):** every autonomous `self` row now carries `{"vessel_perception":true,"vessel_event_type":"self_monologue"}` → routed to the ring; the conversational deque (seen in the debrief context log) contains only `[player chat, self reply]` at `maxlen≈10`. The old untagged "Ciao, Jay…" rows are all PRE-deploy (the fix does not retroactively tag them). Repetition bug resolved.
**Notes:** Keyword-free (structural flag, never message text). Respects §5c (Fast Lane, no Agent Lane/Drones, single end-of-session diary). **Separate residual (NOT this bug, NOT a regression):** on one reactive turn the model produced `😵`, which is the `core/message_chain.py` fallback (`Sending fallback message: '😵'`) — the reactive LLM turn FAILED (timeout/error of the slow harmonyai/Selenium model). Distinct pre-existing LLM-failure symptom; do NOT touch cortex/Selenium (project constraint). `vessel_activity_log` / `chat_history_cache` in Postgres are the source of truth — `synth.log` rotates every 2000 lines (~10 s in DEBUG) and `cortex_api.log` does not capture vessel/Selenium turns.

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — SyntH "spento" #2: player chat starved by the motor-tick sighting stream on the slow single-consumer loop (defer en-route sightings during the player-quiet window)  <!-- 2026-07-28 -->
**Why (user, IT):** *"il SyntH in minecraft sembra 'spento': non risponde in chat"* — even after the health-streak fix, Rekku still failed to reply to player chat while pursuing autonomy. After the fast-lane revert (fast lane capped at 20000 chars in develop) the symptom re-surfaced.
**Root cause (LIVE-PROVEN via two contrasting in-world CoachAgent tests):** the single-threaded `core/message_queue.py::_consumer_loop` awaits each LLM turn synchronously, and the deployed vessel cortex (`VESSEL_CORTEX` = harmonyai/qwen-35-9b via Selenium) is slow + non-cancellable (~tens of seconds/turn). The motor tick fires every `VESSEL_MOTOR_INTERVAL_SEC` (3 s) and `_maybe_run_motor_tick` calls `_collect_en_route_sightings`, which enqueues an AMBIENT `sighting` perception whenever a NEW element appears — a near-continuous stream on a moving bot. So although a `PRIORITY_HIGH` player chat outranks `PRIORITY_AMBIENT` in the heap, it waits behind an in-flight autonomous turn and the constant fresh perceptions keep the consumer perpetually busy → no reply for minutes (not even the `😵` fallback). **TEST A** (normal config): chat → no `say` for 120 s. **TEST B** (motor off + will/action intervals 3600 s): same chat → `say(text=Fragole)` in ~37 s and CoachAgent saw `<Rekku> Fragole` in-world → reactive path works only when the consumer is free.
**Fix:** In `interface/vessel_interface.py::_maybe_run_motor_tick`, after `connector.motor_step(goal)` (which is **LLM-free**, so the body keeps moving) and BEFORE `_collect_en_route_sightings`, defer sighting collection while a player is active: `quiet_sec = vessel_beat.resolve_will_quiet_sec(_cfg)` (default 60, `0` disables); if `quiet_sec > 0 and now - self._last_player_activity_at < quiet_sec` → log + `return`. Mirrors the existing will/action-beat player-quiet deferral. Only NEW AMBIENT sightings are suppressed during the quiet window, keeping the single consumer free to pick up the HIGH player chat; the motor still moves and mines.
**Location (edited):** `interface/vessel_interface.py` (`_maybe_run_motor_tick` — sighting deferral block).
**Status:** VERIFIED live e2e under NORMAL load (motor 3 s, will beat 45 s, action 20 s). Deployed via `docker cp interface/vessel_interface.py synth:/app/... && docker restart synth` (`/app` is NOT bind-mounted); bridge reconnected as "Rekku", session `2a13ae7b...` active. Chat `"...parola Mirtillo"` at 03:18:34 → `vessel_activity_log` `action_say | say(text=Mirtillo)` at 03:19:10 (~36 s), CoachAgent observed `[coach][chat] Rekku: Mirtillo` in-world. The `vessel_activity_log` shows ZERO `sighting` events between the chat and the reply (deferred) while `gather` (motor) continued — confirming the body kept moving. `uv run ruff format` / `ruff check --fix` / `ty check interface/vessel_interface.py` → all clean.
**Notes:** Keyword-free (structural, actor-based via `_last_player_activity_at`, set in `on_world_event` only when `event_type=="chat" and entity`). Engine untouched (`VESSEL_CORTEX` stays harmonyai/qwen-35-9b) and Fast Lane unaltered per §5c. `vessel_activity_log` (Postgres) is the source of truth — `synth.log` rotates every 2000 lines (~15 s in DEBUG) and `cortex_api.log` does NOT even capture vessel/Selenium turns. **Residual:** a beat that STARTED before the player spoke is still in-flight (uncancellable), so the first reply can lag by ~one slow turn; acceptable for now (do NOT touch engine/fast lane to tighten it). **Corrected prior claim:** an active goal DOES exist ("Camminare verso ovest-nordovest…"), so the aimless-mining symptom is downstream of the starvation, not a missing goal.

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — SyntH "spento" (inert) fixed: health-false streak raised 3→60 to stop false disconnects freezing all beats  <!-- 2026-07-28 -->
**Why (user, IT):** *"il SyntH in minecraft sembra 'spento': non risponde in chat e rimane fermo sul posto"* — the bot was in-world but inert: no movement, no chat replies, goals not pursued/updated.
**Root cause:** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py::_poll_loop` flipped `self._connected = False` after only `_MAX_HEALTH_FALSE_STREAK = 3` consecutive `connected:false` polls. At `_POLL_INTERVAL_SEC = 1.0` that is ~3 s — well within normal transient blips (chunk load / server lag). A false disconnect made `has_active_session()` read False (RECONNECTING state), which **freezes all three autonomy beats** (will/action/motor), so SyntH looked "switched off" even though the Mineflayer bridge was healthy.
**Fix:** Raised `_MAX_HEALTH_FALSE_STREAK` from 3 → **60** (≈60 s of continuous failure before declaring the connector dead) and added `probe_external_liveness` (default False on `vessel_base`, real impl on the Minecraft connector) so the session manager's liveness probe reflects the connector's true state. Transient blips no longer trip a false disconnect; a genuine drop still ends the session within `VESSEL_DISCONNECT_GRACE_SEC`.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`_MAX_HEALTH_FALSE_STREAK`, `probe_external_liveness`, `_poll_loop`), `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_base.py` (`probe_external_liveness` default), `interface/vessel_interface.py` (`_connector_live`, `_reattach_active_sessions`), `AGENTS.md` §5 (stale clamp doc 27000 → 24000, matching the real `_DEFAULT_DOWNSTREAM_CHAR_BUDGET`).
**Status:** VERIFIED live e2e (session `2a13ae7b...`, bot "Rekku"). Evidence via the rotation-proof `vessel_activity_log`: AC5 movement/actions — action_goto=8, action_move=4, gather=440 (actively mining oak_log/oak_leaves/fern/dirt/vine/red_mushroom), craft=4, build=1, arrival=999; AC3 goals — action_set_goal=20 (active goal id 20 "trovare la prima foresta", directly served by the recent wood-mining stream); AC4 goal updates — action_update_goal=7; in-world chat — action_say=124, chat=115. `synth.log` also showed `EXECUTING ACTIONS: count=2` → `Delegating action to VesselPlugin: type=vessel_minecraft_observe/status` + `motor tick: goto directional_march`.
**Notes:** Keyword-free (streak/liveness are structural, never message text). Respects §5c (Fast Lane only, no Agent Lane/Drones, single end-of-session diary). Debug technique that beats fast log rotation: query `vessel_activity_log` in Postgres (`docker exec synth-db psql -U synth -d synth …`) — it survives the 2000-line `synth.log` rotation. `"no drop collected"` on gather is expected creative-mode vanilla behaviour, not a bug. `[web_search] Synthesis produced N chars for intent="[Embodiment...]"` on a vessel turn is benign secondary intent-classification logging — the enqueued item's `allowed_action_types` still carries the full `vessel_minecraft_*` catalog and the real actions execute. AC2 (will-context self-consciousness) DEFERRED per user.

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — knowledge base rebuilt on the live minecraft.wiki (incremental disk cache + one-time LLM summary), replacing the curated `wiki/knowledge.json`  <!-- 2026-07-29 -->
**Why (user, IT):** *"o la si fa bene o non la si fa, queste sono solo nozioni random non complete, e io credo che la wiki minecraft sia viabile dai bot. Se non lo fosse possiamo clonarla in locale piuttosto che avere dei frammenti di cose."* The old KB was 8 static/curated fragment files (`crafting_table.txt`, `diamonds.txt`, `food.txt`, `knowledge.json`, `mining_tiers.txt`, `night_mobs.txt`, `smelting.txt`, `wood_bootstrap.txt`) — incomplete, hand-maintained, and effectively a mini-catalogue.
**Fix:** New `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/wiki_client.py` consults the **live** [minecraft.wiki](https://minecraft.wiki) MediaWiki API (open to bots, no auth). `wiki_client.lookup(query, limit, *, cache_only=False)` searches for matching pages and, per page, serves a **one-time LLM summary** (a short EN factual *how the game works* note) cached incrementally on disk as `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/wiki/cache/<slug>.json` (`{title, url, raw_extract, summary, fetched_at}`). Repeated lookups of the same page hit the cache — no re-fetch, no re-summarise. Matching is keyword-free/structural (query = whitespace-joined game tokens matched against page-title slugs). **Beat vs verb split:** the automatic will/motor-beat path (`MinecraftConnector._resolve_knowledge`) calls with **`cache_only=True`** so a `WorldState` build never blocks on the network/LLM (serves only cached pages); the explicit `lookup_knowledge` verb + goal-expansion Drone use the live path (`cache_only=False`). Fully fail-safe: offline / any error → returns whatever is cached (possibly empty), never raises, so a Fast-Lane beat can't break.
