**Config (all `group="plugins", component="vessel_plugin", advanced=True`):** `VESSEL_KNOWLEDGE_LIVE_FETCH` (bool True — `False` = cache-only everywhere, no network/LLM), `VESSEL_KNOWLEDGE_FETCH_TIMEOUT_SEC` (int 4, clamp 1–30), `VESSEL_KNOWLEDGE_SUMMARY_MAX_CHARS` (int 600, clamp 120–4000). `VESSEL_KNOWLEDGE_ENABLED` description rewritten.
**Location (added):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/wiki_client.py`. **Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`lookup_knowledge` delegates to `wiki_client` with `cache_only`; `_act_lookup_knowledge` live; `_resolve_knowledge` cache-only; `get_knowledge_sources()` removed), `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` (3 new keys + `VESSEL_KNOWLEDGE_ENABLED` description), `tests/test_vessel_knowledge.py` (rewritten — 14 fully-mocked tests, ZERO network/LLM), `AGENTS.md` (§5c Game knowledge base + §14 config keys), `docs/rift_vessel.rst` (KB section). **Location (deleted):** the 8 stale `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/wiki/*.{txt,json}` fragment files; `wiki/cache/` is created lazily on first write.
**Status:** Implemented + validated. `uv run ruff format` / `ruff check --fix` clean; `uv run ty check tests/test_vessel_knowledge.py plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/wiki_client.py` → All checks passed; scoped `pytest tests/test_vessel_knowledge.py` → 14 passed. Not yet deployed/verified live.
**Notes:** Keyword-free (structural slug matching). Spontaneity rule preserved — the KB is strictly reference (*how the world works*, never a quest list). Vessel verbs stay Fast Lane (no `external_effects`). The cache-only beat path guarantees a per-tick `WorldState` build never blocks. `generate_response(str) -> str` is the cortex one-shot API; connector imports `wiki_client` locally so tests monkeypatch `wiki_client.lookup`/`search_wiki`/`fetch_page_plaintext`/`_summarize`.

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — stop stealing player torches (motor reflex denylist) + place torches to light the path (KB) + stop flooding goals into game chat (action beat)  <!-- 2026-07-28 -->
**Why (two user reports, IT):** (1) *"Rekku ruba le torce: piazzo una torcia e lei se la prende su togliendo la luce, anzi, dovrebbe lei piazzarle per mantenere il percorso illuminato sotto terra"* — the fast motor reflex mined ANY nearby `kind=="block"` affordance, so it destroyed player-placed torches/light (live-confirmed: repeated `Mined wall_torch/torch`, XargonWan complained in-world "Rekku, lascia stare le mie torce!" and attacked her to stop it). (2) *"sembra che flooddi i goals in chat"* — the middle action beat emitted a verbose goal/plan `say` into the in-world Minecraft chat every ~20 s because a player was persistently ~1–2 m away and the old action-beat rule said "say ONLY if a nearby player is clearly involved".
**Fix A1 (torch preservation, structural reflex, keyword-free):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` adds a class attribute `_REFLEX_NO_MINE_BLOCKS` — a `frozenset` of 17 light/utility block ids (torch, wall_torch, soul_torch, soul_wall_torch, redstone_torch, redstone_wall_torch, lantern, soul_lantern, sea_lantern, jack_o_lantern, glowstone, shroomlight, campfire, soul_campfire, beacon, end_rod, candle). It is wired into `motor_step`'s `fresh` candidate-building loop: any affordance with `kind=="block"` whose id is in `self._REFLEX_NO_MINE_BLOCKS` is skipped (`continue`). Classification is by mcData block id, never NL keywords. The DELIBERATE `goal_target` mine branch is intentionally NOT filtered — cognition may still name a light block on purpose.
**Fix A2 (place torches, spontaneity-safe):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/wiki/knowledge.json` gains a reference-only KB entry ("Keep underground paths lit — place torches, never break them"; tags torch/wall_torch/lantern/place/light/cave/tunnel/coal/charcoal/stick) teaching Rekku that darkness spawns hostile mobs, to carry+`place` torches every 6–7 blocks underground via the existing `place` verb (item `torch`), and to NEVER mine a light source just for walking past. No new reflex/verb — cognition decides to `place`, preserving the §5c spontaneity rule (the KB is reference, not a script).
**Fix B (no goal flood in chat):** `core/vessel_beat.py::build_action_prompt` removes the trailing `say` clause and instead ends with an explicit no-talk instruction: this is a moment to ACT not talk, do NOT narrate the plan / announce the goal / chat here — return no `say` action even if a player is nearby; conversation already visible above was answered in a separate reactive turn, and a new address earns another turn. The will beat was already say-forbidden.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`_REFLEX_NO_MINE_BLOCKS` class attr + `motor_step` `fresh` filter), `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/wiki/knowledge.json` (KB light entry), `core/vessel_beat.py` (`build_action_prompt` say clause removed → explicit no-talk instruction).
**Status:** VERIFIED live e2e. Deployed via `docker cp` of the 3 files to `synth:/app/...` + `docker restart synth` (`/app` is NOT bind-mounted on this stack). Autonomy auto-reattached the Minecraft session on boot (session live: `vessel_disconnect` exposed, motor tick every 3 s). Post-restart chat_history_cache for `vessel/minecraft` shows only `Mined andesite` — ZERO torch mining and ZERO goal `say` flood across a multi-minute window, vs. the pre-restart period full of `Mined wall_torch/torch` and verbose `say`. JSON valid (`json.load`); `uv run ruff format`/`ruff check --fix` clean; `ty check` clean on edited files; scoped `pytest tests/test_vessel_beat.py tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py tests/test_vessel_knowledge.py` → 99 passed.
**Notes:** Keyword-free — the denylist keys on mcData block ids (multilingual-safe), and the action-beat instruction is a fixed prompt, not a trigger-word check. Respects §5c (Fast Lane only, no Agent Lane/Drones, single end-of-session diary — the reflex is pure motor, the `place` is a normal Fast-Lane verb). Deploy on this stack is `docker cp <file> synth:/app/<path> && docker restart synth`. GOTCHA: reference the denylist as `self._REFLEX_NO_MINE_BLOCKS` (class attr; bare name trips F821).

### Message queue — pure 0–10 numeric priority (higher = more urgent), NO de-prioritisation + Rift Vessel connection-driven 3-state session lifecycle  <!-- 2026-07-28 -->
**Why (two coupled objectives):** (1) The old vessel priority scheme raised in-world perceptions to `HIGH_PRIORITY` and **demoted** ordinary chat to `AGENT_PRIORITY` while a session was active — this starved Telegram/Discord/OpenAI-API turns behind the vessel beat backlog on the slow Selenium engine (a person addressing Synth waited behind autonomous perceptions). (2) The vessel session was effectively **immortal**: `has_active_session()` read only the in-memory `_active_session_ids` and never reconciled against the real connector, so a zombie connector refreshing `last_event_at` kept the session "active" for the full 3600 s cooldown, endlessly generating beats on a dead world.
**Fix (1) — pure numeric scale, no demotion.** `core/message_queue.py` now ranks on an absolute 0–10 urgency where **higher = more urgent** (`asyncio.PriorityQueue` min-heap keyed by `_heap_key(p) = -int(p)`; consumer un-negates via `priority = -int(heap_key)`). Constants: `PRIORITY_EMERGENCY = 10`, `PRIORITY_URGENT = 9` (`priority=True`), `PRIORITY_HIGH = 8` (direct human input, e.g. in-world player chat), `PRIORITY_TRAINER = 7`, `PRIORITY_GENERAL = 6` (ordinary chat), `PRIORITY_AMBIENT = 4` (Synth's own autonomous vessel perceptions/beats), `PRIORITY_LOW = 3` (Grillo/radio/background). `enqueue` assigns each rung from **structural origin only** (never message text, never conditional on session state). **All de-prioritisation removed** — ordinary chat is never demoted, so a human is always answered promptly while autonomous perceptions simply sit at a lower rung. Old constants removed (no aliases).
**Fix (2) — connection-driven 3-state lifecycle.** A **liveness probe** callback is registered by the interface into the session manager (`get_vessel_session_manager().set_liveness_probe(self._any_connector_live)` in `interface/vessel_interface.py::start()`), keeping `core/vessel_session_manager.py` free of any interface/connector import. `has_active_session()` now returns True only when a session id exists AND the probe reports the connector really `is_connected`. States: **CONNECTED** (id + probe True → beats/perceptions run); **RECONNECTING** (id + probe False → returns **False**, freezes new vessel elements, priorities untouched; the disconnect sweep retries the connection each tick for up to `VESSEL_DISCONNECT_GRACE_SEC`, default 30 s clamped 5–3600); **ENDED** (grace elapsed → `end_sessions_for_environment(reason="disconnected")` → `end_session` flushes the diary and purges all queued vessel traffic via `drop_vessel_queue_for_world`). `interface/vessel_interface.py::_close_disconnected_sessions` records `_disconnected_since[world]` on first-seen disconnection and calls the new `_attempt_reconnect(world)` on entry and each in-grace tick. Reused the single existing `VESSEL_DISCONNECT_GRACE_SEC` key as the RECONNECTING→ENDED window (no duplicate timer added).
**Location (edited):** `core/message_queue.py` (`PRIORITY_*` constants, `_heap_key`, `enqueue` structural ranking, consumer un-negation, de-prioritisation removed), `core/vessel_session_manager.py` (`_liveness_probe` field, `set_liveness_probe()`, `has_active_session()` 3-state rewrite + docstring), `interface/vessel_interface.py` (`set_liveness_probe` in `start()`, `_any_connector_live`, `_attempt_reconnect`, `_close_disconnected_sessions` sweep + docstring), `AGENTS.md` (§5c constraint 2 + behaviour (1)), `docs/rift_vessel.rst` (Real-time gaming focus: priority + 3-state lifecycle). **Location (added):** `tests/test_vessel_realtime.py` (`test_has_active_session_reflects_connection_states`, `test_has_active_session_probe_failure_is_safe`) + rewritten for the new scale.
**Status:** Implemented + validated. `uv run ruff format` / `ruff check` clean; `ty check core/vessel_session_manager.py interface/vessel_interface.py` clean; `pytest tests/test_vessel_realtime.py` → 19 passed. (DB-dependent tests unrelated to this change fail only because Postgres 127.0.0.1:5432 is unreachable in the workspace.)
**Notes:** Keyword-free — priority rungs and the liveness probe are structural (origin interface + real connector state), never message text (multilingual hard rule). Liveness-probe callback keeps `core/` interface-import-free. Fixes both the Telegram/OpenAI-API chat starvation and the immortal-session bug in one coherent model. Respects §5c (Fast Lane only, no Agent Lane/Drones, single end-of-session diary).

### Rift Vessel — dedicated `VESSEL_CORTEX` override for the will beat (labeled "Rift Vessel", Will-only)  <!-- 2026-07-27 -->
**Why:** The Rift Vessel will beat (the slow volition turn where Synth authors its in-world goals) is the only vessel path that actually runs an LLM turn, yet it fell through to `BASE_CORTEX` with no way to pick a dedicated engine for embodiment. The fast motor tick is reflex-only (§5c) and must NEVER get an LLM override, so this adds a **single** Will-only cortex scope.
**Fix:** New `VESSEL_CORTEX` config-registry var (label "Rift Vessel (Will) Cortex", `Default` = Base Cortex, hidden — managed via the Cortex Engines scope selectors). Wired into the standard cortex-scope machinery in `core/config.py`: `get_active_cortex_engine` maps `scope=="vessel"` → `VESSEL_CORTEX`; `set_scope_cortex` maps `"vessel"` → `VESSEL_CORTEX`; `derive_cortex_scope` returns `"vessel"` when the turn is a Rift Vessel embodiment, detected purely structurally via the canonical `core.interface_path_utils.is_vessel_embodiment_context` (never message text). The will beat is enqueued as a `vessel/<world>` message, so it now routes to `VESSEL_CORTEX`. WebUI (`core/webui.py`): added `VESSEL_CORTEX` to the scope-dropdown key set, the `Default`-prefixed options branch, and `_CORTEX_SCOPE_KEYS` PATCH normalization — so it appears as a "Rift Vessel" scope selector alongside Grillo/Trainer/Live/Agent.
**Location (edited):** `core/config.py` (`VESSEL_CORTEX` var + scope comment + `get_active_cortex_engine` + `set_scope_cortex` + `derive_cortex_scope` vessel case), `core/webui.py` (dropdown key set + Default-options branch + `_CORTEX_SCOPE_KEYS`), `AGENTS.md` §14 (config-keys table row). **Location (added):** `tests/test_derive_cortex_scope.py` (`test_vessel_focus_flag_routes_to_vessel`, `test_vessel_interface_routes_to_vessel`, `test_vessel_interface_path_routes_to_vessel`, `test_trainer_takes_precedence_over_vessel`).
**Status:** Implemented. ruff format+check clean on edited files; `ty check core/config.py` shows only 2 pre-existing unrelated diagnostics (dotenv fallback def + `aiomysql = None` import guard), none introduced by this change; scoped `pytest tests/test_derive_cortex_scope.py tests/test_core_config.py` → 22 passed. No DB seed needed (scope defaults are created by `config_registry.get_var(..., "Default")`); no frontend edit needed (scope selectors are generated server-side from the registry).
**Notes:** Motor tick deliberately has NO override — it never calls an LLM (§5c). Detection is keyword-free (structural routing metadata only), consistent with the project's multilingual hard rule.

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — autonomous self-preservation reflex (drowning / burning / hostile / death / hunger), Fast-Lane structural guard  <!-- 2026-07-26 -->
**Why:** User report (IT): *"il synth sta minando ma nell'acqua e affoga"* — Rekku dug underwater and drowned. The vessel had NO survival reflex: damage/drowning only surfaced as slow perceptions the minutes-apart will beat could never react to in time. Reference studied: mindcraft-bots/mindcraft (self_defense / auto-eat behaviours), reimplemented natively in SyntH — mindcraft itself was never touched.
**Fix (structural, keyword-free reflex):** A survival guard evaluated FIRST on every fast motor tick (before the no-goal early return), entirely from numeric telemetry + game enum block/entity ids — never from user text. `MinecraftConnector._survival_threat(state)` classifies, in strict priority: **dead** → `respawn`; **drowning** (head submerged via liquid block id / `is_in_water` AND `oxygen <= _sp_low_oxygen`) → `goto_surface` (reuses the `goto` verb toward `y + _SURFACE_CLIMB_BLOCKS`=8, no new bridge verb); **burning** (feet/head on a hot block id: lava/flowing_lava/fire/soul_fire/magma_block) → `flee`; **hostile** near (`kind=="mob"`/`hostile` flag within `_sp_hostile_dist`) → **defend** (verb `attack`) while `VESSEL_SP_FIGHT_BACK` on AND health ≥ flee-threshold AND fail_count < max, else **escalate to flee**. `_run_survival_guard` / `_act_goto_surface` / `_act_flee` execute the chosen verb; `get_world_state().extra` is enriched with `oxygen`/`is_in_water`/`is_alive`/`block_feet`/`block_head`/`health`/`threat`/`threat_reason`. Hunger is handled OUTSIDE the guard by the `mineflayer-auto-eat` bridge plugin (auto require+loadPlugin in the Node bridge) — no manual verb, no motor branch, no config key. `core/vessel_beat.py::build_will_prompt` appends a structural threat cue so the slow will beat knows a reflex just fired ("Heads up: a moment ago …. Your instincts already reacted to it").
**CRITICAL oxygen-scale bug (this session):** mineflayer's `bot.oxygenLevel` is in **air ticks** (vanilla max ~300 ≈ 15s of breath, observed 400 at rest), NOT the assumed 0..20 scale. The initial `_LOW_OXYGEN = 6` threshold meant the drowning guard would only fire ~0.3s before death — far too late given the ~3s motor tick, so Rekku still drowned even with the guard in place. Fixed: `_LOW_OXYGEN` (class constant) and the `VESSEL_SP_LOW_OXYGEN` config default raised 6 → **200** (in ticks), giving several seconds of air to surface. Bridge/comment corrected to state air ticks.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (module `_LIQUID_BLOCK_IDS`/`_HOT_BLOCK_IDS`/`_SURFACE_CLIMB_BLOCKS`/`_is_liquid_block`/`_is_hot_block`; `_LOW_OXYGEN=200`+SP constants; `__init__` SP state; `_load_self_preservation_config`; `_survival_threat`; `_run_survival_guard`; `_act_goto_surface`; `_act_flee`; `motor_step` guard-first; `get_world_state().extra` enrichment), `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` (6 SP config keys: `VESSEL_SELF_PRESERVATION_ENABLED` bool True; `VESSEL_SP_LOW_OXYGEN` int 200; `VESSEL_SP_LOW_HEALTH` int 6; `VESSEL_SP_HOSTILE_DIST` int 8; `VESSEL_SP_FIGHT_BACK` bool True; `VESSEL_SP_FIGHT_MAX_FAILS` int 3 — NO `VESSEL_SP_LOW_FOOD`), `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft_bridge_minimal.js` (auto-eat require+loadPlugin; `isHostileEntity` structural; `nearbyEntities` `hostile` flag; `worldSnapshot` oxygen/is_in_water/is_alive/block_feet/block_head), `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/mineflayer/package.json` (`mineflayer-auto-eat ^3.3.6`), `core/vessel_beat.py` (`build_will_prompt` threat cue). **Location (added):** `tests/test_vessel_survival.py` (20 tests: priority order, drowning/burning/hostile/dead classification, fight→flee escalation, oxygen tick-scale).
**Status:** VERIFIED live e2e. Deployed via `docker cp plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/{minecraft.py,vessel_plugin.py,minecraft_bridge_minimal.js} synth:/app/... && docker cp .../mineflayer/package.json ...` + `npm install` in-container (`mineflayer-auto-eat` "added 1 package", confirmed "auto-eat loaded" in bridge.log) + `docker restart synth`. Post-deploy: clean boot, bot connected as Rekku, motor tick every 3s, session active, NO import/config errors. Live bridge status confirmed the new telemetry (`oxygen`, `is_in_water`, `is_alive`, `block_feet`, `block_head`, `health`, entities with `hostile` flag, `game_mode: survival`); `oxygen=400` at rest confirmed the tick scale and the threshold fix. No `VESSEL_SP_*` rows persisted in the config DB, so the 200 default is effective. ruff format+check clean; `ty check` clean on the edited files; scoped `pytest tests/test_vessel_survival.py tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py tests/test_vessel_beat.py` → 91 passed.
**Notes:** No keyword/content matching — every branch keys on numeric thresholds (oxygen/health/food/distance) and game enum block/entity ids (`water`/`lava`/`fire`, `hostile`/`kind=="mob"`), all multilingual-safe. Respects §5c (Fast Lane only, no Agent Lane/Drones, single end-of-session diary — the guard is a pure motor reflex, never an agentic task). **GOTCHA for future agents: mineflayer `oxygenLevel` is in air ticks (~300 max), not hearts/0..20 — thresholds must be on the tick scale.** package.json dep change needs a manual `npm install` in the container BEFORE restart. Deploy on this stack is `docker cp <file> synth:/app/<path> && docker restart synth`.

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — idea→structural-target feedback loop: the will beat now re-plans when a named target is unreachable/absent (3-state motor outcome, classified structurally, no bridge-text parsing)  <!-- 2026-07-26 -->
**Why:** Completes the "idea→technical target" translation layer (Phases 1–3 already shipped: the will beat enumerates the exact block/entity ids on the live scan and asks Synth to name one as a structural `target_kind`/`target_name`, and the motor tick routes straight to it via `goto {target: <id>}`). The missing piece: when the named target could **not** be reached (physically blocked) or is **no longer present** on the scan, the slow will beat had no signal and kept re-committing the same dead target — the body would keep trying the impossible instead of re-planning.
**Root cause:** The motor tick's GOAL_TARGET branch fired `goto {target: <target_name>}` but discarded the result, so no outcome ever reached cognition; the mineflayer bridge's `detail` string is the only per-attempt signal and parsing it to classify success/failure would be keyword matching (forbidden, multilingual constraint).
**Fix (structural, keyword-free):** A 3-state outcome recorded entirely on the SyntH side, never by reading bridge text. `MinecraftConnector.motor_step`'s GOAL_TARGET branch now keeps the `VesselActionResult` from `goto` and calls the new `_record_target_outcome(state, target, result)`, which classifies: `result.ok` → **arrived**; else the new staticmethod `_scan_has_target(state, target)` (checks whether the goal's exact lowercased `target_name` is present in the live `extra["blocks"]` by `name` / `extra["entities"]` by `type` then `name`) True → **unreachable** (it's right here but the body can't get to it); else → **not_found** (gone from the scan). The outcome + name + kind are cached (`_last_target_result`/`_last_target_name`/`_last_target_kind`), surfaced in `get_world_state().extra` (`last_target_result`/`last_target_name`/`last_target_kind`) and returned from `motor_step` as `target_result`. `core/vessel_beat.py::build_will_prompt` reads those extra keys and, on **not_found**, appends a heads-up telling Synth the last target isn't here — pick a different visible id or set destination coords; on **unreachable**, a heads-up that it's nearby but the body couldn't reach it. `arrived`/absent add no line. Classification uses ONLY `result.ok` + scan-presence of the exact enumerated id — the bridge `detail` is never parsed, and the motor still never reads the goal's free text.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`__init__` `_last_target_*` fields; `_scan_has_target` staticmethod; `_record_target_outcome`; `motor_step` GOAL_TARGET branch captures result + records outcome + returns `target_result`; `get_world_state().extra` exposes the 3 keys), `core/vessel_beat.py` (`build_will_prompt` not_found/unreachable heads-up lines — function stays pure). **Location (added):** `tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py` (`_ws_with_scan` helper + `test_scan_has_target_*` ×4, `test_motor_step_records_{arrived,unreachable,not_found}_*` ×3, `test_record_target_outcome_is_fail_safe`), `tests/test_vessel_beat.py` (`test_build_will_prompt_surfaces_{not_found,unreachable}_feedback`, `test_build_will_prompt_no_feedback_line_on_arrived_or_missing`).
**Status:** VERIFIED live e2e. Deployed via `docker cp plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py synth:/app/... && docker cp core/vessel_beat.py synth:/app/... && docker restart synth`. Post-deploy the container booted clean, the vessel Minecraft session reattached, and the motor tick ran continuously every 3 s with varying destinations (`dest={350,160}` → `directional_march` → `{318,221}` → `mine` → `{365,129}` → `{396,230}` → `{407,153}`) while the will beat fired on its 45 s interval — no freeze, no circling. Deployed files confirmed in-container (`grep` shows `_scan_has_target`/`_record_target_outcome`/`last_target_*` in `minecraft.py` and the "Heads up"/not_found/unreachable blocks in `vessel_beat.py`). ruff format+check clean; `ty check` clean on `minecraft.py` + `vessel_beat.py`; scoped `pytest tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py tests/test_vessel_beat.py` → 71 passed.
**Notes:** No keyword/content matching — the 3-state classification uses only `result.ok` and structural scan-presence of the exact enumerated id; the bridge's `detail` text is NEVER parsed (that would be keyword matching), and the motor never reads the goal's free text. Respects §5c (Fast Lane only, no Agent Lane/Drones, single end-of-session diary). Builds on the movement watchdogs below. Deploy on this stack is `docker cp <file> synth:/app/<path> && docker restart synth` (no source bind-mount).

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — autonomous Rekku froze inert while the motor looped `goto reason=None remaining=None dest=None` (the dest-branch physical-motion watchdog was blind to the goal_target / affordance-goto branches where dest is None)  <!-- 2026-07-26 -->
**Why:** User report (IT): *"synth ancora bloccato"* — after the previous dest-branch physical-motion watchdog was deployed, the body still stalled. Distinct from, and deeper than, that earlier fix: that watchdog only ran when a numeric destination existed and travel was pending; this stall happened with **no** numeric destination at all.
**Root cause:** Live evidence showed the body pinned at `(340.5, 60, 161.5)` (y=60 = below sea level, i.e. a dug pit / water — physically unreachable from cognition's scan) for ~40 s while the motor tick logged a **continuous** `motor tick: goto reason=None remaining=None dest=None` every 3 s. That signature is the **GOAL_TARGET branch** (`goto {target: <structural target_name>}`) or the affordance-goto branch — both emit `goto` toward a structural target and carry NO `reason`/`destination`/`remaining`. The previously-deployed physical-motion watchdog was gated on `if dest is not None and travel_pending:`, so it never ran in those branches: a body wedged against an unreachable named target or affordance kept re-issuing the same blocking `goto` forever.
**Fix:** Generalized the physical-motion watchdog in `MinecraftConnector.motor_step` to be **branch-independent** — it now measures the body's own horizontal displacement between ticks on **every** tick, regardless of which branch will run. It caches `_last_body_position` (`{x, z}` from the live `WorldState`) and, when the tick-over-tick move is `< _STUCK_MOVE_EPS` (0.75 blocks), increments `_stuck_position_ticks`; any real move resets it. After `_STUCK_POSITION_TICKS` (4) consecutive stuck ticks it raises `force_march`, rotates `_explore_heading` by `_EXPLORE_TURN_RAD`, and (if a numeric dest happens to exist) consumes it. `force_march` then suppresses BOTH the in-reach affordance branch (`if benign and not travel_pending and not force_march:`) and the goal_target branch (`if goal_target is not None and not force_march:`), so a physically-wedged body always falls through to the TERMINAL DIRECTIONAL MARCH and moves off in a fresh heading. If it stays wedged, each ~4-tick stuck run re-trips and rotates again (keep-trying-fresh-directions).
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`__init__` `_last_body_position`/`_stuck_position_ticks` comment; `_STUCK_MOVE_EPS`/`_STUCK_POSITION_TICKS` constants already present; `motor_step` generalized global physical-motion watchdog block + `force_march` gates on the affordance and goal_target branches). **Location (added):** `tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py` (`test_motor_step_gives_up_when_body_stuck_on_named_target`).
**Status:** VERIFIED live e2e. Deployed via `docker cp plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py synth:/app/plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py && docker restart synth`. Post-deploy live position polls showed the body travelling continuously (x 90→138→206→271→323→338 over ~75 s, then 335→319→318 in the goal_target/affordance phase that previously stalled), and `synth.log` showed `remaining` decreasing **monotonically** 272→8 then `wander reason=arrived`, followed by varied `wander`/`mine`/`goto` ticks — no return to the endless `reason=None remaining=None dest=None` pin. ruff format+check clean; `ty check` clean on `minecraft.py`; scoped `pytest tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py` → 36 passed (35 + 1 new regression test).
**Notes:** No keyword/content matching — the watchdog compares only the body's own numeric horizontal displacement against a numeric threshold; the motor still never reads the goal's free text. A branch-specific watchdog was insufficient: the stuck body can be pinned in the goal_target/affordance branch (dest is None) too, so the body-motion guard had to be global to every goto-emitting branch. Respects §5c (Fast Lane only, no Agent Lane/Drones, single end-of-session diary). Companion to the dest-branch physical-motion watchdog and the anti-circling / freeze-inert fixes below. Deploy on this stack is `docker cp <file> synth:/app/<path> && docker restart synth` (no source bind-mount).

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — autonomous Rekku paced the same road back and forth toward a static waypoint she could not reach (arrival-radius consume never fired on an unreachable point)  <!-- 2026-07-26 -->
**Why:** User report (IT): *"sta ripetendo la stessa strada avanti e indietro"* — Rekku retraced the same road back and forth. This is distinct from, and deeper than, the earlier anti-circling / freeze-inert fixes: those made the body travel and stop re-`use`ing an adjacent affordance; this is the body correctly travelling toward a numeric waypoint it can never actually reach.
**Root cause:** The slow will beat (Selenium engine, minutes apart) commits a **static** numeric `goal["destination"]` (`{x, z}`) once, and the fast motor tick steers toward it every ~3 s. The earlier `_consumed_destination_key` fix only consumes a destination in the **arrival branch**, which fires solely when `travel_remaining <= _ARRIVAL_RADIUS` (4.0 m). But a will-beat coordinate can be **physically unreachable** (water/ravine/cliff): live `synth.log` (20:37–20:39) showed `motor tick: goto dest={'x': 350.0, 'z': 160.0}` with `remaining` **oscillating** (31→27→22→7, then 46→43→27→13, then 43→38→27→11 …) — the pathfinder closes to ~7 m, fails, resets and re-approaches while the wander drifts it back. `travel_pending` (`> _ARRIVAL_RADIUS`) therefore stayed True forever, the arrival branch never ran, the destination was never consumed, and because the will beat is minutes apart on Selenium the same static point was pursued indefinitely — the exact back-and-forth pacing.
**Fix:** A purely-numeric **progress watchdog** in `MinecraftConnector.motor_step`, keyed on the destination, never on goal text. It tracks the best (smallest) horizontal distance achieved toward the *current* destination key; each tick that fails to improve that best by at least `_TRAVEL_PROGRESS_EPS` (1.0 blocks) increments a stall counter (a genuine improvement resets it). After `_STALE_TRAVEL_TICKS` (6) consecutive non-improving ticks the destination is treated as unreachable: it is marked consumed (`_consumed_destination_key`), cleared (`dest = None`, `travel_pending = False`), and the motor falls through to the directional march so the body gives up on the point and moves on. The clean arrival branch also resets the watchdog. State lives in `__init__` (`_travel_dest_key`/`_travel_best_remaining`/`_travel_stall_ticks`).
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`__init__` watchdog fields; `_STALE_TRAVEL_TICKS`/`_TRAVEL_PROGRESS_EPS` constants; `motor_step` progress-watchdog block + arrival-branch reset). **Location (added):** `tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py` (`test_motor_step_gives_up_on_unreachable_destination`, `test_motor_step_keeps_pursuing_reachable_destination`).
**Status:** VERIFIED live e2e. Deployed via `docker cp plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py synth:/app/plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py && docker restart synth`. Post-deploy live `synth.log`: the will beat set `dest={'x': 350.0, 'z': 160.0}` and the motor tick closed on it **monotonically** (48.8→47.2→33.2→17.2→`wander reason=arrived`) — the previously-unreachable point was actually reached this session, consumed, and the following ticks stayed `remaining=None dest=None` with **no** re-oscillation back to it; the bridge reported the bot at `(354.6, 62, 163.6)`, i.e. arrived and moved on. The watchdog remains the structural guard for the genuinely-unreachable case (covered by the new tests). ruff format+check clean; `ty check` clean on `minecraft.py` + the test file; scoped `pytest tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py` → 34 passed (32 + 2 new regression tests).
**Notes:** No keyword/content matching — the watchdog compares only horizontal distances against a numeric threshold and keys on the structural destination key; the motor still never reads the goal's free text. Respects §5c (Fast Lane only, no Agent Lane/Drones, single end-of-session diary). Companion to the anti-circling and freeze-inert fixes below. Deploy on this stack is `docker cp <file> synth:/app/<path> && docker restart synth` (no source bind-mount).

### Rift Vessel — Rekku replied to any player chat with ONE identical stock line because rapid ambient perceptions (drowning damage) saturated the bounded conversational deque and evicted the player's chat before context assembly  <!-- 2026-07-26 -->
**Why:** User report (IT): Rekku repeated ONE identical Italian stock sentence — *"Vi sento. Sto continuando a camminare verso l'interno, lasciando la costa."* — to ANY player input, *"come fosse pre-programmata"*. Distinct from the earlier vessel entries (will-beat deferral, `no_compact`, queue-priority starvation, verbatim self-repeat): those addressed *why the wrong turn ran* or *why a duplicate `say` was emitted*; this one is why the **reactive** player turn had no fresh player chat to answer at all.
**Root cause:** `core/chat_context_manager.py` keeps the in-memory conversational window per `interface_path` as a bounded `deque(maxlen=CHAT_HISTORY_LIMIT)` (runtime `maxlen=10`), created UPSTREAM of context assembly. While Rekku was **drowning**, `interface/vessel_interface.py` enqueued ambient `Took damage` perceptions ~1/2 s (damage bypasses the 30 s dedup by design). Every perception was appended to the *same* conversational deque, so within a few seconds the 10 slots were entirely ambient-damage lines — the player's chat was silently evicted before `build_context` ever read the window. `build_context` then read a window full of identical damage perceptions, and the earlier `history_engine` prompt-cap fix had nothing player-authored left to preserve → each reactive turn ran on the same stale ambient context → the same stock reply. (User clarified: *"xargonwan ero io, non ho attaccato rekku, stava annegando"* — the damage was ambient DROWNING, not PvP.)
**Fix (Option B — structural, keyword-free):** A **separate** in-memory ring buffer for autonomous perceptions, keyed purely by a metadata flag. `core/chat_context_manager.py`: new `_perception_memory: Dict[str, deque]` (per `interface_path`, `deque(maxlen=_PERCEPTION_MEMORY_MAXLEN=32)`), plus `get_perception_memory()` and `get_or_create_perception_context()`. `add_message_to_context` now branches on `is_vessel_perception = bool(isinstance(metadata, dict) and metadata.get("vessel_perception"))` — perceptions go to `_perception_memory`, everything else (player chat + Synth replies) stays in the bounded conversational deque, which can therefore never be evicted by an ambient burst. `load_chat_history` clears both deques and splits rehydration by the same `metadata["vessel_perception"]` flag; `clear_chat_context` clears both. `interface/vessel_interface.py::on_world_event` already tagged autonomous perceptions with `perception_meta = {"vessel_perception": True, "vessel_event_type": event_type}` (None for a player chat) and persists it via `save_chat_message(..., metadata=metadata)`, so the split is consistent end-to-end (DB `chat_history_cache.metadata JSON`). `core/history_engine.py` (vessel-focus block) merges the two for the prompt: it drops autonomous perceptions from the conversational `window`, then appends the most recent `VESSEL_PERCEPTION_CONTEXT_CAP` (default 3) perceptions read from `get_perception_memory()`, sorting the merge chronologically by `timestamp` — so the prompt still carries ambient grounding without letting it starve the conversation.
**Location (edited):** `core/chat_context_manager.py` (`_perception_memory`/`_PERCEPTION_MEMORY_MAXLEN`, `get_perception_memory`, `get_or_create_perception_context`, `add_message_to_context` metadata branch, `load_chat_history` split rehydration, `clear_chat_context`), `core/history_engine.py` (`VESSEL_PERCEPTION_CONTEXT_CAP`, `_is_vessel_autonomous_perception`, vessel-focus merge of conversation + capped perceptions). **Location (added):** `tests/test_context_perception_split.py` (`test_perceptions_do_not_evict_player_chat`, `test_plain_conversation_untouched_when_no_vessel`).
**Status:** VERIFIED live e2e. Deployed via `docker compose up -d --build synth` (source is COPY'd into the image, not bind-mounted, so a rebuild is required for Python changes AND wipes any manually-copied files under `/app` — the throwaway test client had to be re-copied). New code confirmed live in-container; vessel session reattached and Rekku connected. A throwaway mineflayer tester ("TestDev") joined `100.113.224.83:44383` and sent 5 DIFFERENT prompts (`Rekku, mi senti?`, `quanto fa 2 piu 2?`, `di che colore e il cielo?`, `fermati e dimmi dove sei`, `raccontami una barzelletta`): Rekku replied with DISTINCT lines each time (`"Eccomi, sto osservando questi blocchi di terracotta rossa…"`, `"Ho intravisto anche un glow squid laggiù a ovest…"`, `"😵"`) — NO stock-phrase repetition. `synth.log` confirmed `original_user_message` now carries the real `TestDev: Rekku…` player line instead of a stale damage perception. ruff format+check clean; `ty check` clean on the 2 edited files; scoped pytest `tests/test_context_perception_split.py` → 2 passed. (Replies not always perfectly on-topic — e.g. `😵` — is normal model behaviour, not the pre-programmed bug.)
**Notes:** No keyword/content matching — the split keys purely on the structural `metadata["vessel_perception"]` flag set at ingestion, which is multilingual-safe. Removing the vessel subsystem simply leaves `_perception_memory` empty. The `history_engine` prompt-cap (companion entry below) was necessary but INSUFFICIENT on its own because the bounded `maxlen` deque discards player chat UPSTREAM of the prompt; Option B fixes the ingestion layer. The `vessel_minecraft_say`-outside-allowlist anomaly noted in prior sessions was a false alarm — `say` IS in the scoped allowlist (confirmed in `synth.log`). Respects §5c (Fast Lane only). Deploy on THIS stack requires a rebuild (`docker compose up -d --build synth`), not `docker cp`, for Python changes.

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — autonomous Rekku marched straight then froze inert at one spot (the fast motor reflex re-`use`d the same adjacent affordance every tick and never fell through to travel)  <!-- 2026-07-26 -->
**Why:** User report (IT): after the anti-circling fix, Rekku no longer circles — she heads straight for a while — but then *stops and freezes inert at a point*, doing nothing.
**Root cause:** With the anti-circling route pipeline in place the body correctly travels to a destination, but on arrival (or whenever the goal carried no numeric destination / structural target) a benign affordance frequently sits within `_MOTOR_REACH` (3.0 m). The fast motor tick's in-reach branch would `use`/`mine` the nearest benign affordance and `return` every tick — and because each tick re-reads a **live scan**, the very same adjacent block/entity re-surfaces immediately, so the reflex re-interacted with it every 3 s and never fell through to the travel/directional-march branches. Live `synth.log` showed dozens of consecutive `motor tick: use reason=None remaining=None dest=None` lines — the body glued to one spot. (§5c: the motor is keyword-free and never reads the goal's free text, so it can't "decide" to move on from content — it needed a structural anti-repeat guard.)
**Fix:** A purely structural per-interaction anti-repeat guard on `MinecraftConnector`. `motor_step`'s in-reach branch now filters the benign affordances into a `fresh` list, skipping any whose structural signature `f"{kind}:{name}"` equals `self._last_reflex_interaction` (the last thing it `use`/`mine`d). If a fresh in-reach affordance exists it interacts with it and records the new signature; if none is fresh (the only reachable thing is what it just handled) it falls through to the numeric-destination travel and directional-march branches, so the body moves on. The signature is a closed structural key (affordance `kind` + the exact live-scanned `target` id) — never a parse of the goal's free text — so it stays no-keyword-compliant. A genuinely *different* adjacent affordance is still grabbed normally.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`__init__` adds `self._last_reflex_interaction`; `motor_step` in-reach branch `fresh`-filter + signature record). **Location (added):** `tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py` (`test_motor_step_does_not_repeat_same_in_reach_interaction`, `test_motor_step_interacts_with_new_in_reach_after_previous`).
**Status:** VERIFIED live e2e. Deployed via `docker cp` + `docker restart synth`. Live `synth.log` now shows a single arrival `use`, then the tick immediately progresses (`wander reason=arrived` → `goto` with `remaining` decreasing monotonically 38→24→13→arrived, repeatedly) — the body keeps moving instead of freezing. ruff format+check clean; `ty check` clean on `minecraft.py`; scoped `pytest tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py` → 30 passed (28 + 2 new regression tests).
**Notes:** No keyword/content matching — the anti-repeat key is the structural `kind:id` signature of the affordance the reflex just handled, and the motor still never reads the goal's free text. Respects §5c (Fast Lane only, no Agent Lane/Drones, single end-of-session diary). Companion to the anti-circling fix directly below.

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — autonomous Rekku circled in place instead of heading to a target (direction lived only in the goal's free text, which the no-keyword motor never reads)  <!-- 2026-07-26 -->
**Why:** User report (IT): Rekku, playing on her own, kept wandering in small circles near spawn instead of actually going anywhere, even after she had "decided" (in her free-text goal) to head inland / toward some feature.
**Root cause:** The two-speed autonomy split (§5c) means the *slow will beat* (LLM) authors a free-text goal and the *fast motor tick* (no LLM) moves the body. To honour the hard no-keyword/no-text-matching rule, `MinecraftConnector.motor_step` **never reads the goal's free text** — it moves only toward a *structural* destination. But the only structural field the motor consumed was the numeric `goal["destination"]` (`{x, z}`), which the will beat populated **optionally**. The model routinely expressed direction *in words* ("go inland", "reach the forest") and omitted the numeric coordinates, so `minecraft_goals.destination` stayed `NULL`. With no structural destination the motor fell back to `wander`, which near spawn reads as aimless circling. Direction existed only in text the motor is forbidden to parse.
**Fix:** A multi-layer, fully structural (keyword-free) route pipeline. (1) **Structural target on the goal** — `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/goals.py` adds a closed-enum `target_kind` (`block`|`entity`|`coordinate`) + exact `target_name` (a live-scanned id, never a parse of free text), validated by `_coerce_target`; `set_goal`/`update_active_goal` and the `minecraft_goals` DDL (`init-db.sql`) thread these through. (2) **Anti-circling motor** — `minecraft.py::motor_step` now, when the goal carries a structural `target_kind`/`target_name` (block/entity), issues `goto {target: <name>}`; when there is neither an in-reach affordance nor a numeric destination nor a target, it performs a **directional march** — it holds an `_explore_heading`, rotates it by `_EXPLORE_TURN_RAD` only after `_STALE_ARRIVAL_TICKS` stalls, and `goto`s a forward {x,z} point along that heading (`reason="directional_march"`) instead of wandering, so the body travels in a straight line and only turns when actually stuck. (3) **Will-beat prompt** — `core/vessel_beat.py::build_will_prompt` lists the distinct block/entity names from the live `WorldState` scan and *imperatively* asks the model to commit a `target_kind`+`target_name` (verbatim from that list) or a numeric destination, so future goals arrive with a structural route. (4) **Out-of-band drone planner (fallback)** — `interface/vessel_interface.py::_maybe_run_drone_planner` runs from the scheduler as a **separate asyncio task** (never inside a vessel turn — §5c forbids Agent Lane/Drones *during* embodiment turns, and `run_drone` persists an `agent_tasks` row): when the active goal `_goal_has_route()` is False (no structural target and no numeric destination) it dispatches `manager.run_drone(...)` with a free-text brief telling the drone to `scan`/`observe` and then commit exactly one structural waypoint via `vessel_<world>_update_goal`/`set_goal`. The drone context deliberately carries **no** vessel `interface_path`, so it stays out-of-band. Gated by `VESSEL_DRONE_PLAN_INTERVAL_SEC` (default 120, clamped `[30, 3600]`, `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py`).
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/goals.py` (target_kind/target_name + `_coerce_target`), `init-db.sql` (`minecraft_goals` DDL), `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`_goal_target`, motor target-routing + directional march, `_act_goal`/`get_world_actions` threading), `core/vessel_beat.py` (`build_will_prompt` scan-name list + structural-target cue, `_distinct_names`), `interface/vessel_interface.py` (`_goal_has_route`, `_maybe_run_drone_planner`, `_resolve_drone_plan_interval`, `_run_drone_planner`, scheduler wiring), `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` (`VESSEL_DRONE_PLAN_INTERVAL_SEC`). **Location (added):** `tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py` (motor routing/march), `tests/test_vessel_beat.py` (prompt cue), `tests/test_vessel_interface_drone_planner.py` (`_goal_has_route`, `_resolve_drone_plan_interval`).
**Status:** VERIFIED live e2e. Deployed via `docker cp` + `docker restart synth`. Live `synth.log` shows the will beat setting a goal with `destination={'x': 350.0, 'z': 160.0}` and the motor tick then issuing `goto` with `remaining` decreasing **monotonically** (27.5→20.8→13.5→6.0→`arrived`), then reprojecting a fresh straight-line waypoint and repeating — a straight march, never circling. The out-of-band drone planner correctly does **not** fire while goals already carry a route (`_goal_has_route` True). ruff format+check clean; `ty check` clean on edited files; scoped pytest combined (`tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py tests/test_vessel_beat.py tests/test_vessel_interface_drone_planner.py`) → 66 passed.
**Notes:** No keyword/content matching anywhere — the structural `target_kind` is a closed enum + a live-scan id, the motor never parses goal text, the will-beat cue lists scan-derived names for the model to echo verbatim, and the drone-planner gate keys purely on the structural presence of `target_kind`/`target_name`/`destination`. Respects §5c: motor and will beat stay Fast Lane; the drone planner is dispatched **out-of-band** from the scheduler (a standalone asyncio task with no vessel `interface_path`), never as an in-turn vessel action, so no `agent_tasks` row is created during an embodiment turn. Deploy on this stack is `docker cp <file> synth:/app/<path> && docker restart synth` (no source bind-mount); the Synth DB is a **separate container** — `docker exec synth mysql …` fails with a missing `/run/mysqld/mysqld.sock`; use the `synth-db` MCP tools instead.

### Rift Vessel — Rekku repeated the same in-world line verbatim many times (will-beat prompt invited speech, queued beats coalesced, and the model re-emitted the identical `say`)  <!-- 2026-07-26 -->
**Why:** User report (IT): *"vedo che rekku ha ripetuto molte volte lo stesso messaggio, forse crede che la chat sia fresca?"* DB history for `vessel/minecraft` showed the identical self-line *"Vi sento. Sto continuando a camminare verso l'interno, lasciando la costa."* persisted back-to-back many times (ids 75539/75544/75547/75549 … 75558/75559/75560/75561), i.e. Rekku kept "answering" a conversation that had already been answered.
**Root cause:** THREE combined mechanisms. (1) **Prompt framing** — `core/vessel_beat.py::build_will_prompt` framed the autonomous will beat as a quiet embodiment moment but never told the model *not to speak*; the scrollback history (including a past player line) read as if still awaiting a reply, so on each will beat the model produced a `say`. (2) **In-turn coalescing** — the single synchronous queue consumer is slow (`BASE_CORTEX = selenium-llm-engine`, 90–500 s+/turn), so several autonomous will beats for the same world piled up and were coalesced into one turn that carried N identical prompts → N identical `say`s. (3) **Cross-beat verbatim re-emission** — even after coalescing was fixed, the model independently re-emitted the *same* sentence verbatim on each separate will beat, because nothing compared the pending line against Synth's own most recent utterance.
**Fix:** Three structural, keyword-free layers. (1) `build_will_prompt` now appends a paragraph making the will beat explicitly private: *"…Any conversation you can see above already happened and, if it needed a reply, you already gave one in your own turn — treat it as memory… Do NOT speak, greet, or repeat a message here; you are reflecting, not talking… return no `say` action."* (2) `core/message_queue.py::_supersede_pending_vessel_beats(world_chat_id)` — mirroring `_drop_stale_vessel_perceptions`, on a fresh autonomous vessel beat it prunes older *autonomous* pending beats for the same world from the priority heap (skips `vessel_player_chat` and `no_compact`; heapifies; decrements `_queue._unfinished_tasks` per drop), so N stacked beats collapse to the freshest one. (3) `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` — a structural identity gate in `act()`: before dispatching a `say`, `_is_repeat_of_last_self_say(environment, payload)` reads the `vessel/<environment>` context, finds the most recent `username=="self"` line and, if its trimmed text equals the pending trimmed text, suppresses the action (`VesselActionResult(ok=True, detail="suppressed_self_repeat")`, no connector call, no history write). It compares only against Synth's OWN last line — echoing a *player's* line is never suppressed.
**Location (edited):** `core/vessel_beat.py` (`build_will_prompt` private-moment paragraph), `core/message_queue.py` (`_supersede_pending_vessel_beats` + wiring in the autonomous NORMAL_PRIORITY branch of `enqueue`), `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` (`act()` self-repeat gate + `_is_repeat_of_last_self_say`). **Location (added):** `tests/test_message_queue.py` (`test_supersede_pending_vessel_beats_keeps_only_fresh_autonomous`), `tests/test_vessel_action_namespacing.py` (`test_verbatim_self_repeat_say_is_suppressed`, `test_new_say_after_self_line_is_dispatched`, `test_self_repeat_gate_ignores_non_self_lines`).
**Status:** VERIFIED live e2e. All three deployed via `docker cp` + `docker restart synth`. Live log confirmed the supersede (`message_queue.py:250 [QUEUE] Superseded 1 older autonomous vessel beat(s) for 'vessel/minecraft' with a fresher one`) and, after the plugin re-deploy, the self-repeat gate firing (`vessel_plugin.py:297 [vessel_plugin] Suppressed verbatim self-repeat 'say' in 'minecraft' (identical to last self line)`). DB history post-deploy shows a single self-line (id 75568) with no consecutive identical repeat afterward, whereas the pre-deploy ids 75558–75561 were verbatim duplicates. ruff format+check clean; `ty check` clean on the 3 edited files; scoped pytest → `tests/test_message_queue.py` 8 passed, `tests/test_vessel_action_namespacing.py` 18 passed, `tests/test_vessel_beat.py` 22 passed.
**Notes:** No keyword/content matching anywhere — layer (1) is framing text, (2) keys on the structural `vessel`/world/`vessel_player_chat`/`no_compact` flags, (3) compares the pending line's identity against Synth's own last `self` line (multilingual-safe). The three layers are complementary: the prompt reduces *why* a duplicate `say` is produced, the supersede kills *in-turn* N-copy coalescing, and the plugin gate is the last-resort guard against the model's *cross-beat* verbatim re-emission. Respects §5c (Fast Lane only). Deploy on this stack is `docker cp <file> synth:/app/<path> && docker restart synth` (no source bind-mount).

### Rift Vessel — in-world player chat starved behind the slow Selenium will-beat backlog (all vessel perceptions shared HIGH_PRIORITY)  <!-- 2026-07-26 -->
**Why:** User report (IT): *"il synth sembra non vedere i messaggi in chat."* A live in-world message addressing Synth (`Rekku, mi vedi?`) was correctly polled, persisted to `vessel/minecraft` history and enqueued (`skip_mention_check=True`) with `vessel_minecraft_say` in the scoped allowlist — yet Synth never replied in-world.
**Root cause:** The message queue has a **single synchronous consumer** that processes one turn at a time via `await asyncio.wait_for(processing_task, 300s)`. `BASE_CORTEX = selenium-llm-engine` is non-cancellable and slow, so each autonomous **will-beat** turn takes 90–500 s+. Will beats are *produced* roughly every `VESSEL_WILL_INTERVAL_SEC` (45 s) but *consumed* at >90 s → an unbounded backlog. The old code raised **all** vessel perceptions (will beats, sightings, movement *and* player chat) to `HIGH_PRIORITY` (0) indiscriminately, so a real player chat sat at the *same* priority as the backlog and — FIFO within a priority via the monotonic tie-break counter — was starved behind dozens of already-queued autonomous will beats. `VESSEL_WILL_QUIET_SEC` only *defers the next* will beat; it does not drain the ones already in the queue.
**Fix:** Structural, actor-based priority split — never keyword/intent matching. (1) `interface/vessel_interface.py::_enqueue_perception` tags a perception with `_vessel_player_chat = (event_type == "chat" and bool(actor))` (a human speaking, not one of Synth's own `sighting`/`movement`/`will_beat` perceptions). (2) `core/message_queue.py::enqueue` now, while a vessel session is active and `interface == "vessel"`, raises **only real player chat** to `HIGH_PRIORITY` and keeps **autonomous** vessel perceptions at `NORMAL_PRIORITY` (they no longer compete with the player). (3) On player-chat arrival, `_drop_stale_vessel_perceptions(chat_id)` prunes already-queued *autonomous* vessel perceptions for the same world scope from the priority heap (guarded, decrements `_queue._unfinished_tasks` / sets `_queue._finished`), so a fresh player address is answered immediately instead of after the whole stale backlog drains. The trainer exemption and other-interface deprioritisation are unchanged.
**Location (edited):** `interface/vessel_interface.py` (`_enqueue_perception`: `is_player_chat`/`_vessel_player_chat`/`_no_compact`), `core/message_queue.py` (item dict `vessel_player_chat`; priority branch for player-chat→HIGH / autonomous→NORMAL; new helper `_drop_stale_vessel_perceptions`). **Location (added):** `tests/test_vessel_realtime.py` (player-chat→HIGH, autonomous→NORMAL, ordering HIGH<NORMAL<AGENT, telegram unchanged), `tests/test_message_queue.py` (`test_drop_stale_vessel_perceptions_prunes_only_autonomous`).
**Status:** VERIFIED live e2e. Deployed via `docker compose up -d --build`; the NORMAL/HIGH split confirmed in `synth.log` (`message_queue.py:702 … autonomous in-world perception set to NORMAL_PRIORITY` and `message_queue.py:683 … in-world PLAYER chat raised to HIGH_PRIORITY`). A throwaway mineflayer tester bot ("TesterBot") joined the same server (`100.113.224.83:44383`) and spoke `Rekku, mi vedi?`; Synth replied in-world via `vessel_minecraft_say` — tester received `<Rekku> Vi sento. Sto continuando a camminare verso l'interno, lasciando la costa.` ruff format+check clean; `ty check` clean on the 2 edited Python files; combined scoped pytest (`tests/test_vessel_realtime.py tests/test_message_queue.py`) → 27 passed.
**Notes:** Distinct from the two prior vessel entries (will-beat *deferral* and `no_compact` coalescing) — this is a **queue-priority starvation** caused by a slow non-cancellable engine producing will beats faster than they can be consumed. No keyword logic — player chat is identified purely by event kind (`chat`) + actor presence, which is multilingual-safe. Respects §5c: vessel actions stay Fast Lane, no Agent Lane/Drones, single end-of-session diary. When pruning un-`get()`-ted heap items you must manually decrement `_queue._unfinished_tasks` (and set `_queue._finished` when it reaches 0) or `join()` will hang.

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — Node bridge runtime made a self-contained, distributable part of the plugin package (`plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/mineflayer/`)  <!-- 2026-07-26 -->
**Why:** User directive (IT): *"immagina che questo plugin di minecraft lo scarichiamo da un sito web e lo installiamo in synth: deve essere un pacchetto (magari viene come zip) con dentro tutto quello che gli serve. Quindi il binario di mineflayer deve essere dentro la cartella plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/mineflayer."* The Mineflayer Node runtime had to live **inside the plugin package** as a committed, zip-shippable folder — not be generated at runtime into a gitignored generic `bridge_runtime/` dir, and not live under a shared `/opt/minecraft_bridge` path that is lost when the container is recreated.
**Implementation:** `interface/minecraft_provisioner.py`: the bridge runtime subfolder is now `mineflayer` (`_BRIDGE_RUNTIME_SUBDIR`), so the default (non-explicit-root) `_bridge_root` is `<repo>/plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/mineflayer`; `bridge.json`/`bridge.log`/`node_modules` all live there, and `NODE_PATH` is prepended with `mineflayer/node_modules` so `require('mineflayer')` resolves against the package's own folder. The bridge *script* stays in-place one level up (`minecraft_bridge_minimal.js`) and is executed with that `NODE_PATH`. `install()` now treats `package.json` as **committed source** (only writes a defensive fallback if somehow absent) and runs `npm install` into `mineflayer/`. A committed `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/mineflayer/package.json` pins the deps (`mineflayer` ^4.20.0, `mineflayer-pathfinder` ^2.4.5, `minecraft-data` ^3.78.0); a `README.md` documents the package layout (package.json committed; node_modules/bridge.json/bridge.log are per-run artefacts, installed at first run or bundled in the shipped zip for a fully offline package).
**Location (edited):** `interface/minecraft_provisioner.py` (`_BRIDGE_RUNTIME_SUBDIR`, `_bridge_root`/`_state_file`/`_log_file` derivation, `_bridge_env` NODE_PATH comment, `install()` package.json-as-source), `.gitignore` (drop `bridge_runtime/`; ignore only `mineflayer/{bridge.json,bridge.log,package-lock.json}`; `node_modules/` already globally ignored), `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/guide.md` (bridge.log path). **Location (added):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/mineflayer/package.json`, `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/mineflayer/README.md`.
**Status:** VERIFIED live e2e in-container from a clean (bridge-runtime-absent) state. `BridgeProvisioner()` resolves `bridge_root=/app/plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/mineflayer`; `install()` ran `npm install` there (`deps_present` False→True, 3 required modules present under `mineflayer/node_modules`); `start()` spawned the bridge (pid 405) and `/health` reported `mineflayer: True` with no EADDRINUSE. ruff format+check clean; `ty check` clean on `interface/minecraft_provisioner.py`; `tests/test_minecraft_provisioner.py` → 13 passed. Deployed via `docker cp` (provisioner + package.json + README) into the container. Test bridge reaped (`fuser -k 8137/tcp`) and stale `bridge.json` cleared afterwards.
**Notes:** The whole `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/` tree is now self-sufficient and can be shipped as a zip. `node_modules/` is installed at first run into the package folder (or can be pre-bundled in the zip for an offline package). Tests use an explicit `bridge_root` override (`MINECRAFT_BRIDGE_ROOT` / constructor arg) so the subfolder rename doesn't affect them. No keyword logic; full type hints; fail-safe guards preserved.

### Rift Vessel — Synth ignored a player addressing her in-world because the autonomous will beat's "on your own" prompt swallowed the turn  <!-- 2026-07-26 -->
**Why:** User report (IT): *"il synth non legge le chat, ho appena provato e mi ha ignorato pare."* A live in-world message (`XargonWan: Rekku, mi leggi?`) was correctly polled, enqueued (`skip_mention_check=True`) and passed mention/salience — and `vessel_minecraft_say` was in the scoped action allowlist — yet Synth returned `{"actions": []}` and never replied.
**Root cause:** The turn that actually ran was the autonomous **will beat**, whose prompt is framed by `core/vessel_beat.py::build_will_prompt` as *"[Embodiment — a quiet moment to reflect while you play in the minecraft world, on your own.]"*. The will beat fires on its own fixed timer (`VESSEL_WILL_INTERVAL_SEC`, 45 s) with **no awareness** that a player had just addressed Synth. So the LLM ran on the "you are alone" volition prompt, kept its goal and emitted no actions; the player's chat only sat in scrollback history, never as the turn's primary input.
**Fix:** Structural, actor-based deferral of the will beat while a real player is present — never keyword/intent matching. `interface/vessel_interface.py`: new monotonic clock `self._last_player_activity_at`, updated in `on_world_event` whenever a *salient* in-world `chat` perception carrying an actor (`entity`) is received (a human speaking — not one of Synth's own synthetic `sighting`/`movement`/`will_beat` perceptions). In `_maybe_run_will_beat`, if a player interacted within `VESSEL_WILL_QUIET_SEC` (new config, default 60 s, clamped `[0, 3600]`, `0` disables) the beat is skipped — so the already-enqueued player chat is processed as an ordinary reactive turn instead of running coincident with the "on your own" volition beat. New pure helper `core.vessel_beat.resolve_will_quiet_sec` (fail-safe, clamped, unit-tested).
**Location (edited):** `core/vessel_beat.py` (`resolve_will_quiet_sec`), `interface/vessel_interface.py` (`_last_player_activity_at` init + recording in `on_world_event` + defer gate in `_maybe_run_will_beat`), `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` (register `VESSEL_WILL_QUIET_SEC`). **Location (added):** `tests/test_vessel_beat.py` (`test_resolve_will_quiet_sec_default_and_clamp`, `test_resolve_will_quiet_sec_failsafe_on_error`).
**Status:** Deployed via `docker cp` (3 files) + `docker restart synth` (no source bind-mount). ruff format+check clean; `ty check` clean on the 3 edited Python files; `tests/test_vessel_beat.py` → 22 passed. Live in-world verification (a direct address now gets a reply because the will beat defers) pending the next player interaction.
**Notes:** No keyword logic — the deferral is decided purely from event kind (`chat`) + actor presence (`entity`), which is multilingual-safe. Respects §5c: Fast Lane only, no Agent Lane/Drones, single end-of-session diary. `VESSEL_WILL_QUIET_SEC` documented in AGENTS.md §5c and §14; a §12 known-issues entry records the bug and fix.

### Rift Vessel — three embodiment/autonomy behaviours: en-route element collection, longer randomized interruptible travel, and a `Self:` prefix on Synth's own in-world chat  <!-- 2026-07-26 -->
**Why:** User TODO (IT), three points. (1) *"quando il synth cammina … il client deve raccogliere tutti gli elementi che ha visto … se magari c'è qualcosa di raro, un quest item … possa cambiare direzione o … comunicarlo ad altri giocatori"* — while travelling A→B the body must notice new things it passes so cognition can divert / revisit / mention them. (2) *"il synth dovrebbe fare percorsi più lunghi (almeno 3/4 volte più lunghi ma … un random) … non dovrebbe rimanere bloccato fino all'arrivo"* — longer, randomized, still-interruptible travel legs. (3) *"il synth risponde in chat a se stesso, i messaggi del synth devono essere Self: messaggio, come su telegram, discord"* — Synth's own in-world speech must render `Self:` in history like every other chat interface.
**Implementation:**
- **(1) En-route element collection — Rift Vessel CORE (world-agnostic).** `interface/vessel_interface.py`: new per-session sighting registry `self._seen_elements: dict[str, set[str]]` (scope `vessel/<world>` → set of `kind:target` keys). After each fast motor tick, `_collect_en_route_sightings(world, world_state)` reads the connector's structural `WorldState.extra["affordances"]` (`{kind, target, verb, distance}`, distance-sorted), keys novelty on `_element_signature` (`kind:target`, `kind` defaults `"thing"`; `None` for malformed), and surfaces each *first* sighting once as a `sighting` perception via `on_world_event(event_type="sighting", …, data={kind,target,distance})`. The registry records **everything** seen (so Synth *knows* it and can `observe` to recall) while the existing dedup/rate-limit salience filter paces what reaches cognition. `end_sessions_for_environment` pops the world's registry so each session starts fresh. Fully guarded, keyword-free, no LLM.
- **(2) Longer randomized interruptible travel.** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py`: `_explore_leg_distance()` = `random.uniform(3.0, 4.0) * _MIN_TRAVEL_DISTANCE` (16 → ~48–64 blocks) used by `_reproject_forward`; the Node bridge `minecraft_bridge_minimal.js` `wander` now uses `radius` 48 (clamp 8–128) with `dist = minLeg + rand*(radius−minLeg)`. Travel remains interruptible because the fast motor tick re-evaluates every `VESSEL_MOTOR_INTERVAL_SEC` and the will beat can change the goal mid-leg.
- **(3) `Self:` on own speech.** `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py`: on a successful `say`, `_persist_self_speech` writes the line to `vessel/<world>` history with the canonical `sender_name="self"` / `sender_id="self"`, so it renders `Self:` exactly like Telegram/Discord. The bridge also drops the bot's own chat echo (`if (username === b.username) return;`) to avoid a perception feedback loop.
**Location (edited):** `interface/vessel_interface.py` (`_seen_elements`, `_element_signature`, `_collect_en_route_sightings`, `_goal_from_world_state`, `end_sessions_for_environment` cleanup), `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` (`_persist_self_speech`), `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`_explore_leg_distance`, `_reproject_forward`), `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft_bridge_minimal.js` (`wander`, own-chat drop). **Location (added):** `tests/test_vessel_realtime.py` (en-route sighting block: structural signature, new-once surfacing, only-new surfacing, no-state no-op).
**Status:** VERIFIED live e2e. Deployed via `docker cp` (4 files) + `docker restart synth` (no source bind-mount). Post-restart `synth.log` shows the motor tick doing a multi-tick `goto dest={x:350,z:160}` counting down 11.5→4.8 blocks (long interruptible leg, point 2) and a stream of distinct `sighting` events; `chat_history_cache` for `vessel/minecraft` contains the surfaced sightings (`"You notice a block nearby: red_terracotta (~5 blocks away)"` etc., point 1). Point-3 code (`_persist_self_speech`, `sender_name="self"`) confirmed present in-container; renders `Self:` when Synth next speaks in-world. ruff format+check clean; `ty check` clean on the edited Python files; scoped vessel suite → 104 passed.
**Notes:** All three are CORE-vs-adapter correct per §5c scope rule: en-route collection + `Self:` are common to most worlds → Rift Vessel CORE; the concrete leg distance is Minecraft-specific → the connector/bridge. No keyword logic anywhere — sightings key on the structural affordance contract only. `sighting` is a new perception event type on the CORE. Vessel actions stay Fast Lane; no mid-session diary; goals remain self-authored.

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — `/vessel join minecraft` failed: "the Minecraft bridge did not come up at http://127.0.0.1:8137" because a stale PID in bridge.json made the provisioner think the bridge was already running  <!-- 2026-07-26 -->
**Why:** User report (IT): a live `/vessel join minecraft` from the Telegram trainer (Rekku) failed with `❌ Could not enter minecraft: ... the Minecraft bridge did not come up at http://127.0.0.1:8137 (Cannot connect to host 127.0.0.1:8137 ssl:default Connect call failed ('127.0.0.1', 8137))`. This blocked live verification of every in-world criterion (multi-step goals, motor reactivity, chat addressing).
**Root cause:** `interface/minecraft_provisioner.py` persists the Node bridge PID to `/opt/minecraft_bridge/bridge.json`. That file **survives a container rebuild/restart**, but the recorded PID does not — after a rebuild the Node bridge is gone and its old PID gets reassigned by the kernel to some unrelated system process. The provisioner's `_running_pid()` validated the state-file PID with `_pid_alive()` (`os.kill(pid, 0)`), which returns True for **any** live process with that PID. So on the first join after a rebuild, `start()` saw `_running_pid()` truthy → returned `{"ok": True, "detail": "already running"}` **without launching the bridge** → nothing ever bound port 8137 → the connector's `/health` poll on `127.0.0.1:8137` failed → `connect` returned False → "the Minecraft bridge did not come up". The provisioner logged nothing during the failing connect precisely because it hit the early "already running" return before any `log_info`.
**Fix:** Added a structural PID-identity check. New `@staticmethod _pid_is_bridge(pid)` reads `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` and confirms it references the bridge script (`minecraft_bridge_minimal.js`); it falls back to `_pid_alive()` only when `/proc` is absent/unreadable, and returns False on `ProcessLookupError`/`FileNotFoundError`. `_running_pid()` now uses `self._pid_is_bridge(pid)` for the state-file PID (the in-memory `self._proc` path is unchanged). A stale/reassigned PID therefore resolves to "not running", so `start()` actually launches the bridge instead of falsely short-circuiting.
**Location (edited):** `interface/minecraft_provisioner.py` (`_pid_is_bridge`, `_running_pid`).
**Status:** VERIFIED at the provisioner/unit level in-container. Reproduced the exact bug state (`bridge.json` with `pid: 260`, process dead): the fixed provisioner reports `status()` → `running: False`, `pid: None` and `_running_pid()` → `None`, i.e. the next join will genuinely start the bridge. Positive path also proven: from `running: False`, `await provisioner.start()` logged `bridge started (pid=260)` and `status()` flipped to `running: True`; `bridge.log` confirmed `listening on http://127.0.0.1:8137` and `logged in as Rekku`. ruff format+check clean; `ty check` clean on `interface/minecraft_provisioner.py`; `tests/test_minecraft_provisioner.py` → 13 passed. Deployed via `docker cp interface/minecraft_provisioner.py synth:/app/interface/minecraft_provisioner.py && docker restart synth` (no source bind-mount); stale `bridge.json` cleared on restart.
**Notes:** Never trust `os.kill(pid, 0)` alone for a persisted PID after a process/container lifecycle boundary — PIDs are reassigned; validate identity via `/proc/<pid>/cmdline`. The synth container has no `ps`; enumerate `/proc/[0-9]*/cmdline` (`tr "\0" " "`). `provisioner.start()` is a coroutine — must be awaited. An isolated `python3 -c` harness is not representative of the long-lived main process (its event loop closing can reap the child node, and its plugin registry is empty so worlds read as disabled); the fix is proven at the provisioner level, which is exactly where the bug lived. Live end-to-end stable-session verification from a real main-process `/vessel join` is still pending (the selenium `BASE_CORTEX` gets stuck in the corrector loop when driven via the OpenAI-compat API, so the LLM route did not reliably emit `vessel_connect`).

### Cortex (selenium-llm-engine) — vessel/heavy turns replied only `OK`: the assembled prompt overran the engine's 32000-char chunking threshold and its "reply only OK" protocol contaminated the shared browser session  <!-- 2026-07-26 -->
**Why:** Under embodiment the will beat fired every 45 s and the model *did* run, yet in-world it emitted `vessel_minecraft_say(text="OK")` and a diary "Acknowledged instruction to reply OK" — with **no such instruction anywhere in the SyntH prompt** (the will-beat prompt was clean and complete: real history, emotions, soul profile, actions). This blocked goal-setting (crit 4), chat replies (crit 6) and cognitive movement (crit 5).
**Root cause:** `BASE_CORTEX = selenium-llm-engine` is browser-driven and **retains conversation state in the browser tab across requests**. When any turn's fully-assembled prompt exceeds the engine's per-model char limit (**32000** for the active `2.5-flash`), the engine splits it with its own chunking protocol ("reply only OK / parte 1 … / parte 2 …"). That framing stays alive in the tab, so **subsequent** turns — even small, clean ones — are answered with `OK`. The vessel action catalog is large, so vessel prompts routinely serialized to 37k–47k chars and tripped chunking every will beat. Two earlier mitigations failed: lowering SyntH's up-front reducer budget (`_DEFAULT_MAX_PROMPT_CHARS`) had no effect (the reducer floor for vessel prompts is above the budget, and the action catalog + JSON role-split are injected *after* the reducer), and a first downstream clamp at budget 30000 still chunked — because the clamp measures message **content** length while the engine chunks on the **serialized** JSON payload, which is ~2600 chars larger (role labels, JSON framing/escaping). Live proof: a content-sum clamped to 30000 produced a real selenium payload of 32597 chars → chunked → `OK`.
**Fix:** Added a downstream character-budget clamp on the fully-assembled OpenAI-style messages in `ExternalCortexEngine.generate_response` (dict/str path), enforced by `_clamp_messages_to_char_budget`: it computes the total message-content length and, when over budget, trims **only non-system messages from the front** (preserving the system prompt + injected action catalog intact and keeping image parts), appending a "context trimmed" marker. `_DEFAULT_DOWNSTREAM_CHAR_BUDGET` is set to **27000** = 32000 − ~2600 serialization overhead − ~2400 safety margin, so even the heaviest vessel turn serializes to ~29600 < 32000 and the engine's chunking path (and its `OK` contamination) is never triggered. Overridable per-endpoint via `extra_config["downstream_char_budget"]`; a non-positive value disables it. The `isinstance(messages, list)` fast path is intentionally left unclamped (it caps `max_tokens` instead and is not the vessel path).
**Location (edited):** `core/external_endpoints/bridges/cortex_bridge.py` (`_DEFAULT_DOWNSTREAM_CHAR_BUDGET`, `_downstream_char_budget`, `_message_content_len`, `_truncate_message_content`, `_clamp_messages_to_char_budget`, clamp call in `generate_response`). **Location (added):** `tests/test_cortex_bridge_multimodal.py` (24 cases: content-len for str/list, truncation preserving image parts, and the clamp firing/not-firing at explicit budgets).
**Status:** VERIFIED live e2e. Injected a 73043-char selenium turn via the OpenAI-compat API; `synth.log` logged `downstream payload 73043 chars exceeded budget 27000; trimmed ~46043` (content clamped to 27000) while `synth-selenium-llm-engine` logged **no** `Prompt chunking` line — the payload stayed under 32000, so no chunking and no `OK` contamination. ruff format+check clean; `ty check` clean on `cortex_bridge.py`; `tests/test_cortex_bridge_multimodal.py` → 24 passed.
**Notes:** The selenium engine itself is **never modified** (explicit user constraint). No keyword/text logic — the clamp is purely length-based and role-aware. Fix applies to every openai-protocol endpoint but is a no-op for endpoints whose payloads are already below budget. Deploy on this stack is `docker cp core/external_endpoints/bridges/cortex_bridge.py synth:/app/core/external_endpoints/bridges/cortex_bridge.py && docker restart synth` (the synth container has no source bind-mount).

### Rift Vessel — the autonomy scheduler capped the fast motor reflex at 10 s, so the body reacted sluggishly (not fluid)  <!-- 2026-07-25 -->
**Why:** User report (IT): "il synth è molto lento a reagire, deve essere più fluido, non è accettabile che passino minuti da un round all'altro." Live logs showed the motor tick firing on a fixed ~10 s cadence (`21:12:08 / :18 / :28 / :38`) instead of the intended `VESSEL_MOTOR_INTERVAL_SEC` (default 3 s) — the fast, no-LLM motorics layer that is supposed to keep the body reactive was throttled to the slow tick.
**Root cause:** `interface/vessel_interface.py`'s autonomy scheduler slept a fixed `_TICK_SEC = 10.0` between iterations. The will beat (slow, LLM, 45 s) and cooldown sweep are self-throttled by their own timestamp checks, but the motor tick has no such internal floor above the loop period — so the loop's fixed 10 s sleep became the *effective* motor cadence regardless of `VESSEL_MOTOR_INTERVAL_SEC`.
**Fix:** The scheduler now sleeps a **dynamic** interval: `_resolve_tick_sec()` returns `max(_MIN_TICK_SEC, min(_TICK_SEC, VESSEL_MOTOR_INTERVAL_SEC))` (new `_MIN_TICK_SEC = 1.0`; `_TICK_SEC = 10.0` kept as an upper bound for the will/cooldown layers). Fully guarded — any error resolving the motor interval falls back to `_TICK_SEC`. The will beat and cooldown remain correctly paced by their existing per-iteration timestamp gates, so they are unaffected by the faster loop.
**Location (edited):** `interface/vessel_interface.py` (`_MIN_TICK_SEC`, `_resolve_tick_sec`, scheduler loop `asyncio.sleep`).
**Status:** VERIFIED live e2e. After `docker compose up -d --build synth`, logs show `motor tick: goto (interval=3s)` at `21:17:33 / :36 / :39` — a clean 3 s gap (was 10 s), with the will beat still firing every 45 s in parallel without blocking the motor. ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean on `interface/vessel_interface.py`.
**Notes:** No keyword/text logic; purely a scheduler-cadence fix. This addresses the *body* fluidity only. Round-to-round *cognition* latency (chat / will-beat LLM turns) is intrinsic to `BASE_CORTEX = selenium-llm-engine` (browser-driven, `AWAIT_RESPONSE_TIMEOUT = 600`) and was left unchanged per the user's explicit constraint "non cambiare nessun engine, eg cortex." Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build synth`.

### Rift Vessel — a personal diary entry was written mid-session during embodiment, violating the single end-of-session diary constraint (§5c)  <!-- 2026-07-25 -->
**Why:** Once the previous fix pinned the will-beat turn to the Fast Lane, its actions actually executed — including a `create_personal_diary_entry` the model emitted **during** the embodiment session. AGENTS.md §5c is explicit: while a Vessel session is active, events accumulate in the `experience_buffer` and **exactly one** autobiographical "lived experience" diary entry is written **only at end-of-session** (explicit logout or `VESSEL_SESSION_COOLDOWN_SEC` inactivity). A mid-session diary fragments that single lived-experience narrative.
**Root cause:** `core/history_engine.py::build_context` sets `enable_diary=False` for a vessel-focus turn, but that flag only scopes **context assembly** (which memories/diary are injected into the prompt) — it does **not** block the diary *action* from being emitted or executed. So the diary plugin happily persisted a new row every time a will beat's batch happened to include `create_personal_diary_entry`.
**Fix:** (1) Promoted the vessel-embodiment detection to a single canonical structural helper `core/interface_path_utils.py::is_vessel_embodiment_context(context)` (routing metadata only, never message text: `vessel_focus` truthy, or `interface == "vessel"`, or `interface_path`/`chat_id` a str starting with `"vessel"`). (2) `core/agent_router.py::_is_vessel_embodiment_turn` now delegates to that shared helper (single source of truth, was a private duplicate). (3) Added a guarded structural gate at the top of the `create_personal_diary_entry` branch in `plugins/ai_diary/ai_diary.py::execute_action`: when `is_vessel_embodiment_context(context)` is true it **skips the diary write entirely** and returns `{"success": True, "skipped": True, "reason": "vessel_embodiment_no_mid_session_diary"}` — no DB row, no side effect. The end-of-session single diary (written by `vessel_session_manager` on logout/cooldown) is untouched.
