**Location (edited):** `core/interface_path_utils.py` (`is_vessel_embodiment_context`), `core/agent_router.py` (`_is_vessel_embodiment_turn` delegates to the shared helper), `plugins/ai_diary/ai_diary.py` (skip gate in `execute_action`). **Location (added):** `tests/test_vessel_embodiment_context.py` (8 cases: None/non-dict/empty → False; `vessel_focus`/`interface==vessel`/`interface_path` prefix/`chat_id` prefix → True; telegram → False; `interface_path="telegram_bot/vessel"` → False since it is not a prefix).
**Status:** Code DONE + validated + deployed. ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean on `interface_path_utils.py` and `agent_router.py` (the 35 pre-existing diagnostics in `ai_diary.py` are legacy `= None` param defaults, unrelated, left untouched); scoped pytest → 39 passed; `docker compose up -d --build synth` rebuilt. **Live e2e re-verification pending** a working cortex engine that reliably emits `vessel_connect` to open a session (during this session both the harmonyai engine — stuck in the corrector-middleware loop — and the selenium engine — returning empty replies with no actions — failed to open a vessel session via the OpenAI-compat API, so the diary-skip could not be exercised live yet).
**Notes:** No keyword/text matching — detection is purely structural routing metadata, reused identically by `agent_router` (Fast-Lane pinning) and `ai_diary` (diary skip); the helper and both call sites are fully guarded (any error → False / no skip). `update_emotion_state`, also emitted mid-turn, is intentionally **not** blocked — only the diary violates §5c. Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build synth`.

### Rift Vessel — autonomous will beat was misrouted to the Agent Lane, so self-authored goals never persisted (bot frozen)  <!-- 2026-07-25 -->
**Why:** After the goal-relevant-travel fix, live agent-mode verification in the survival world showed autonomy still stalled: the will beat fired every 45 s and the model *did* emit `vessel_minecraft_set_goal`, yet no active goal ever reached `minecraft_goals` (stuck at id=5 `done`) and `motor_step` kept returning `no_goal` — the body never moved on its own.
**Root cause:** The vessel will-beat cognition turn was being **misrouted to the Agent Lane**. `core/message_chain.py` logged `🤖 Agent Lane engaged for this turn` then `Actions result: 0 successful, 0 failed` — the already-emitted `vessel_minecraft_set_goal` was never dispatched (the Agent Lane's `run_agentic_turn` does not execute pre-emitted actions), so no goal row was written. The trigger: the message context carried `agent_needed`/`agent_task_title` (set pre-LLM by the `recon_agent_intent` recon plugin), and `core/agent_router.py::classify()` returns AGENT whenever `context.get("agent_needed")` is truthy. This violated AGENTS.md §5c — **vessel actions declare no `external_effects` and must never leave the Fast Lane**.
**Fix:** Added a structural vessel-embodiment gate to `agent_router.classify()`. New guarded helper `_is_vessel_embodiment_turn(context)` mirrors the canonical structural detection in `core/history_engine.py::build_context` (routing metadata only, never message text): true when `context["vessel_focus"]` is truthy, or `context["interface"] == "vessel"`, or `context["interface_path"]`/`context["chat_id"]` is a str starting with `"vessel"`. `classify()` now returns FAST for any vessel embodiment turn **before** the `agent_needed` gate, so vessel turns are pinned to the Fast Lane and their actions run via `run_actions`.
**Location (edited):** `core/agent_router.py` (`_is_vessel_embodiment_turn` helper + FAST gate in `classify()`), `tests/test_agentic_runtime.py` (new `test_router_vessel_embodiment_stays_fast_even_when_agent_needed` covering interface/interface_path/chat_id/vessel_focus each with `agent_needed=True`).
**Status:** VERIFIED live e2e. After deploy, the next will beat logged `classify: vessel embodiment turn -> FAST lane`, `adopted self-authored goal: "..."`, `act('set_goal') dispatched via 'minecraft' in 3 ms`, and `Actions result: 5 successful, 0 failed`; `minecraft_goals` gained id=6 `status='active'` with `destination {x:185, z:150}`; the motor tick then issued `goto` every 3 s and the bot travelled from ~`(151,134)` to `(183.5, 150.5)` — ~35 blocks toward the self-chosen destination. ruff format+check clean, `ty check` clean on `agent_router.py`, `tests/test_agentic_runtime.py -k router` → 8 passed.
**Notes:** No keyword/text matching — detection is purely structural routing metadata (the `vessel` interface name / `vessel*` path prefix / `vessel_focus` flag), reusing the same signal `history_engine` already uses; the helper is fully guarded (any error → False). This also lets the will-beat turn's other actions (emotion/diary) actually execute, which they silently did not under the Agent Lane. Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build synth`.

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — goal-relevant travel: a pending destination now beats far-off incidental affordances (no more freezing on desert sand)  <!-- 2026-07-25 -->
**Why:** Live agent-mode verification in a survival world showed the body **freezing in place** under autonomy even with a valid numeric goal destination. The will beat authored goal id=5 ("move inland to find a forest/trees for wood") with destination `{x:120, z:100}`, the motor tick fired every interval, and a *direct* manual `goto` moved the bot — yet pure autonomy left it stuck at `(113.5, 92.5)`. The user's governing directive (IT): "il movimento dovrebbe essere attinente all'obbiettivo: se il mio obbiettivo è cercare un mostro e non ce ne sono, vagando in tondo non risolvo niente" — motion must be *goal-relevant*, not aimless circling.
**Root cause:** In `MinecraftConnector.motor_step`, benign affordances were processed **before** the travel destination in *all* cases. In a desert/coast the world snapshot always returns ubiquitous incidental blocks (`sand`, `sandstone`) as benign `use`/`mine` affordances. Even when out of reach (~5 m), the reflex did `goto {target: <sand>}` toward the nearest one, so the body kept re-targeting adjacent scenery and **never advanced toward the chosen coordinate** — the exact "circling in place" the directive forbids.
**Fix:** `motor_step` now computes whether the goal has a **pending travel destination** (destination present *and* horizontal distance `> _ARRIVAL_RADIUS`) up front, and reorders priority purely structurally (still no keyword/text matching): (1) a benign affordance **within `_MOTOR_REACH`** is acted on first — grab what is literally in front of you; (2) otherwise, if a destination is still pending, **steer toward that coordinate** (`goto {x,z[,y]}`) *even when far-off benign affordances exist* — the numeric destination is cognition's structural signal that "what I'm looking for is not here"; (3) only when there is **no pending destination** does the body walk toward a far affordance; (4) arrived (within radius) → local `wander`; (5) nothing + no destination → `wander`.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`motor_step` reordered priority + docstring), `tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py` (new `test_motor_step_pending_destination_wins_over_far_affordance`; existing within-reach/out-of-reach/arrived cases still assert unchanged).
**Status:** VERIFIED live e2e under **pure autonomy** (no manual goto). After deploy + in-process reconnect, with the goal destination set to `(150,135)` the bot travelled from `(119.5, 99.6)` to `(150.5, 133.4)` in one interval and then oscillated locally around the target — directional, goal-relevant travel across ~46 blocks, no longer trapped by surrounding sand. ruff format+check clean, `ty check` clean on `minecraft.py`; `tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py` → 20 passed.
**Notes:** No keyword/text matching — priority is decided from the structural affordance contract (`verb`/`distance`) and the numeric destination only; the reflex never reads the goal free text. Within-reach affordances still win (so incidental pickups en route are not lost). Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build synth`. **Deploy lesson (re-confirmed):** the Mineflayer bridge child only survives if launched by the long-lived MAIN process; after a rebuild/restart, recover by triggering `vessel_connect{game:minecraft}` **in-process** via the OpenAI-compat API (relaunches the bridge + reopens the session + resumes autonomy) — never from a transient one-shot `docker exec python`.

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — new `craft` verb, `game_mode` in world snapshot, and a `place` fix  <!-- 2026-07-25 -->
**Why:** End-to-end autonomous-play verification (agent mode) surfaced three gaps: (1) Synth could mine/place but **could not craft**, so it could never turn gathered materials into tools/blocks; (2) mining reported `ok` yet the inventory stayed empty and there was no way to tell why; (3) `place` timed out and never built anything.
**What:**
- **`craft` verb (Minecraft-specific, per the scope rule).** New world-specific verb `vessel_minecraft_craft` added on the Minecraft connector — required `item` (lowercase item id, e.g. `oak_planks`/`stick`/`crafting_table`/`wooden_pickaxe`), optional `count` (clamped `[1, 64]`), `search_radius`, `timeout_ms`; `security_level: "low"`, **no** `external_effects`. The bridge `case 'craft'` resolves the recipe via `bot.recipesFor()`/`bot.craft()` against per-version `minecraft-data` (`minecraftData(bot.version)`), **auto-locates a nearby `crafting_table` and pathfinds to it** when a 3×3 recipe needs one, and returns a structural fail-safe result (`ok:false` with a clear reason) when materials or a reachable table are missing — no keyword logic.
- **`game_mode` in the world snapshot.** `worldSnapshot()` now also returns `game_mode` (e.g. `creative`/`survival`) via `bot.game.gameMode`, so cognition can reason about world limitations. This resolved the "mining yields nothing" report: the server was in **creative**, where mined blocks produce **no drops** (vanilla behaviour, not a bug).
- **`place` fix.** The old `place` anchored on the block directly **below the bot** and targeted its top face `(0,1,0)` — a cell the bot's own body occupies, so `blockUpdate` never fired and the call timed out. The verb now scans reference-block candidates around the feet, picks the first `(refBlock, faceVector)` whose resulting target cell is **air and clear of the bot's two body cells**, `lookAt`s the target, then places — no keyword logic, fully guarded.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft_bridge_minimal.js` (`case 'craft'`, `game_mode` in `worldSnapshot()`, rewritten `case 'place'`), `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`craft` schema in `get_world_actions()` + `"craft"` in `possible_actions`), `AGENTS.md` §5c (scope rule, spontaneity rule, Minecraft world-specific verbs).
**Status:** craft VERIFIED live e2e — `oak_planks` from `oak_log` (no table, `used_table:false`), `crafting_table`+`stick`, and `wooden_pickaxe` using an auto-located table (`used_table:true`), inventory updated correctly; fail-safe path returns `ok:false` with a clear reason when materials/table are missing. `game_mode` VERIFIED (`creative`). `place` rewrite: `node --check` clean, live reconnect verification pending. ruff format+check + ty clean on `minecraft.py`.
**Notes:** No keyword/text matching — recipe resolution is `minecraft-data`-driven, affordances stay structural. Bridge JS change takes effect on reconnect (the provisioner re-copies the JS from `/app` src at each bridge start) or on a container rebuild. Connect/reconnect must be triggered **in-process** in the live backend via an LLM action (the backend interpreter `/app/.venv/bin/python` has `aiohttp`); a standalone `docker exec python3` connect fails (no `aiohttp` + fresh default config). Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build synth`.

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — skin never applied because only the SkinsRestorer *plugin* syntax was sent  <!-- 2026-08-11 -->
**Symptom:** Skin upload succeeded and the connector logged `skin command sent: /skin url <url>`, but the skin never appeared. The user runs the **SkinRestorer *mod*** by Lionarius (Fabric/Forge/NeoForge/Quilt, Modrinth), whose command syntax is `/skin set web <model> "<url>"` (URL must be double-quoted) — the classic `/skin url <url>` is the *SkinsRestorer plugin* (Bukkit/Spigot) syntax, so the mod silently ignored it. User request (IT): "metti il supporto per entrambi, provali tutti al login lol" — support both providers and try all syntaxes at spawn.
**Root cause:** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py::_apply_skin()` sent a **single** command from `MINECRAFT_SKIN_COMMAND_TEMPLATE` (default `/skin url {url}`), which matched only one of the two common skin providers.
**Fix:** New static helper `_skin_command_templates()` resolves an ordered list of commands (first non-empty wins): `MINECRAFT_SKIN_COMMAND_TEMPLATES` (newline-separated list) → the legacy single `MINECRAFT_SKIN_COMMAND_TEMPLATE` → the built-in defaults covering **both** providers (`/skin set web {model} "{url}"` then `/skin url {url}`). `_apply_skin()` now loops over the resolved list, substitutes `{url}`/`{model}`, dedupes, and POSTs each to the bridge `skin` action best-effort — the server accepts the syntax it understands and ignores the rest, with **no keyword logic** (templates are static config strings). New config key `MINECRAFT_SKIN_COMMAND_TEMPLATES` (advanced, empty); legacy `MINECRAFT_SKIN_COMMAND_TEMPLATE` default changed `/skin url {url}` → empty and relabeled "(legacy)".
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`_skin_command_templates`, rewritten `_apply_skin`, `_register_config`); tests `tests/test_minecraft_connector.py` (default tries both providers, list override, legacy single still works); docs `docs/rift_vessel.rst`, `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/guide.md`, `AGENTS.md` §5c.
**Status:** DONE 2026-08-11 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean; `tests/test_minecraft_connector.py` → 34 passed). Live re-verification pending a reconnect against the MC server.
**Notes:** No keyword/text matching — templates are static config strings, the server does the syntax matching. Best-effort: if `MINECRAFT_SKIN_FILE` is empty no command is sent, and a failed/ignored command never breaks the session. Separately, the served skin URL must be **reachable** from the MC server (publish the WebUI HTTP port on the host + set `MINECRAFT_SKIN_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` to a LAN/public IP; `synth.local` was unresolvable in the field report). Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build synth`.

### Rift Vessel — autonomous play split into volition (slow, LLM) + motorics (fast, reflex)  <!-- 2026-08-10 -->
**Why:** The original autonomy design used a single "decision beat" that conflated *deciding what to want* with *moving the body* — every movement cost a full LLM cognition turn. The user asked (IT) to split the motor part from the reflective/will part: "la volontà nasce dal synth, deve avere il suo prompt e le sue memorie; la motorietà invece deve essere veloce: niente prompt pesanti, reattiva." Will must emerge from Synth's personality (own prompt + memories); motion must be fast and reflexive.
**What:** Autonomy is now two independently-paced layers. (1) **Will beat — volition (slow, LLM).** Fires every `VESSEL_WILL_INTERVAL_SEC` s (default 45, falls back to legacy `VESSEL_BEAT_INTERVAL_SEC`, clamp `[10, 3600]`) via `_maybe_run_will_beat`; builds a will-framed prompt with `core/vessel_beat.py::build_will_prompt` and enqueues a normal Fast-Lane `vessel` perception where Synth authors/updates its free-text goal. This is where will + memories live. (2) **Motor tick — motorics (fast, no LLM).** Fires every `VESSEL_MOTOR_INTERVAL_SEC` s (default 3, clamp `[1, 60]`, gated by `VESSEL_MOTOR_ENABLED`, default True) via `_maybe_run_motor_tick`; calls `await connector.motor_step(goal)` **directly** — no prompt, no cognition turn, no diary. `motor_step` is a pure reflex over the structural affordance contract only (`{kind,target,verb,distance}`): nearest benign affordance → `mine`/`use` within `_MOTOR_REACH` (3.0 m), else `goto`, else `wander`; never reads the goal's free text.
**Location (edited):** `core/vessel_beat.py` (`build_will_prompt`, `build_decision_prompt` alias, `resolve_will_interval` + legacy fallback, `resolve_motor_interval`, `is_motor_enabled`, `resolve_beat_interval` alias), `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_base.py` (`async motor_step(goal)` base no-op hook returning `{"acted": False, "reason": "no_motorics"}`), `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`_MOTOR_REACH`, `motor_step` structural reflex), `interface/vessel_interface.py` (`_last_will_beat_at`/`_last_motor_tick_at`, `_maybe_run_will_beat`, `_maybe_run_motor_tick` in the scheduler loop). **Location (added):** `tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py`. **Docs:** `docs/rift_vessel.rst`, `AGENTS.md` §5c/§14.
**Config keys:** `VESSEL_WILL_INTERVAL_SEC` (45, aliases legacy `VESSEL_BEAT_INTERVAL_SEC`), `VESSEL_MOTOR_ENABLED` (True), `VESSEL_MOTOR_INTERVAL_SEC` (3, clamp `[1, 60]`).
**Status:** DONE 2026-08-10 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean on all edited files; scoped `tests/test_vessel_beat.py` + `tests/test_vessel_minecraft_motor.py` + `tests/test_vessel_minecraft_goals.py` → 40 passed).
**Notes:** No keyword/content matching — the motor reflex reads only the structural affordance contract, never the goal free text (the goal only gates whether to move). All three Vessel constraints preserved: the motor tick creates no cognition turn and no mid-session diary (Fast Lane only, single end-of-session diary). Fully fail-safe/guarded/lazy-imported — removing the beat module, disabling `VESSEL_AUTONOMY_ENABLED`/`VESSEL_MOTOR_ENABLED`, or a world whose connector has no `motor_step` (base no-op) never breaks the reactive Vessel. Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build synth`.

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — goals are now self-authored free text, not a hard-coded catalogue  <!-- 2026-08-09 -->
**Why:** The original Minecraft goal engine shipped a fixed catalogue of structured goal templates (`gather_wood`, `build_shelter`, `find_diamonds`, …) with prerequisites and inventory-count-based progression. A fixed quest menu makes **every** Synth play Minecraft identically — like a scripted bot, which is exactly what SyntH is not. Goals must emerge from each Synth's personality and wants, so different Synths behave differently.
**What:** Stripped the catalogue entirely. `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/goals.py` is now a thin **goal store** that only *persists* and *recalls* the free-text goals Synth writes for itself — no templates, no prerequisites, no item-count progression. During a decision beat Synth reads its situation (`observe`/inventory/world state) and authors a goal in its own words via `vessel_minecraft_set_goal` (required free-text `description` + optional `note`). `vessel_minecraft_goals` returns `current_goal` + `recent_goals`; `vessel_minecraft_update_goal` records Synth's own progress `note` or marks the goal `done`/`abandoned`. Setting a new goal auto-abandons the previous active one. Progress is judged by Synth from what it perceives, never by an item counter.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/goals.py` (rewritten — `set_goal(description, session_id, note)`, `get_active_goal`, `list_recent_goals`, `update_active_goal(note, status)`; removed `GOAL_CATALOGUE`/`GoalTemplate`/`get_template`/`_match_count`/`evaluate_progress`/`available_goal_types`/`refresh_active_goal_progress`), `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`_GOAL_VERBS` = `set_goal`/`goals`/`update_goal`, `_act_goal` rewritten, `_resolve_goals`, `WorldState.extra` now exposes `current_goal`/`recent_goals`, action schemas), `core/vessel_beat.py` (`_fmt_goal` free-text, prompt surfaces "Your current goal" + "Things you set out to do before" + a self-direction cue to author your own goal), `init-db.sql` (`minecraft_goals` DDL → `description TEXT NOT NULL`, `note TEXT`, `status`, `created_at`/`updated_at`), tests `tests/test_vessel_minecraft_goals.py` (rewritten — free-text store + asserts the catalogue symbols are gone), `tests/test_vessel_beat.py` (free-text goal fixture + assertions). **Docs:** `docs/rift_vessel.rst`, `AGENTS.md` §5c/§13.
**Status:** DONE 2026-08-09 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean on `goals.py` + `vessel_beat.py`; scoped `tests/test_vessel_minecraft_goals.py` + `tests/test_vessel_beat.py` → 21 passed).
**Notes:** No keyword/content matching anywhere — goals are opaque free text authored by cognition, the store never inspects them. Fully fail-safe/guarded (DB errors degrade to no-goal/empty). All three Vessel constraints preserved. Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build synth`.

### Rift Vessel — autonomous play (decision beat + generic self-awareness + Minecraft goal engine)  <!-- 2026-08-08 -->
**What:** Synth can now **play on its own** in a Vessel world — wander, look around, set and pursue its own goals, gather, build, interact — instead of only reacting to chat. The design keeps a clean split: the **generic core** (self-awareness + the decision loop) lives in the Vessel/core, while **what to play** (Minecraft-specific goals, crafting, progression) lives in the connector.
**Mechanism (Grillo-style, keyword-free):** when `VESSEL_AUTONOMY_ENABLED` is on, the interface scheduler (`interface/vessel_interface.py`, 10 s tick) fires a **decision beat** every `VESSEL_BEAT_INTERVAL_SEC` s (default 45, clamped `[10, 3600]`) via `_maybe_run_decision_beat` while a session is active. The beat reads the live connector's `WorldState`, builds a **structural** situational prompt with `core/vessel_beat.py::build_decision_prompt` (position/health/time/entities/blocks/inventory/affordances/current+available goals straight from the contract — no text matching), and enqueues it as a **normal** `vessel` message so `build_context` applies world-scoped context and the core runs **one ordinary Fast-Lane cognition turn** in which Synth picks one or two `vessel_<world>_…` actions. No new lane, no Drones, no mid-session diary.
**Generic self-awareness:** new `observe` **core** verb (`plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py::_observe_surroundings`) reads the current `WorldState` and reports nearby affordances/entities/blocks in character. **Affordances** follow a generic structural contract `{kind, target, verb, distance}` built by the connector from the raw snapshot — world-agnostic, never keyword matching.
**Minecraft goal engine (world-specific):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/goals.py` — structured goal templates (`gather_wood`, `build_shelter`, `find_diamonds`, …) with prerequisites and **inventory-count-based** progression (matching the bot's inventory against per-goal item *families* — MC domain data, not routing keywords), persisted in the new `minecraft_goals` table so a goal survives across beats within a session. The connector exposes extra verbs via `get_world_actions()` — bridge-backed `goto`/`scan`/`mine`/`place`/`inventory`/`wander` plus goal-engine `set_goal`/`goals` — and enriches `WorldState.extra` with `current_goal`/`available_goals` refreshed against the live inventory. Synth chooses/switches goals itself (`vessel_minecraft_set_goal`) during a beat.
**Location (added):** `core/vessel_beat.py` (pure prompt builder + autonomy gating + interval clamp), `tests/test_vessel_beat.py`, `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/goals.py`, `tests/test_minecraft_goals.py`. **Location (edited):** `interface/vessel_interface.py` (10 s scheduler tick, `_maybe_run_decision_beat`, `_read_active_world_state`, `_decision_interface_path`, `_enqueue_perception`), `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` (`observe` verb + `_observe_surroundings`, `VESSEL_AUTONOMY_ENABLED`/`VESSEL_BEAT_INTERVAL_SEC` config), `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (`get_world_actions`, `WorldState.extra` enrichment, `_build_affordances`, goal-table init on connect), `interface_dev/minecraft_bridge_minimal.js` (`worldSnapshot` + `scan`/`goto`/`mine`/`place`/`inventory`/`wander`), `interface/minecraft_provisioner.py` (`minecraft-data` bridge dep), `init-db.sql` (`minecraft_goals` table). **Docs:** `docs/rift_vessel.rst`, `AGENTS.md` §5c/§13/§14.
**Config keys:** `VESSEL_AUTONOMY_ENABLED` (False — enable autonomous play), `VESSEL_BEAT_INTERVAL_SEC` (45, clamped `[10, 3600]`).
**Status:** DONE 2026-08-08 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean on edited files; scoped `tests/test_vessel_beat.py` + `test_minecraft_goals.py` + Vessel suite → 49 passed; `node --check` clean on the bridge). Live in-world verification pending a reconnect against the MC server + a container rebuild.
**Notes:** All three Vessel constraints preserved — the beat is just another perception-shaped message (Fast Lane only, no Agent Lane/Drones, single end-of-session diary). No keyword/text matching anywhere: affordances are a structural `{kind,target,verb,distance}` contract and goal progress is an inventory item-family count (domain data). Fully fail-safe/guarded/lazy-imported — removing `core/vessel_beat.py`, the goal engine, or disabling `VESSEL_AUTONOMY_ENABLED` never breaks the reactive Vessel. The Node bridge now needs `minecraft-data` (the provisioner npm-installs it). Backend Python + Node change → `docker compose up -d --build synth`.

### Rift Vessel — Synth's own in-world actions were missing from the Activities tab  <!-- 2026-08-07 -->
**Symptom:** The WebUI Vessel Activities (🌀) tab showed only **inbound** events (Session Start, Spawn, Proximity, chat received from other players) but never Synth's **own** in-world responses/actions. The user reported (IT) "in activity non vedo le risposte del synth" — Synth would `say`/`move`/`follow`/etc. in the world, yet nothing appeared in the log.
**Root cause:** `vessel_activity_log` was written only for `session_start`, `session_end`, and inbound `on_world_event` (all in `interface/vessel_interface.py`). The **outbound** dispatch path `vessel_plugin.act()` → `connector.act()` never wrote an activity row, so successful embodiment verbs were invisible in the audit log.
**Fix:** (1) `interface/vessel_interface.py`: new `_resolve_session_for_environment(environment)` (maps an environment to its live `session_id`/`interface_path` from `self._sessions`) and a new guarded `async log_outbound_action(environment, action, summary, metadata=None)` that buffers the lived experience (audit-only, never touches cognition) and inserts an `action_<verb>` row via the single write sink `_log_activity`. (2) `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py`: `act()` now calls a new guarded `_log_outbound_action(name, action, payload)` after a **successful** result; the summary is built by `_describe_outbound_action(action, payload)` — **structural only** (surfaces non-empty payload fields as `key=value`, truncates long values, no keyword/content matching). `event_type` uses an **underscore** (`action_say`) so the History JS (`res/synth_webui/js/history.js::renderVesselSession`) renders it as "Action Say".
**Location (edited):** `interface/vessel_interface.py` (`_resolve_session_for_environment`, `log_outbound_action`), `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` (`act()` hook, `_log_outbound_action`, `_describe_outbound_action`), tests `tests/test_vessel_action_namespacing.py` (+3: success logs an `action_<verb>` row, failed action is not logged, structural summary).
**Status:** DONE 2026-08-07 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean on both files + the test; scoped `tests/test_vessel_action_namespacing.py` → 15 passed, `test_vessel_realtime.py` + `test_vessel_session_manager.py` → all green). Live re-verification pending a reconnect.
**Notes:** No keyword/text matching — the summary just enumerates payload fields. Fully fail-safe: a logging failure is caught and logged as a warning, never breaking the in-world action; only **successful** actions are logged. Constraint 3 preserved — the activity log stays audit-only and does not feed cognition. Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build synth`.

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — skin never visible because the auto-derived public base URL defaulted to loopback  <!-- 2026-07-25 -->
**Symptom:** Skin upload succeeded and the connector logged `skin command sent: /skin url http://127.0.0.1:8080/api/config/MINECRAFT_SKIN_FILE/file`, but the skin never appeared on a remote MC server. The `127.0.0.1` base URL is unreachable from any server not running on the SyntH host itself, so the skin plugin could never fetch the texture.
**Root cause:** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py::_skin_public_base_url()` fell back to `127.0.0.1` (or the raw `SYNTH_WEBUI_HOST`, often a loopback/`0.0.0.0`) when `MINECRAFT_SKIN_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` was empty. A loopback host in the served skin URL cannot be opened by a remote/LAN Minecraft server.
**Fix:** New module-level helper `_detect_lan_ip()` discovers the machine's primary outbound IP via a route-only UDP socket (no packet sent, works offline, non-blocking with a 0.2 s timeout; `socket.gethostbyname` fallback; returns `None` for loopback/undetectable). `_skin_public_base_url()` now substitutes that LAN IP whenever the derived WebUI host is a loopback (`127.0.0.1`/`localhost`/`0.0.0.0`/`::1`), falling back to `127.0.0.1` only if no LAN IP is detectable. An explicit `MINECRAFT_SKIN_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` still wins for VPN/public/reverse-proxy setups.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (added `import socket`, `_detect_lan_ip()`, updated `_skin_public_base_url()`); tests `tests/test_minecraft_connector.py` (3 new); docs `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/guide.md`, `docs/rift_vessel.rst`, `AGENTS.md` §5c.
**Status:** DONE 2026-07-25 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean; `tests/test_minecraft_connector.py` → 32 passed). Live re-verification pending a reconnect against the remote server.
**Notes:** No keyword/text matching — the loopback→LAN substitution is driven purely by IP-class checks. Still best-effort: a server on a *different* network (VPN-only peer, public host, other subnet) or without a server-side skin plugin (SkinsRestorer) still needs `MINECRAFT_SKIN_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` set explicitly. Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build synth`.

### Rift Vessel — bot inert (no chat reply + immobile) because the cached actions block was never rebuilt on connect  <!-- 2026-08-06 -->
**Symptom:** Even with the bot connected (`/health` → `connected:true`, health 20, valid position), Synth still "didn't read chat and stood still". Chat perceptions **did** reach cognition, but the LLM returned only `create_personal_diary_entry`, literally stating no `vessel_say`/messaging action was available to reply. A cortex-log grep for `vessel_minecraft_*`/`vessel_say` returned **empty** — the gameplay verbs were never in any prompt.
**Root cause:** `core/core_initializer.py` builds `self.actions_block` (the `{available_actions, static_context}` cache read by every prompt-build path — `prompt_engine`, `cortex_bridge`, `transport_layer`, `message_chain`, `action_parser`, `action_safety`) **once at startup**, *before* the Vessel connects (or reattaches). The Vessel's `get_supported_actions()` is connection-driven — it only exposes the namespaced gameplay verbs (`vessel_<world>_say/move/look/...`) once a world is connected — but nothing rebuilt the cache after connect, so the prompt engine kept serving the stale disconnected snapshot (`vessel_connect` only). Result: no `vessel_say` → no chat reply, no `vessel_move` → immobility.
**Fix:** (1) `core/core_initializer.py`: new public, fail-safe, loop-aware `schedule_actions_block_refresh(reason="")` that wraps the rebuild — skips during initial init, waits out an in-flight build, detects a running loop (`create_task` vs `asyncio.run`), and never raises. This is the single reusable entry point to refresh the cache whenever the exposed action set changes. (2) `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py`: new guarded static helper `_refresh_actions_block(reason)` (lazy-imports `core_initializer`, never raises) called after a successful connect (both the fresh-connect and the already-connected/reattach branches → `vessel_connect:<world>`) and after a successful disconnect (`vessel_disconnect:<world>`), so the gameplay verbs are exposed/hidden live without a restart.
**Location (edited):** `core/core_initializer.py` (new `schedule_actions_block_refresh`), `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` (new `_refresh_actions_block` + 3 call sites in `connect_world`/`disconnect_world`).
**Status:** DONE 2026-08-06 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean on `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py`; scoped `tests/test_vessel_action_namespacing.py` + `test_vessel_realtime.py` → 18 passed; container rebuilt). Live re-verification of verb exposure is pending the remote MC server `100.113.224.83:44383` coming back online — at rebuild it was returning `ECONNREFUSED`, so reattach failed cleanly (stale session closed) with no world connected; the refresh path only fires on a **successful** connect.
**Notes:** No keyword/text matching — the refresh is driven purely by the connect/disconnect lifecycle events, not by message content. Fully fail-safe: removing the Vessel plugin can never break the core (helper is guarded + lazy-imported; the core method never raises). The same `schedule_actions_block_refresh()` is the intended hook for future callers (config-var changes, plugin enable/disable) so the cache stays coherent everywhere. Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build synth`.

### Blocklist — `invalid input for query argument $1: 'minecraft'` on non-numeric interface ids  <!-- 2026-08-06 -->
**Symptom:** Log spam `ERROR blocklist Failed to check if user minecraft is blocked: invalid input for query argument $1: 'minecraft' ('str' object cannot be interpreted as an integer)`. Vessel perceptions carry `from_user.id = <environment>` (e.g. the string `"minecraft"`), which was bound against the `BIGINT` `user_id` column.
**Root cause:** `plugins/blocklist/blocklist.py::is_user_blocked()` passed the raw id straight into `WHERE user_id = %s` (a `BIGINT` column). Non-numeric ids — Vessel environment names and WebUI UUIDs (the identical, previously-known-unfixed 2026-07-08 UUID case) — cannot be coerced by the driver and raise. Only error spam; it was **not** the cause of the bot inertia.
**Fix:** `is_user_blocked(user_id: int | str)` now coerces the id with `int(user_id)` up front and, on `TypeError`/`ValueError`, returns `False` (fail-open — a non-numeric id can't be in a numeric blocklist), before touching the DB. Error log uses the coerced value.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/blocklist/blocklist.py`.
**Status:** DONE 2026-08-06 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean; container rebuilt). Also resolves the 2026-07-08 WebUI-UUID variant of this bug.
**Notes:** No keyword/text matching — pure numeric coercion. Fail-open is correct here: the blocklist is keyed by numeric user id, so a non-numeric caller is by definition not on it.

### Rift Vessel — bot still inert after a long-downtime restart: reattach abandoned stale-but-in-world sessions  <!-- 2026-08-05 -->
**Symptom:** Even after the 2026-08-04 suspend/pathfinder fixes and a rebuild, the Minecraft bot "did nothing" again. Live diagnosis: `GET /health` on the bridge returned `{"ok":true,"connected":false,"last_error":null}` — the bot was simply **not in-world**. With no connector connected, `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py::_connected_world()` returns `None`, so `get_supported_actions()` exposes **only** `vessel_connect` and hides the gameplay verbs (`vessel_minecraft_follow/move/say`). The LLM therefore had no verb to follow with and just wrote a diary entry. A manual `POST /connect` to the bridge spawned the bot instantly (`{"ok":true,"detail":"spawned"}` → `connected:true`), proving the connect path and MC version handling were fine — the only problem was that the bot was left disconnected.
**Root cause:** `interface/vessel_interface.py::_reattach_active_sessions()` filtered the reattach query by the inactivity cooldown (`last_event_at >= now - VESSEL_SESSION_COOLDOWN_SEC`, default 1 h). When the container was down longer than the cooldown (e.g. session at 14:04, boot at 23:08), every `active` session was **older than the cutoff** and silently skipped — so a bot that may still be physically logged into the world was never reattached, leaving an `active` DB row with no connector draining its perception stream. The cooldown governs when an *idle* session is **closed**, not whether to reattach; conflating the two abandoned recoverable sessions.
**Fix:** `interface/vessel_interface.py::_reattach_active_sessions()` now reattaches **every** `active` session regardless of the cooldown (dropped the `last_event_at >= cutoff` filter and the now-unused `datetime`/`timedelta` import). `connect_world` remains reattach-safe/idempotent, so an already-connected bridge is re-embodied and a fresh boot re-runs `connect()`. New guarded helper `_close_stale_sessions(environment)` ends any `active` row that could **not** be reattached (bridge dead / server unreachable) via `end_session(reason="reattach_failed")`, so a phantom `active` session can never keep gameplay verbs hidden while the DB claims Synth is embodied.
**Location (edited):** `interface/vessel_interface.py` (reattach no longer cooldown-gated + new `_close_stale_sessions`).
**Status:** DONE 2026-08-05 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean on `interface/vessel_interface.py`; scoped `tests/test_vessel_session_manager.py` + `test_vessel_realtime.py` + `test_vessel_commands.py` → 15 passed; container rebuild pending).
**Notes:** No keyword/text matching. Backend-agnostic query (plain `WHERE status='active'`, no `INTERVAL`). The cooldown still ends genuinely-idle sessions via `close_expired_sessions` (unchanged). Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build synth`.

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — bot immobile/unresponsive: restart ended sessions + pathfinder never installed  <!-- 2026-08-04 -->
**Symptom:** After a container restart Synth's Minecraft bot was fully inert — it didn't reply in chat, wouldn't `follow`, and just stood there getting hit by mobs. Two independent causes.
**Root cause #1 (total unresponsiveness):** On a clean container restart (Docker `SIGTERM` → `interface/vessel_interface.py::stop()`), every active session was ended with `end_session(reason="shutdown")` → the `vessel_sessions` row was marked `status='ended'` **and the experience buffer was prematurely flushed to a diary entry**. At the next boot `_reattach_active_sessions()` only re-embodies rows with `status='active'`, so nothing was reattached: no world perception ever re-entered the message chain and Synth stayed inert while the old Mineflayer socket lingered as an orphaned ghost until the server timed it out. **A restart is not a logout.**
**Root cause #2 (won't move/follow even when embodied):** `mineflayer-pathfinder` (which powers the `follow`/`move` navigation verbs) was **never installed**: `interface/minecraft_provisioner.py` had `_BRIDGE_NPM_DEPS = ["mineflayer"]` and its idempotency short-circuit only checked `node_modules/mineflayer`, so a bridge that already had mineflayer never got pathfinder added. The bridge already degrades gracefully (`follow` returns `follow unavailable (pathfinder not loaded)`), so the bot connected but could never navigate.
**Fix:** (1) `core/vessel_session_manager.py`: new `suspend_session(session_id)` — refreshes `last_event_at` and keeps the row `status='active'` (so reattach finds it) **without** flushing a diary entry, and drops the in-memory bookkeeping for the destroyed connector; fully guarded. (2) `interface/vessel_interface.py::stop()`: now calls `manager.suspend_session(...)` per session instead of `end_session(reason="shutdown")`, so a restart preserves the lived experience and reattaches at boot — the real end-of-session diary flush still happens on explicit logout or the inactivity cooldown (`close_expired_sessions`), unchanged. (3) `interface/minecraft_provisioner.py`: added `"mineflayer-pathfinder"` to `_BRIDGE_NPM_DEPS`; the install idempotency check and `status().installed` now require **every** dep in `_BRIDGE_NPM_DEPS` to be present (so older mineflayer-only installs re-run `npm install` to add the missing pathfinder on the next connect).
**Location (edited):** `core/vessel_session_manager.py`, `interface/vessel_interface.py`, `interface/minecraft_provisioner.py`, `tests/test_vessel_session_manager.py` (new `test_suspend_session_keeps_active_and_skips_diary`).
**Status:** DONE 2026-08-04 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean on the 3 backend Python files; scoped `tests/test_vessel_session_manager.py` → 2 passed, full vessel suite → 34 passed; container rebuilt). First boot after this rebuild reattaches nothing (the pre-fix shutdown had already ended the sessions) — the fix applies from the next restart onward.
**Notes:** No keyword/text matching. `suspend_session` is backend-agnostic (plain `UPDATE ... WHERE status='active'`, no `INTERVAL`). Pathfinder installs on the next `vessel_connect` (bridge provisioning is on-demand); `node`/`npm` are baked into the Docker image so the install works out of the box. Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build synth`.

### Rift Vessel — world-agnostic `respawn` core verb (come back to life after death)  <!-- 2026-08-03 -->
**Motivation:** Synth died in the Minecraft world and had no way to come back — there was no embodiment verb to respawn. Most game worlds support a respawn-on-death concept, so it belongs in the world-agnostic Vessel **core set** (shared by every world), not as a Minecraft-only extra.
**Fix (end-to-end, following the existing core-verb pattern):** (1) `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py`: added a `respawn` entry to `_ACTION_VERBS` (no required/optional fields, `security_level: "low"`, **no** `external_effects` → Fast Lane only) whose description explicitly states it no-ops in worlds without a death/respawn concept or when already alive — keeping it portable; and added `"respawn"` to the gameplay-verb dispatch tuple in `handle_custom_action` so it routes to `connector.act("respawn", …)` like the other core verbs. It is auto-exposed namespaced `vessel_<world>_respawn` only while connected. (2) `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py`: added `"respawn"` to the connector's `get_world_state` `possible_actions` list; the connector's generic `act()` already POSTs the verb verbatim to the bridge, so no other connector change was needed. (3) `interface_dev/minecraft_bridge_minimal.js`: new `case 'respawn'` in `runAction` that guards on `bot.isAlive` (returns `already alive` when not dead) and otherwise calls Mineflayer `bot.respawn()`, returning `{ok:true, detail:'respawned', data:{position}}`; also enriched the existing `death` perception event summary to `"Died in the world. Respawn to come back to life."` with `data:{dead:true}` so Synth's cognition knows the state and the available recovery (no keyword logic — it's descriptive perception text).
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py`, `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py`, `interface_dev/minecraft_bridge_minimal.js`, `tests/test_minecraft_connector.py` (assert `respawn` in `possible_actions`), `tests/test_vessel_action_namespacing.py` (respawn exposed + dispatched).
**Status:** DONE 2026-08-03 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean on the 2 backend Python files; scoped `tests/test_minecraft_connector.py` + `tests/test_vessel_action_namespacing.py` → 41 passed; container rebuilt).
**Notes:** No keyword/text matching — `respawn` is a plain verb dispatched verbatim through `connector.act` to the bridge, which maps it to `bot.respawn()`. `bot.respawn()` is effective only when the bot is dead; the bridge guards on `bot.isAlive` to avoid a no-op/throw when already alive. The bridge JS is re-copied by the provisioner on each connect (`shutil.copyfile`), so a fresh `vessel_connect` applies it. Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build synth`.

### Rift Vessel — surface the real connection-failure reason to Synth  <!-- 2026-08-02 -->
**Symptom:** When entering a world failed (version mismatch like `No data available for version 26.2`, a kick, a timeout, a missing `mineflayer` module, or the bridge not coming up), Synth could only report a generic `connect_failed`. The concrete reason lived **only** in `synth.log` / the bridge log, so Synth could not tell the requester *why* it couldn't join.
**Root cause (two layers):** (1) The connector's `connect()` returns a bare `bool`, and `connect_world()` turned any failure into a hardcoded `VesselActionResult(detail="connect_failed")` — discarding all diagnostics (it even swallowed a raised exception into a log line). (2) **Deeper:** the Node bridge's `connectBot` returned `{ok:true,detail:'connecting'}` *optimistically* the moment `mineflayer.createBot` was called, before the async spawn — so terminal failures arriving later on `bot.on('error')`/`'kicked'`/`'end'` were only `log()`'d and never returned to the Python side.
**Fix (end-to-end propagation):** (a) `interface_dev/minecraft_bridge_minimal.js`: `connectBot` now returns a **Promise** that resolves only on the real terminal outcome — `spawn` → `{ok:true}`, `error`/`kicked`/`end` → `{ok:false, detail:<reason>}`, plus a 20 s watchdog → `{ok:false, detail:"timed out ... waiting to enter the world"}`. A module-level `lastError` records the reason and is also exposed in `/health`; `/connect` `await`s the promise and returns `result.ok ? 200 : 500`. (b) `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py`: new `self.last_error` set at every failure branch of `connect()` (bridge health failure, missing mineflayer, `/connect` failure — each with the resolved server address), cleared at the start of each attempt. (c) `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_base.py`: `VesselConnectorBase` now declares a class-level `last_error: str | None = None` so the core can read it generically on **any** connector; the `connect()` docstring documents the contract. (d) `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py::connect_world()`: on failure it now builds `detail = f"Could not enter '{name}': {connector.last_error or <exception message>}"` (falling back to `connect_failed`) and puts the reason in `data["reason"]` too. This flows straight through `handle_custom_action` (`{"status":"error","message":result.detail,...}`) to Synth.
**Location (edited):** `interface_dev/minecraft_bridge_minimal.js`, `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py`, `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_base.py`, `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py`, `tests/test_minecraft_connector.py` (3 new tests: `last_error` set on bridge `/connect` failure incl. version message + server address, on health failure, on missing mineflayer).
**Status:** DONE 2026-08-02 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean on the 3 backend Python files; scoped `tests/test_minecraft_connector.py` → 28 passed; container rebuilt).
**Notes:** No keyword/text matching — the reason is a plain string carried verbatim from the bridge/connector to `VesselActionResult.detail`. Backward compatible with the `connect() -> bool` contract (the reason travels out-of-band via `last_error`). The bridge JS is re-copied by the provisioner on each connect (`shutil.copyfile`), so a fresh connect applies it. Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build synth`.

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — configurable protocol version to bypass `No data available for version X`  <!-- 2026-08-01 -->
**Symptom:** After the loopback/reachability fix, the Mineflayer bot *reaches* the game server (no more `ECONNREFUSED`) but still fails to spawn. `/opt/minecraft_bridge/bridge.log` shows `Error: No data available for version 26.2` → `bot error: No data available for version 26.2` → `connection ended`. The server announces a protocol version that the bundled `minecraft-data` (3.111.0) doesn't recognise, so Mineflayer's auto-detection aborts.
**Root cause:** The bridge always let Mineflayer auto-detect the server version and never accepted an explicit version, so any server advertising a snapshot/new/proxy protocol unknown to the bundled data was unjoinable.
**Fix (general — benefits any user with a non-auto-detectable server):** threaded a configurable protocol version end-to-end. (1) `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py`: new advanced config key `MINECRAFT_SERVER_VERSION` (default `""` = auto-detect); `_resolve_server_target()` now resolves the version from a per-connect `settings["version"]` override else the config key and adds it to the `/connect` body **only when non-empty** (backward compatible — empty preserves auto-detection). (2) `interface/minecraft_provisioner.py::_bridge_env()`: seeds `MC_VERSION` from `MINECRAFT_SERVER_VERSION`. (3) `interface_dev/minecraft_bridge_minimal.js`: `CFG.version` reads `MC_VERSION`; `connectBot(overrides)` honours a per-connect `opts.version` (falling back to `CFG.version`) and passes `version` into `mineflayer.createBot` only when truthy; startup/connect log now states the pinned version or "auto version".
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py`, `interface/minecraft_provisioner.py`, `interface_dev/minecraft_bridge_minimal.js`, `tests/test_minecraft_connector.py` (3 new tests: no version by default, version from override, version from config), `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/guide.md` (new Troubleshooting section + config-table row).
**Status:** DONE 2026-08-01 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean on the 2 backend Python files; scoped `tests/test_minecraft_connector.py` → 25 passed).
**Notes:** No keyword/text matching — the version is a plain config/override string passed straight to Mineflayer. Empty default = full backward compatibility. If a server is genuinely newer than the bridge's bundled data, the user may additionally need to bump `mineflayer`/`minecraft-data` in the bridge `package.json` (documented in the guide). The bridge JS is a Node subprocess asset re-copied by the provisioner on each `_ensure_bridge_running` (`shutil.copyfile`), so a fresh connect applies it. Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build synth`.

### Rift Vessel (Minecraft) — bot connected but invisible in-world: general container→host loopback fix  <!-- 2026-08-01 -->
**Symptom:** Synth "connects" to Minecraft (connector + local Mineflayer bridge HTTP link both report OK) but the bot never spawns in-world. Bridge log shows `Error: connect ECONNREFUSED <host>:<port>` — the Mineflayer bot cannot reach the Minecraft *game server*.
**Root cause:** The common one-click setup is a world hosted ("Open to LAN" / local server) on the **same machine** that runs the SyntH container, with the server address left at the natural `127.0.0.1`/`localhost`. Inside a container that loopback points at the container itself, not the Docker host where the world actually listens, so the bot silently fails to join. On a **custom** Docker bridge network (`synth_network`) the stable name `host.docker.internal` is also not resolved by default.
**Fix (general, network-agnostic — benefits every user, not tied to any specific LAN/VPN):** (1) `docker-compose.yml`: added `extra_hosts: ["host.docker.internal:host-gateway"]` to the `synth` service so the container can reach services on the Docker host by that stable name. (2) `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py`: `_resolve_server_target()` now auto-remaps a loopback server host (`127.0.0.1`/`localhost`/`::1`/`0.0.0.0`) to `host.docker.internal` **only when running inside a container** (new module helper `_is_in_container()`, mirroring `plugins/agent_plugin/agent_plugin.py`). A real LAN/remote/VPN host is always left untouched; on a bare-metal (non-container) host the loopback is preserved as-is. This makes the "just play on my machine" case work with no manual Docker networking.
**Location (edited):** `docker-compose.yml`, `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py`, `tests/test_minecraft_connector.py` (3 new tests: in-container remap of all loopback aliases, no-remap on host, no-remap for non-loopback in container).
**Status:** DONE 2026-08-01 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean on `minecraft.py`; scoped `tests/test_minecraft_connector.py` → 22 passed).
**Notes:** Pure host-string normalization — **not** keyword/text matching on user messages (compliant with the no-keyword-logic rule). A truly unreachable server on a *different, LAN-isolated* machine still needs a reachable address (e.g. a VPN/Tailscale IP) — no code can reach an unroutable host — but the far more common same-machine scenario now works out of the box. Compose-only change → `docker compose up -d synth`; backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build`.

### Rift Vessel — `vessel_connect` was hidden from the prompt on every non-vessel interface  <!-- 2026-07-31 -->
**Symptom:** `vessel_connect` was correctly registered (present in the static `core_initializer.actions_block` snapshot and returned by `VesselPlugin.get_supported_actions()`), yet it never appeared in the LLM prompt on Telegram/WebUI/ollama_serve/Grillo — so Synth could never decide to enter a world.
**Root cause:** `interface/vessel_interface.py::get_supported_actions()` **delegated** to `vessel_plugin.get_supported_actions()`, re-declaring the plugin's actions from the interface. The core merge (`core_initializer._build_actions_block`) then appended the interface name (`vessel`, from `INTERFACE_NAME`) to each action's `source`, producing `source="vessel_plugin, vessel"`. `core/prompt_engine._derive_default_prompt_action_types()` intersects an action's source tokens with the set of registered interface names and, when the intersection is non-empty and does **not** contain the *current* interface, drops the action as "interface-scoped". Since `vessel` is a registered interface but Synth never builds a prompt *from* the vessel interface (it enters worlds from ordinary chat), the action was filtered out everywhere.
**Fix:** `interface/vessel_interface.py::get_supported_actions()` now returns `{}` (with a comment explaining why re-declaration is forbidden). Embodiment actions live **only** on `vessel_plugin` (always loaded); the interface is a pure I/O channel for world-perception events. The `source` is now clean (`vessel_plugin`), so the prompt-scope derivation no longer treats `vessel_connect` as interface-scoped.
**Location (edited):** `interface/vessel_interface.py`.
**Status:** DONE 2026-07-31 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean; scoped `tests/test_vessel_action_namespacing.py` → 12 passed; verified at runtime: `[json_prompt] Derived default prompt action scope: 43/61 ... allowlist [..., 'vessel_connect']` on `interface=ollama_serve` after `docker compose up -d --build`).
**Notes:** General trap — an **interface must never re-declare a plugin's actions** via `get_supported_actions()`; doing so poisons the action `source` with the interface name and makes `_derive_default_prompt_action_types` hide the action on all other interfaces. Interfaces should only declare actions they *own*. Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build`.

### Rift Vessel — connection-driven action exposure + orange coherence LED  <!-- 2026-07-30 -->
**Change (feat + docs):** Replaces the always-on per-world namespacing with a **connection-driven** action set, and adds a WebUI coherence LED. Answers "how does Synth enter a world, and which verbs does it see when?". The Vessel now exposes actions based on the **live connection state** (read purely from `get_supported_actions()`, so they change on the next prompt with no restart): **(a) Disconnected** → a single `vessel_connect` action whose required `game` field is an **enum of every enabled world** (`_enabled_worlds()`), plus optional `host`/`port` overrides — no gameplay verbs visible. **(b) Connected to world W** → the world-agnostic core set (minus `connect`) plus W's `get_world_actions()` extras appear namespaced `vessel_<W>_<verb>` alongside `vessel_disconnect`, and `vessel_connect` disappears. **(c) Logout / idle cooldown** (`VESSEL_SESSION_COOLDOWN_SEC`) → session closes, `has_active_session()` goes false, and on the next prompt the gameplay verbs vanish and `vessel_connect` returns — free, driven by `close_expired_sessions`. **(d) No enabled worlds** → empty set. **Orange coherence LED:** a world sub-plugin (e.g. `minecraft_vessel`) can be enabled while the core `vessel_plugin` is disabled — a state in which it can never connect. The classic WebUI Plugins tab now shows an **orange** status dot on any `Vessels`-category world sub-plugin whose LED would otherwise be green when `vessel_plugin` is not loaded (tooltip explains why); enabling the core plugin restores green/grey.
**How:** `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py`: new `_connected_world()` (structural probe — `vessel_session_manager.has_active_session()` + first cached connector with `is_connected` True; fail-safe, never loads fresh/raises), `_world_enabled(world)` / `_enabled_worlds()` (worlds whose `<world>_vessel` sub-plugin is on, via `PLUGIN_ENABLED__<world>_vessel`; fail-safe), `_world_extra_verbs_for(world)` (per-world connector extras, skipping core collisions). `get_supported_actions()` rewritten: disconnected → single `vessel_connect` with a dynamic description listing the enabled games and `game_choices`/`required_fields: ["game"]` + optional host/port; connected → per-world prefix over both tiers + `vessel_disconnect`, `vessel_connect` hidden. `_parse_action_verb()` matches against the core set + the connected world's extras. `handle_custom_action()` reads the `game` field on connect (falling back to a legacy namespaced form), builds host/port `overrides`, and calls `connect_world(connector_name=game, overrides=...)`; `connect_world()` rejects a world not in `_enabled_worlds()` (`world_unavailable`). `is_enabled()` = connected OR any enabled world. `core/webui.py`: `vessel_core_enabled` gate flips a green LED to orange for `Vessels`-category world sub-plugins when `vessel_plugin` is not in `PLUGIN_REGISTRY`.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py`, `core/webui.py`, `docs/rift_vessel.rst`, `AGENTS.md` §5c, `plugins/rift_vessel/guide.md`, `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/guide.md`, `tests/test_vessel_action_namespacing.py` (rewritten for the connection-driven model, 12 tests).
**Status:** DONE 2026-07-30 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean on `vessel_plugin.py` + `minecraft.py` + the test — `core/webui.py` not ty-checked per repo rule; scoped `tests/test_vessel_action_namespacing.py` → 12 passed; regression `tests/test_vessel_registry.py tests/test_vessel_commands.py tests/test_vessel_session_manager.py tests/test_vessel_realtime.py` → 22 passed).
**Notes:** No keyword/regex logic — the connection probe is structural (`has_active_session()` + `is_connected`) and the `game` field is an enum *value*, never text matched. Vessel actions stay off the Agent Lane (no `external_effects`, `security_level: "low"`), auto-exposed as MCP tools. The cooldown/logout auto-hide is free (scheduler-driven). Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build`; `core/webui.py`/templates hard-refresh.

### Rift Vessel — core verbs: say audio flag, attack out of use, follow/unfollow  <!-- 2026-07-29 -->
**Change (feat + docs):** Three refinements to the world-agnostic **core set** of embodiment verbs, per design review. (1) `say` gains an optional `audio` flag mirroring the interface audio-message flag (e.g. `audio_telegram_bot`) — in worlds with no voice channel it silently falls back to plain text. (2) `attack` is now a **dedicated hostile verb** of its own, removed from `use`'s description (`use` is documented as benign interaction "for combat use `attack`"). (3) `follow`/`unfollow` are added to the core set as **universal** entity-following verbs — the player and NPCs are followable in nearly every game world; on the edge case where there is nothing to follow the command fails cleanly at runtime. All three stay `security_level: "low"` with **no** `external_effects` (Fast Lane, never Agent Lane/Drones), and are auto-exposed as MCP tools (`synth_vessel_<world>_attack`, …).
**How:** (1) `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py::_ACTION_VERBS` — `say` gets `optional_fields: ["audio"]`; `use` description cleaned; new `attack` (`optional_fields: ["target"]`), `follow` (`optional_fields: ["target"]`), `unfollow` (no fields). `handle_custom_action()` extends the core dispatch tuple to include `attack`/`follow`/`unfollow` (all forwarded to `connector.act(verb, payload)`). (2) `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py::get_world_state()` adds the three verbs to `possible_actions`. (3) `interface_dev/minecraft_bridge_minimal.js`: optional `mineflayer-pathfinder` require (deferred, sets `pathfinder = null` on failure) + `b.loadPlugin(pathfinder.pathfinder)` on login; new `resolveTargetEntity(target)` helper (match player/entity by name else nearest); new `runAction` cases — `attack` → `bot.attack(entity)`, `follow` → `bot.pathfinder.setGoal(new GoalFollow(entity, range), true)` (fails gracefully when pathfinder is not loaded or there is nothing to follow), `unfollow` → `bot.pathfinder.setGoal(null)`.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py`, `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py`, `interface_dev/minecraft_bridge_minimal.js`, `docs/rift_vessel.rst`, `AGENTS.md` §5c, `plugins/rift_vessel/guide.md`, `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/guide.md`, `tests/test_vessel_action_namespacing.py` (2 new tests: `say` audio flag + combat/follow dispatch).
**Status:** DONE 2026-07-29 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean on the 2 backend Python files + the test; scoped `tests/test_vessel_action_namespacing.py tests/test_vessel_registry.py tests/test_vessel_commands.py tests/test_minecraft_connector.py` → 42 passed).
**Notes:** No keyword/regex logic (verbs are structural). Vessel actions stay off the Agent Lane. `follow` needs the optional `mineflayer-pathfinder` npm package; the bridge degrades gracefully without it. The bridge JS is a Node subprocess asset (not in the Python import graph) — a bridge restart / fresh connect applies it. Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build`.

### Rift Vessel — per-world action namespacing + hybrid action ownership  <!-- 2026-07-28 -->
**Change (feat + docs):** Answers "who owns the embodiment actions?". The Vessel is *infrastructure*; the active **game** owns the concrete actions. So the core plugin no longer exposes bare `vessel_connect`/`vessel_say`/… — it now exposes them **namespaced per active world** as `vessel_<world>_<verb>` (e.g. `vessel_minecraft_connect`, `vessel_minecraft_say`), where `<world>` is the active connector from `ACTIVE_VESSEL`. With no world active it degrades to the generic `vessel_<verb>` form. Ownership is **hybrid**: (a) a world-agnostic **core set** (`connect`/`disconnect`/`say`/`move`/`look`/`use`/`status`) stays owned by the Vessel and is shared by every world — guaranteeing world-agnostic portability; (b) each connector may add its **own** world-specific verbs (e.g. a future Minecraft `craft`/`mine`, Skyrim `cast_spell`/`sneak`) via a new optional `VesselConnectorBase.get_world_actions()` hook, exposed under the same `vessel_<world>_` prefix and dispatched via the existing `connector.act(verb, payload)`. Because every action is auto-exposed as an MCP tool, the tools become `synth_vessel_<world>_*` automatically.
**How:** (1) `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_base.py` adds an optional `get_world_actions() -> Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]]` (default `{}`) to `VesselConnectorBase` — connectors override it to declare extra verbs keyed by bare verb (same schema shape as `get_supported_actions`, must NOT declare `external_effects`). (2) `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py`: `_ACTION_VERBS` class attr holds the core set (each description carries a `{world}` placeholder); `_action_world()` resolves the active world (or `"vessel"`); `_world_extra_verbs()` fail-safely pulls the active connector's `get_world_actions()`, skipping collisions with the core set; `get_supported_actions()` builds the per-world prefix and emits both tiers, popping any stray `external_effects` from world verbs (Fast Lane, `security_level: "low"`); `_parse_action_verb()` accepts both the legacy `vessel_<verb>` and the namespaced `vessel_<world>_<verb>`; `handle_custom_action()` dispatches core verbs as before and forwards any other resolved verb to `connector.act(verb, payload)`. The Minecraft connector adds no extra verbs yet (stays core-set-only — correct default).
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_base.py`, `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py`, `docs/rift_vessel.rst`, `AGENTS.md` §5c, `plugins/rift_vessel/guide.md`, `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/guide.md`, `tests/test_vessel_action_namespacing.py` (NEW, 6 tests).
**Status:** DONE 2026-07-28 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean on the 2 backend Python files + the new test; scoped `tests/test_vessel_action_namespacing.py tests/test_vessel_registry.py tests/test_vessel_commands.py tests/test_minecraft_connector.py` → 40 passed).
**Notes:** No keyword/regex logic (verb parsing is prefix/suffix structural matching against the known verb set, not message text). Vessel actions stay off the Agent Lane (no `external_effects`, `security_level: "low"`). Backward compatible: `vessel_<verb>` still parses for the core set. Backend Python change → `docker compose up -d --build`.

### Rift Vessel — vessel_connect server-address override  <!-- 2026-07-27 -->
**Change (feat + docs):** The generic `vessel_connect` action can now override the world's server address for a single connect. The saved plugin config (`MINECRAFT_SERVER_HOST`/`MINECRAFT_SERVER_PORT`) stays the default; passing optional `host`/`port` in the action payload lets Synth join a different server on request without changing the saved defaults. `vessel_connect`/`vessel_disconnect` remain the login/logout capability (world-agnostic, in the core plugin — the Minecraft sub-plugin still exposes no actions of its own).
**How:** (1) `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py::get_supported_actions()` adds `optional_fields: ["host", "port"]` to `vessel_connect` (still `security_level: "low"`, no `external_effects` → Fast Lane); `connect_world()` gains an `overrides: dict | None` param that merges non-empty overrides onto a **copy** of the saved settings before `connector.connect(settings, ...)` (persistent config never mutated); `handle_custom_action()` threads the payload `host`/`port` into it. (2) `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` adds static `_resolve_server_target(settings)` (override `host`/`port` else the `MINECRAFT_SERVER_*` config) and sends the resolved target in the `/connect` body instead of `{}`. (3) `interface_dev/minecraft_bridge_minimal.js`: `connectBot(overrides)` reads `host`/`port`/`username` (falling back to `CFG`), and `POST /connect` now parses the request body and forwards it.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py`, `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py`, `interface_dev/minecraft_bridge_minimal.js`, `tests/test_minecraft_connector.py` (2 new `_resolve_server_target` tests).
**Status:** DONE 2026-07-27 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean on the 2 backend Python files; scoped `tests/test_minecraft_connector.py tests/test_vessel_commands.py tests/test_vessel_registry.py` → all pass, 19 in the connector file).
**Notes:** No keyword/regex logic (override comes from typed payload fields). Vessel actions stay off the Agent Lane (no `external_effects`). The bridge JS is a Node subprocess asset, not in the Python import graph — a bridge restart (or fresh connect) applies the change, a WebUI hard-refresh does not. ### Rift Vessel — real-time gaming focus: chat deprioritisation + world-scoped context  <!-- 2026-07-25 -->
**Change (feat + docs):** The Rift Vessel is used mainly in interactive game worlds, so embodiment must behave like a person *concentrating on the game* rather than multitasking every chat. Added two enforced behaviours, both decided **only from routing metadata (never message text)** and both lazily imported + fully guarded so removing the Vessel plugin can't break queueing or context assembly.
**(1) The game takes top priority during an active session.** `VesselSessionManager` now tracks live sessions in a cheap, DB-free, in-memory set (`_active_session_ids`, maintained by `start_session`/`end_session`) exposed via the new sync `has_active_session()`. When it's true, `core/message_queue.py::enqueue` **raises the Vessel's own in-world perceptions** (`interface == "vessel"`) to `HIGH_PRIORITY` and **lowers ordinary chat** from other interfaces from `NORMAL_PRIORITY` to `AGENT_PRIORITY`, so in-world perceptions drain first. Exemptions: urgent/HIGH (`priority=True`) messages always pass untouched, and the trainer (`TRAINER_CHAT_ID`) is never deprioritised. No active session → queue behaviour unchanged.
**(2) Vessel-focus turns get world-scoped context.** `core/history_engine.py::build_context` detects an embodiment turn from routing metadata (`interface_path` starting with `vessel`, a `chat.type == "vessel"` message, or an explicit `vessel_focus` context flag) and forces `unified_mode = False` while disabling the global diary/memory injections — keeping only persona/profile + local vessel history. SyntH isn't omniscient while playing: it doesn't read other chats in real time mid-session (catch-up happens in quiet moments and at end-of-session).
**Action speed observability.** `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py::act()` now logs the connector round-trip at `INFO` (`act('...') dispatched via '...' in N ms`). The *decision* to act still costs a full cognition turn; a reflex/attention layer that reacts without a full LLM turn is a documented future phase (must also respect constraint 1).
**Location (edited):** `core/vessel_session_manager.py` (`_active_session_ids` set + `has_active_session()`), `core/message_queue.py` (`enqueue` deprioritisation block), `core/history_engine.py` (`build_context` vessel-focus gate), `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` (latency INFO log), `docs/rift_vessel.rst` (new "Real-time gaming focus" section), `AGENTS.md` §5c (two enforced behaviours), tests `tests/test_vessel_session_manager.py` / `tests/test_vessel_realtime.py`.
**Status:** DONE 2026-07-25 (ruff format + check clean on the touched files; `ty check` on the edited files reports only the pre-existing `message_queue.py` diagnostics — `PriorityQueue._queue` access, `None` parameter defaults, external-arg types — none introduced by this change; scoped `tests/test_vessel_realtime.py tests/test_vessel_session_manager.py tests/test_vessel_registry.py tests/test_minecraft_connector.py tests/test_minecraft_provisioner.py tests/test_vessel_commands.py` → 49 passed. New `tests/test_vessel_realtime.py` (6 tests) covers: `has_active_session()` toggle; in-world perception raised to `HIGH_PRIORITY`; ordinary chat lowered to `AGENT_PRIORITY`; trainer stays `NORMAL_PRIORITY`; no-session leaves priority unchanged; `priority=True` stays `HIGH_PRIORITY`).
**Notes:** No keyword/regex logic (decisions come from interface origin + active-session flag only). Both behaviours degrade gracefully: the `has_active_session()` flag is best-effort (empty after a restart until sessions re-register) and every Vessel import in the queue/context path is guarded, so a missing Vessel plugin leaves enqueue/`build_context` untouched.

### Rift Vessel — Minecraft config polish: on-demand bridge, username override, descriptions  <!-- 2026-07-24 -->
**Change (feat + fix + docs):** Four refinements to the Minecraft Vessel sub-plugin. (1) Added descriptive text to the five previously label-only config keys (`MINECRAFT_BRIDGE_HOST`/`PORT`, `MINECRAFT_SERVER_HOST`/`PORT`, and the renamed username key). (2) Renamed `MINECRAFT_BOT_USERNAME` → `MINECRAFT_BOT_USERNAME_OVERRIDE` (default now **empty**, `advanced`): the in-world bot name falls back to Synth's configured `SYNTH_NAME` unless an override is set. (3) The Mineflayer bridge now starts **on demand** — automatically when the connector connects (i.e. when Synth enters the world) — instead of requiring a boot flag; the previously dead `MINECRAFT_BRIDGE_RUN_AT_START` var is now consumed as an optional boot pre-warm. (4) The Minecraft Vessel config guide is surfaced in the plugins screen (verified the guide resolves via the sub-folder reflection path).
**How:** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` `_register_config()` adds descriptions + the rename; new `_ensure_bridge_running()` is awaited at the top of `connect()` (followed by a 10× health-check retry loop) so the bridge is provisioned lazily when Synth actually plays. `interface/minecraft_provisioner.py::_bridge_env()` resolves `MINECRAFT_BOT_USERNAME_OVERRIDE` first, falling back to `SYNTH_NAME`. `interface/vessel_interface.py::start()` gains `_maybe_autostart_bridge()`, which honors `MINECRAFT_BRIDGE_RUN_AT_START` as an opt-in boot pre-warm. Kept the duplicate eager registration in `core/core_initializer.py` in sync.

### Rift Vessel — Minecraft split into a separate attachable sub-plugin (Grillo-style)  <!-- 2026-07-24 -->
**Change (refactor):** The Rift Vessel and its Minecraft world were conflated into a **single** WebUI entity — Minecraft had no banner of its own (a connector ships only `CONNECTOR_CLASS`, so the loader imported it but skipped it as a plugin) and its config lived under the same component as the global Vessel settings. Restructured to the **G.R.I.L.L.O. model**: a **core** plugin plus **attachable** per-world sub-plugins, each a first-class, separately toggleable WebUI entity with its own config namespace.
**How:** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` now exposes **both** module-level classes: the existing `CONNECTOR_CLASS = MinecraftConnector` (self-registers on `VESSEL_REGISTRY` at import — the world driver) **and** a new `PLUGIN_CLASS = MinecraftVesselPlugin` — a thin, action-less `PluginBase` that gives Minecraft its own WebUI banner (`display_name "Minecraft Vessel"`, `category "Vessels"`, `icon.svg`/`guide.md`) and owns the Minecraft-specific config under component `minecraft_vessel` (`MINECRAFT_BRIDGE_ENABLED`, `MINECRAFT_BRIDGE_RUN_AT_START`, `MINECRAFT_BRIDGE_HOST`/`PORT`, `MINECRAFT_SERVER_HOST`/`PORT`, `MINECRAFT_BOT_USERNAME`). Its `get_supported_actions()`/`get_supported_action_types()` return empty — the generic `vessel_*` actions stay in the core. The core `vessel_plugin` keeps only the **global** config (`ACTIVE_VESSEL`, `VESSEL_SETTINGS`, plus newly relocated `VESSEL_SESSION_COOLDOWN_SEC`) under component `vessel_plugin`. `MinecraftConnector._resolve_base_url` now reads `MINECRAFT_BRIDGE_HOST`/`PORT` from `group="plugins", component="minecraft_vessel"`.
**Location (edited):** `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (added `MinecraftVesselPlugin` + `PLUGIN_CLASS`; connector base-url component change), `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` (global-only config under `vessel_plugin`; added `VESSEL_SESSION_COOLDOWN_SEC`), `core/core_initializer.py` (eager config-registration block reconciled: global keys → component `vessel_plugin`, Minecraft keys → component `minecraft_vessel`, added missing labels), `tests/test_minecraft_connector.py` (3 new tests for `MinecraftVesselPlugin`), `docs/rift_vessel.rst` (new "Core + attachable sub-plugins" section + rewritten "Extending — new worlds"), `AGENTS.md` §5c (reconciled the former "connectors are not plugins" note into the Grillo-style core + attachable world sub-plugins pattern).
**Status:** DONE 2026-07-24 (ruff format + check clean on the touched files; `ty check` clean; scoped `tests/test_minecraft_connector.py tests/test_vessel_registry.py tests/test_minecraft_provisioner.py tests/test_vessel_commands.py` → 41 passed; smoke test confirms two distinct plugin banners — core "Rift Vessel" + "Minecraft Vessel" — and the `minecraft` connector still registered on `VESSEL_REGISTRY`).
**Notes:** No keyword/regex logic. Minecraft ships an original non-branded `icon.svg` (no trademarked logo). Adding a new world now means shipping both a `CONNECTOR_CLASS` and a thin `PLUGIN_CLASS` under `plugins/rift_vessel/<world>/`; a world without a `PLUGIN_CLASS` still works as a connector but gets no separate banner/config.

### Rift Vessel — multi-world embodiment framework + Minecraft PoC  <!-- 2026-07-28 -->
**Change (feature):** Added the **Rift Vessel** subsystem, letting SyntH inhabit external game/virtual worlds through pluggable connectors while its identity, memory, and personality persist across worlds and chat interfaces. First shipped connector is a Minecraft proof-of-concept over a Node.js Mineflayer HTTP bridge; Skyrim/VRChat/Hytale are registry-ready only.
**Design constraints (all enforced):** (1) Vessel actions declare **no** `external_effects` — they stay on the Fast Lane (`run_actions`) and never create agentic tasks / Drones (each is still passively auto-exposed as an MCP tool `synth_vessel_*`). (2) **No diary during a session** — events accumulate in an in-DB `experience_buffer`; a single autobiographical "lived experience" diary entry is written only at end-of-session (explicit logout OR `VESSEL_SESSION_COOLDOWN_SEC` idle, default 3600 s). (3) The Vessel has its own Activities voice like Radio/Grillo: `vessel_activity_log` + `/api/history/vessel` + a dedicated History sub-tab (🌀) with per-item delete.
**Location (new):** `core/vessel_registry.py` (`VesselRegistry`, `VESSEL_REGISTRY`, `register_vessel_connector`; connectors expose module-level `CONNECTOR_CLASS`), `plugins/rift_vessel/` (folder-per-plugin, §4): `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_base.py` (`VesselConnectorBase` ABC + `WorldState`/`PerceptionEvent`/`VesselActionResult` dataclasses), `plugins/rift_vessel/vessel_plugin.py` (`VesselPlugin(AIPluginBase)`, `get_metadata()` category `Vessels` + `icon.svg`/`guide.md`, actions `vessel_say`/`vessel_move`/`vessel_look`/`vessel_use`/`vessel_status`, all `security_level: "low"`, no `external_effects`), `plugins/rift_vessel/icon.svg` + `plugins/rift_vessel/guide.md` + `plugins/rift_vessel/__init__.py`, `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/minecraft.py` (aiohttp bridge client, self-registers at import) + `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/icon.svg` + `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/guide.md` + `plugins/rift_vessel/minecraft/__init__.py`, `core/vessel_session_manager.py` (session lifecycle + buffer + end-of-session flush), `interface/vessel_interface.py` (duck-typed I/O; inbound salience filter dedup 30 s + rate-limit 2 s, LLM-free; scheduler closes idle sessions), `interface_dev/minecraft_bridge_minimal.js` (Mineflayer↔HTTP bridge), `interface/minecraft_provisioner.py` (`BridgeProvisioner` install/start/stop/status/logs), `docs/rift_vessel.rst`, tests `tests/test_vessel_registry.py`, `tests/test_minecraft_connector.py`, `tests/test_minecraft_provisioner.py`, `tests/test_vessel_commands.py`.
