**Location (edited):** `core/core_initializer.py` (eager `vessel` config keys), `core/db.py` (`init_vessel_tables` — `vessel_sessions`, `vessel_activity_log`; `created_at`/`event_timestamp`, never bare `timestamp`), `init-db.sql` (seed both tables), `core/webui.py` (`history_vessel` + `delete_vessel_entry` + routes), `core/webui_templates/sections/history.html` (🌀 sub-tab), `res/synth_webui/js/history.js` (vessel history render/delete), `core/command_registry.py` (`/vessel status`, `/minecraft provision start|stop|status|logs [n]`), `Dockerfile` (`ARG INSTALL_NODE=false` + conditional NodeSource install — default build stays node-free; `docker build --build-arg INSTALL_NODE=true` for the PoC), `docs/index.rst` (toctree).
**Config keys:** `ACTIVE_VESSEL` (`"disabled"`), `VESSEL_SETTINGS`, `VESSEL_SESSION_COOLDOWN_SEC` (3600), `MINECRAFT_BRIDGE_ENABLED` (False), `MINECRAFT_BRIDGE_RUN_AT_START`, `MINECRAFT_BRIDGE_HOST` (127.0.0.1), `MINECRAFT_BRIDGE_PORT` (8137), `MINECRAFT_SERVER_HOST` (127.0.0.1), `MINECRAFT_SERVER_PORT` (25565), `MINECRAFT_BOT_USERNAME` (Synth).
**Status:** DONE 2026-07-28 (scoped `tests/test_vessel_*.py tests/test_minecraft_*.py` → 38 passed; ruff format + check + `ty check` on the touched files clean).
**Notes:** No keyword/regex logic anywhere (salience is dedup + rate-limit only; a richer LLM salience worker is a documented future phase that must also respect constraint 1). Minecraft PoC uses offline auth; real Microsoft/XBL auth and advanced multiplayer sync are out of scope. Node is not in the default image (`python:3.12-slim`) — the bridge needs the `INSTALL_NODE=true` build; the provisioner returns a clear error if `node`/`npm` are absent. Removing any connector, the plugin, or the interface must not break the rest of the system (golden rule).

### Interface enable/disable toggles + WebUI self-lockout protection + toggle-persist fix  <!-- 2026-07-23 -->
**Change (feature + fix):** Extended the runtime enable/disable mechanism (previously plugins-only) to **interfaces** (Telegram, Discord, Matrix, OpenAI-compatible API), and fixed a latent persistence bug that affected both plugin and interface toggles.
**Feature — interface toggles:** Added to `core/core_initializer.py`: `_interface_enabled_config_key(name)` → `INTERFACE_ENABLED__<name>` (hidden, group `interfaces`), `is_core_interface(name)`, `_is_interface_enabled(name)` (config-backed, default True, lazily registers the flag), `_teardown_interface_instance(instance)` (best-effort `stop`/`teardown`/`shutdown`/`close`), `disable_interface(name)` (teardown + unregister actions + drop from `active_interfaces` + grey SKIPPED ghost record + persist False; instance kept in `INTERFACE_REGISTRY`), and `enable_interface(name)` (persist True + re-register + restart). `_start_interfaces` gained a startup guard that skips disabled interfaces (grey ghost record) so a toggle survives restarts. `core/webui.py` routes `POST /api/components/toggle` to the interface path when the target is an interface (403 for core interfaces) and emits `enabled` / `disable_allowed` / grey led / `disabled` status in the interfaces grid. The classic WebUI (`res/synth_webui/js/main.js`) already renders a toggle for any non-MCP item with `disable_allowed !== false`, so the interface toggles appear automatically (no Vue frontend toggle exists).
**Protection — WebUI self-lockout:** `CORE_INTERFACE_NAMES` now contains `{"synth_webui"}`. The WebUI is the surface used to manage every toggle, so disabling it would lock the operator out until a manual DB edit + restart. It now reports `disable_allowed: false` and cannot be turned off from the grid.
**Fix — toggle did not persist:** `config_registry.set_value(key, …)` raises `KeyError: Unknown configuration key` when the key was never registered in `_definitions`. The plugin load path uses each plugin's own `is_enabled()`, not `_is_plugin_enabled()`, so `PLUGIN_ENABLED__<name>` was never registered → disabling `radio_host` logged `Failed to persist disabled state for radio_host: 'Unknown configuration key: PLUGIN_ENABLED__radio_host'` and the toggle worked at runtime but reverted on restart. Fixed by calling the corresponding getter (`_is_plugin_enabled` / `_is_interface_enabled`) — which lazily registers the definition — immediately before `set_value` in all four persist blocks (`disable_plugin`, `enable_plugin`, `disable_interface`, `enable_interface`).
**Location:** `core/core_initializer.py` (interface methods + `CORE_INTERFACE_NAMES` + `_start_interfaces` guard + 4 persist-block getter calls), `core/webui.py` (toggle routing + interfaces_data fields).
**Status:** DONE 2026-07-23. Validated by rebuild + runtime test: `docker compose up -d --build synth`; 45 plugins load; disabling `radio_host` and `telegram_bot` via `POST /api/components/toggle` returned `ok:true`, unloaded/stopped cleanly (`RadioHostPlugin stopped`, `Interface 'telegram_bot' disabled (stopped)`), **zero** `Failed to persist` / `Unknown configuration key` warnings, and both `PLUGIN_ENABLED__radio_host=false` / `INTERFACE_ENABLED__telegram_bot=false` were confirmed written to the `config` table. `synth_webui` reports `disable_allowed:false`; all other interfaces `true`. ruff/ty clean on `core/core_initializer.py`.
**Known pre-existing bug (NOT fixed — out of scope):** Re-enabling a **plugin** loaded from a sub-folder via the toggle can return `{"detail":"unknown_plugin_module"}` — `enable_plugin` reads `info.module_name` from the ghost `ComponentInfo`, which was empty for `radio_host`. Runtime re-enable of that plugin fails; the reliable path is toggling the persisted flag and restarting (radio_host was restored this way). Interface re-enable is unaffected (`telegram_bot` re-enabled cleanly at runtime). This is a plugin-toggle limitation predating this change; documented here for the next agent.

### Removed redundant log plugins — agent tools supersede them  <!-- 2026-07-22 -->
**Change (removal):** Deleted the two log-reading plugins now that the Agentic Runtime 2.0 reads logs on demand. `plugins/check_logs.py` (`CheckLogsPlugin`, actions `get_logs`/`search_logs`) was already dead code — its module ended with `PLUGIN_CLASS = None`, so the loader (`core/core_initializer.py`) never registered it, and its capability is fully covered by `agent_read_file` / `agent_search_files` / `agent_list_files` (which reach `SYNTH_LOG_DIR` with no filename allowlist). `plugins/recon_log_reader.py` (`ReconLogReaderPlugin`) passively injected log snippets into the Recon context (recon key `log_request`, emitting `log_flag` + `snippet`); its pre-reasoning role is now handled by the agent fetching logs on demand.
**Location:** deleted `plugins/check_logs.py`, `plugins/recon_log_reader.py`, `tests/plugins/test_check_logs.py` (already skipped at module level), `docs/plugins/check_logs.rst` (orphan, not in any toctree); edited `docs/plugins.rst` (removed `get_logs`/`search_logs` bullets), `docs/recon_debrief_hooks.rst` (dropped `log_flag` from the contribution-type list; swapped the `recon_log_reader.py` example for `recon_tone_evaluator.py`), `AGENTS.md` (removed the `RECON_LOG_READER_LINES` config-key row).
**Status:** DONE 2026-07-22 (`uv run ruff check plugins/ tests/plugins/` — only pre-existing E402 in `test_memory_search.py`, unrelated; `uv run pytest tests/plugins/` → 38 passed, 5 skipped; grep confirms no live references to `CheckLogsPlugin` / `recon_log_reader` / `RECON_LOG_READER` / `log_request` / `log_flag` / `get_logs` / `search_logs` outside CHANGELOG/FIXED_ISSUES history).
**Kept intentionally:** the **LogChat notifier** (`core/logging_utils.py`, `core/notifier.py`, `core/config.py`, `/logchat` command) is **not** redundant — it proactively pushes ERROR/WARNING notifications to a chat (push/event-driven), which the pull-only agent runtime cannot architecturally replace. The dev-facing `mcp_servers/synth_logs.py` `search_logs` tool is a separate MCP server and was untouched. Orphan `RECON_LOG_READER_*` rows in existing DBs are inert and left as-is (no cleanup migration).
**Notes:** Purely a removal, no keyword/regex logic involved. If `AGENT_ENABLED=false` there are now no action-based log-reading paths (there were none live before either, since `check_logs` was dead), but Recon and LogChat-independent diagnostics are unaffected.

### Agent Lane completion via `{"tool": ...}` shape was dropped — raw `attempt_completion` JSON leaked to Telegram and got skipped  <!-- 2026-07-22 -->
**Symptom:** An agentic turn reported (in its own `attempt_completion` summary) that it had delivered a Telegram message to `telegram_bot/-1003098886330/2`, but the user never received it. Logs showed the delivery action DID fire with the correct `target`/`thread_id` (the earlier 2026-07-22 `_deliver_agent_reply` fix works), yet the `text` delivered was the **raw JSON string** `{"tool":"attempt_completion","payload":{"summary":"..."}}`. `cortex_response_send` parsed it as JSON, `corrector_orchestrator` logged `Unrecognized JSON structure`, and `telegram_utils` logged `Message ... queued/skipped due to cooldown or error` — so nothing was sent.
**Root cause:** `AgentLoopManager._extract_tool_calls` (`core/agent_core.py`) normalised only the `actions` / `tool_calls` / `calls` / `type` / `name` JSON shapes. Some engines (here `logfare-claude` via `AGENT_CORTEX`) end the turn with a single top-level object keyed by **`tool`** — `{"tool": "attempt_completion", "payload": {...}}`. That shape matched none of the branches, so the completion sentinel was never recognised: `completion_calls` stayed empty, the turn never entered the completion path, and the raw model JSON fell through as `final_text`, which `_deliver_agent_reply` then sent verbatim to the interface.
**Change (fix):** In `_extract_tool_calls`, the single-object branch now derives the tool name from `type` **or** `name` **or** `tool` (whichever is present) before reading `arguments`/`payload`/`args`, so a `{"tool": ..., "payload": ...}` object is normalised identically to the `type`/`name` forms. This makes `attempt_completion` (and any single tool call) in the `tool`-keyed shape resolve correctly, so the clean `summary` becomes `final_text` and is delivered as plain text.
**Location:** `core/agent_core.py` (`AgentLoopManager._extract_tool_calls`); regression test `tests/test_agentic_runtime.py::test_run_agentic_turn_completed_tool_key`.
**Status:** DONE 2026-07-22 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check core/agent_core.py` clean; `tests/test_agentic_runtime.py` → 27 passed; gitnexus impact on `_extract_tool_calls` → LOW risk, sole d=1 caller is `run_agentic_turn`; detect_changes → LOW, confined to `core/agent_core.py` + the test file).
**Notes:** Purely structural JSON-shape normalisation, no keyword/regex logic — it accepts a synonym key, it does not match on tool *values*. The prompt (`_build_agent_prompt`) lists tool names but does not pin an exact JSON envelope, so tolerating the common `tool`-keyed shape is the robust fix rather than constraining the model. A secondary hardening (making `cortex_response_send` send unrecognised JSON as plain text instead of skipping it) was left out to avoid scope creep — with `final_text` now clean, that path is no longer hit for this case.

### WebUI Agent tab stopped "constantly refreshing" — polling re-render is now diff-guarded and selection-aware  <!-- 2026-07-27 -->
**Symptom (behavioural, not a crash):** The Agent tab felt like it "kept refreshing"; the user could not select text or scroll freely. Every 5s (`fetchAgentTasks`) and 15s (`fetchAgentTools`) the poll blindly did `container.innerHTML = html` on the task list, the tools list, and the conversation pane (`loadAgentTask` → `conv.innerHTML`), **even when the fetched data was identical**. Rewriting innerHTML destroys the DOM nodes backing any active text selection (so a highlight vanished the instant the user tried to copy) and re-attached all click listeners on every tick.
**Change (fix):** Added two small helpers to `res/synth_webui/js/agent.js`. `_userIsSelectingIn(el)` returns true when a non-collapsed selection's anchor lives inside `el`. `_renderIfChanged(el, html)` writes `innerHTML` **only** when `html` differs from the cached `el.__lastRenderedHtml` **and** the user is not selecting inside `el` (otherwise it stashes the markup in `el.__pendingRenderHtml`, applied on the next poll once the selection is released); it returns whether it actually wrote. All innerHTML assignments in `fetchAgentTools`, `fetchAgentTasks` (including the empty-list branch), and the `loadAgentTask` conversation pane now route through `_renderIfChanged`. Task-list click/rename/delete listeners are only (re)bound when the list markup actually changed (`if(listChanged){…}`), and the conversation only auto-scrolls when it was genuinely rewritten. The existing `_isConversationNearBottom` / `wasNearBottom` scroll-sticky logic is preserved, so incremental updates for genuinely new content still work.
**Location:** `res/synth_webui/js/agent.js` (`_userIsSelectingIn`, `_renderIfChanged`, `fetchAgentTools`, `fetchAgentTasks`, `loadAgentTask`).
**Status:** DONE 2026-07-27 (JS-only, no lint/type errors reported by the editor; static asset — verify by hard-reloading the WebUI or rebuilding the container).
**Notes:** Purely structural, no keyword/regex logic. `#agent-task-meta` still uses direct innerHTML — it is a short status bar, not a read/select surface, and is not the source of the complaint.

### Agent Lane delivery/diary actions fixed — source `interface`/`interface_path` now reaches the prompt and tools  <!-- 2026-07-26 -->
**Change (fix):** In an agentic turn (Agent Lane / Drone) the agent could write files but the final steps failed: `message_telegram_bot` was rejected by validation ("payload.interface_path or payload.chat_name is required") and `create_personal_diary_entry` persisted with `interface="unknown"` / `chat_id=None`. Two root causes in the Agent Lane.
(1) `_build_agent_prompt` never surfaced the originating `interface_path` in the prompt text, so the model had no value to place in `message_telegram_bot`'s required `interface_path` field. Fixed by adding a "SOURCE CONVERSATION" block to the prompt that states the exact `interface_path` (and interface) and instructs delivery/message actions to reuse it verbatim.
(2) The router (`core/agent_router.py`) only sets `context["interface_path"]`, never `context["interface"]`; internal tools run by the executor read `context["interface"]`, so the diary saved as "unknown". Fixed by deriving `interface` from `interface_path` once at the top of `run_agentic_turn` (via `core.interface_path_utils.get_interface_from_path`) and enriching the shared `context`, so both the prompt text and every executed tool see it. Drones inherit the enrichment because `run_drone` delegates to `run_agentic_turn`.
**Location:** `core/agent_core.py` (`AgentLoopManager.run_agentic_turn` context enrichment; `_build_agent_prompt` SOURCE CONVERSATION block); `AGENTS.md` §12.
**Status:** DONE 2026-07-26 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check core/agent_core.py` clean; `tests/test_agentic_runtime.py tests/test_agent_plugin.py` → 41 passed; gitnexus detect_changes → LOW risk, confined to `core/agent_core.py`).
**Notes:** Purely structural, no keyword/regex logic. The derivation is best-effort — a failure to parse the path only skips the enrichment (logged at debug) and never blocks the turn. The pre-existing broken `tests/test_agent_core.py` (imports a removed `AgentCore` symbol) is unrelated to this change and was excluded from the scoped run.

### Every Synth action auto-exposed as MCP tool + native `agent_edit_file` / `agent_search_files`  <!-- 2026-07-25 -->
**Change (feature):** Two related changes to the Agentic Runtime 2.0.
(1) **Auto-exposure, no whitelist.** The MCP publish bridge no longer reads a whitelist — *every* Synth action is now automatically published as an MCP tool named `synth_<action_name>`, with zero developer opt-in. This realises the design rule "any Synth action must also be a tool without developers doing anything". The `AGENT_MCP_EXPOSED_ACTIONS` config variable was removed.
(2) **Two new native file tools.** Added `agent_edit_file` (literal find-and-replace with occurrence-count safety) and `agent_search_files` (read-only in-sandbox grep) to the Agent plugin.
**Location:** `core/mcp_bridge/server.py` (rewrote `_get_exposed_action_names()` to enumerate `core.tool_registry.tool_registry.internal_tools()`; removed the `DEFAULT_EXPOSED_ACTIONS` constant and the `AGENT_MCP_EXPOSED_ACTIONS` read); `core/core_initializer.py` (removed the `AGENT_MCP_EXPOSED_ACTIONS` eager-registration block); `plugins/agent_plugin.py` (`import re`; `get_supported_action_types`, `get_supported_actions`, `execute_action` dispatch, new `_edit_file()` / `_search_files()` helpers, `get_prompt_instructions`); `AGENTS.md` §5b (auto-exposure paragraph + the two new tool paragraphs; removed `AGENT_MCP_EXPOSED_ACTIONS` from the config-keys line); `docs/agentic_tools.rst` (Phase F, connect-as-coding-agent, config table — all whitelist references removed); tests `tests/test_agent_plugin.py`, `tests/test_agentic_runtime.py`.
**Status:** DONE 2026-07-25 (ruff format + check clean; `ty check` clean on `plugins/agent_plugin.py core/mcp_bridge/server.py core/core_initializer.py`; `tests/test_agent_plugin.py tests/test_agentic_runtime.py` → 41 passed).
**Spec:** `agent_edit_file` — `required_fields: ["path", "old_string", "new_string"]`, `optional_fields: ["expected_replacements"]`, `security_level: "medium"`, `external_effects: ["filesystem"]`. Counts occurrences of `old_string` and requires the count to exactly equal `expected_replacements` (default 1, clamped 1–10000); `old_string`/`new_string` must be non-empty and differ; result capped at 2 MB. Returns `{"status": "ok", "path", "replacements", "bytes_written"}`. `agent_search_files` — `required_fields: ["pattern"]`, `optional_fields: ["path", "regex", "case_sensitive", "glob", "max_results", "max_file_bytes"]`, **no** `security_level`/`external_effects` (read-only, stays on the Fast Lane). Recurses via `rglob(glob)`, `re.IGNORECASE` unless `case_sensitive`, caps results at `max_results` (default 200) and lines at 1000 chars. Returns `{"status": "ok", "path", "files_scanned", "count", "truncated", "matches": [{"path", "line", "text"}]}`.
**Notes:** MCP tool names have no hard `tool_` prefix requirement — the `synth_` prefix is a namespacing choice, so an action like `message_telegram_bot` surfaces as `synth_message_telegram_bot`. Safety is unchanged: every tool call still funnels through `core.action_safety.is_action_allowed_for_execution`, so each action's security level is enforced regardless of how it is invoked. Both new file tools are **native Python** for the same no-node reason as `agent_write_file` (`python:3.12-slim` runtime image has no npx). The historical CHANGELOG entry that registered `AGENT_MCP_EXPOSED_ACTIONS` (eager-registration change) is intentionally left as immutable history.

### Agent gains shell execution — native `agent_run_shell`, gated by container detection  <!-- 2026-07-24 -->
**Change (feature):** Added a native `agent_run_shell` action to the Agentic Runtime 2.0 toolset so the agent can run build/test/git/system commands it cannot express with the file actions. Security is modulated by whether Synth runs inside a container.
**Location:** `plugins/agent_plugin.py` (new module helper `_is_in_container()`, new `_run_shell()` method, `get_supported_action_types`, `get_supported_actions`, `execute_action` branch, `get_prompt_instructions`, `AGENT_SHELL_ALLOW_HOST` var registration); `AGENTS.md` §5b + §14; tests `tests/test_agent_plugin.py`.
**Status:** DONE 2026-07-24 (ruff/ty clean on edited files; `tests/test_agent_plugin.py` → 8 passed).
**Spec:** `agent_run_shell` — `required_fields: ["command"]`, `optional_fields: ["cwd", "timeout"]`, `security_level: "high"`, `external_effects: ["shell"]`. Returns `{status, exit_code, cwd, stdout, stderr, truncated}`. `cwd` (default first allowed root) is confined to `_allowed_roots()` via `_resolve_safe_path()`; runs through `bash -c`/`sh -c` under `asyncio.create_subprocess_exec`; `timeout` clamped 1–600 s (default 60); stdout/stderr each capped at 40 000 chars. `external_effects` routes it to the Agent Lane automatically.
**Security model:** `_is_in_container()` resolves the environment in order — explicit `SYNTH_IN_CONTAINER` env override → `/.dockerenv` (Docker) / `/run/.containerenv` (Podman) → `docker`/`kubepods`/`containerd`/`libpod` marker in `/proc/1/cgroup` — defaulting to `False` (host) when unsure. The action **only executes inside a container**; on a bare host it refuses unless the new `AGENT_SHELL_ALLOW_HOST` config var (default `False`) is explicitly enabled, because a shell on the host is a real machine-compromise risk for a public persona.
**Notes:** Native Python for the same reason as `agent_write_file` — the runtime image `python:3.12-slim` has no node/npx, so no `npx`-based shell MCP could start in-container. No dedicated container-detection variable existed before this change; `_is_in_container()` is the first one.

### Agent gains write capability — native `agent_write_file` action (no MCP dependency)  <!-- 2026-07-23 -->
**Change (feature):** The Agentic Runtime 2.0 toolset was read-only (`agent_list_files`, `agent_read_file`, `spawn_drone`), so any goal requiring a file write looped until `AGENT_MAX_ITERATIONS` was exhausted with no matching tool. Added a native `agent_write_file` action to `plugins/agent_plugin.py`.
**Location:** `plugins/agent_plugin.py` (`get_supported_action_types`, `get_supported_actions`, `execute_action`, `get_prompt_instructions`); `config/synth_mcp.json` (documented why the standard filesystem MCP stays disabled); `AGENTS.md` §5b; tests `tests/test_agent_plugin.py`.
**Status:** DONE 2026-07-23 (ruff/ty clean on edited files; `tests/test_agent_plugin.py` → 4 passed).
**Spec:** `agent_write_file` — `required_fields: ["path", "content"]`, `optional_fields: ["mode"]` (`"overwrite"` default / `"append"`), `security_level: "medium"`, `external_effects: ["filesystem"]`. Reuses the existing `_resolve_safe_path()` / `_allowed_roots()` sandbox (same roots as `agent_read_file`), creates parent dirs, caps content at 2 MB, and returns `{"status": "ok", "path", "bytes_written", "mode"}` or `{"status": "error", "reason"}`. `external_effects` makes `core/agent_router.py` route it to the Agent Lane automatically.
**Notes:** Chosen as a **native Python** action over the standard filesystem MCP (`@modelcontextprotocol/server-filesystem`) because the runtime image `python:3.12-slim` has **no node/npx** (node lives only in the Dockerfile `stage_builder` build stage), so an `npx`-based MCP server cannot start in-container. The AGENTS.md §5b golden rule (dev MCP vs runtime MCP separation) did **not** need altering — the missing piece was a write tool, not MCP reuse. The pre-registered `filesystem` server in `config/synth_mcp.json` stays `"enabled": false` with a comment explaining the node + host-vs-container-path blockers.

### Legacy agent (proposal/approval model) fully removed — only Agentic Runtime 2.0 + Fast Lane remain  <!-- 2026-07-22 -->
**Change (cleanup, not a bug):** Removed the entire legacy agent subsystem (the proposal/approval/shell-whitelist model with `always_approve`/`whitelist`/`always_ask`/`disabled` modes). The codebase now keeps **only** the Agentic Runtime 2.0 (bounded `run_agentic_turn` loop, tools `agent_list_files`/`agent_read_file`/`spawn_drone`, single safety gate `core.action_safety.is_action_allowed_for_execution`, `agent_tasks` persistence) and the Fast Lane (always used when the agent is off).
**Location:** Python — `core/ai_plugin_base.py` (removed `supports_agent`/`attach_agent`/`detach_agent`/`agent_prepare_prompt`/`agent_execute`), the 5 external engines (`gemini_api.py`, `anthropic.py`, `openapi.py`, `openrouter.py`, `xai_grok.py`), `core/action_parser.py`, `core/auto_response.py`, `core/prompt_engine.py`, `core/command_registry.py`, `core/webui.py` (removed POST create / `/pause` / `/resume` / `/cancel` / all `/proposals` routes; kept task list/get/run/tools/delete/rename + `/message`), `plugins/agent_plugin.py` (now 2.0-only). DB layer — removed all `agent_activity_log` + `agent_action_execs` DDL/specs from `init-db.sql`, `scripts/sql/app_main_postgres.sql`, `core/db.py`, `core/db_backends.py`, `core/main_db_migration.py`, `tools/database_fix.py`. Frontend — `res/synth_webui/js/agent.js` (removed pause/resume/cancel buttons+handlers and the entire inline-proposals block). Docs — `docs/agent_integration.rst` rewritten to the 2.0 model; `docs/agentic_tools.rst`, `docs/api_endpoints.rst`, `docs/llm_engines.rst`, `docs/gemini/gemini_api_engine.rst` updated. Tests — deleted `tests/test_agent_loop.py`, `tests/test_recon_debrief.py`; trimmed `tests/test_xai_grok.py`; rewrote `tests/test_agent_plugin.py::test_is_enabled_reflects_toggle`. `AGENTS.md` DB reference + agent-plugin blurb updated.
**Status:** DONE 2026-07-22 (ruff/ty clean on all edited files; scoped pytest `tests/test_agentic_runtime.py tests/test_drones.py tests/test_agent_plugin.py tests/test_xai_grok.py tests/test_agent_time_injection.py` → 34 passed; import smoke passed; container rebuilt and started clean; `AgentPlugin.get_supported_action_types()` → `['agent_list_files', 'agent_read_file', 'spawn_drone']`).
**Notes:** `agent_tasks` is the 2.0 table and was kept everywhere. No runtime DROP migration was added — the legacy `agent_activity_log` / `agent_action_execs` tables may still exist in databases from earlier runs but are no longer created, backed up, or migration-specced. Dropping those residual tables from a live DB is a separate, destructive step left to the operator. The `CHANGELOG.md` history entries that mention the old proposal/approval model are intentionally left as immutable history.

### Agent loop wrote a diary entry on every iteration — now restricted to start/end of the turn  <!-- 2026-07-21 -->
**Symptom (behavioural, not a crash):** During a single agentic turn the bounded loop (`AgentLoopManager.run_agentic_turn`) executes **every** tool call the model emits on every iteration. Because the model can emit `create_personal_diary_entry` on each pass, a single multi-step task could trigger a diary write on every working iteration instead of a single opening/closing entry. (The write is an UPSERT of today's row, so it did not duplicate DB rows, but it wasted iterations and re-wrote the day's diary repeatedly.)
**Location:** `core/agent_core.py` (`AgentLoopManager._build_agent_prompt` system text; `run_agentic_turn` tool-execution loop). Test: `tests/test_agent_core.py`.
**Status:** FIXED 2026-07-21 (ruff/ty clean on the edited file; new deterministic unit test passing).
**Root cause:** The agent loop is intentionally tool-agnostic — it runs any tool call returned by the model — and `_build_agent_prompt` exposed all tools with no guidance on *when* journaling should happen. With a weak cortex engine the model tended to log a diary note on each iteration.
**Fix (two layers, both language-agnostic):**
- **Prompt guidance:** extended the agentic `system_text` in `_build_agent_prompt` with a "diary discipline" instruction — a single agentic task is one moment, so at most one diary entry at the start and one at the end; no diary tool calls on the intermediate working iterations; if prior observations already show a diary entry for this task, do not write another until the task is complete. This is behavioural/structural guidance, not keyword matching.
- **Deterministic guardrail:** in the `run_agentic_turn` tool-execution loop, diary tools (`create_personal_diary_entry`, `update_diary_entry`) are only executed on the first iteration (start) and the last allowed iteration (`i == max_iterations`, end). On intermediate iterations they are suppressed and recorded as an observation with error `diary_suppressed_mid_task`, so the model sees the feedback but no write occurs. This is an action-identity check on the registered action names — NOT language/keyword matching — and does not depend on the model honouring the prompt (weak engines proven unreliable for prompt-only nudges).
**Verification:** ruff/ty clean on `core/agent_core.py` (the 2 remaining `ty` diagnostics at lines 1089/1179 are pre-existing and unrelated to this change). New unit test `test_diary_only_executed_at_start_and_end` drives a 3-iteration loop where the mocked engine emits a working tool + a diary tool every iteration; it asserts the diary tool executes exactly twice (iterations 1 and 3) while the working tool executes 3 times — `tests/test_agent_core.py` 3 passed. Live E2E could not exercise the loop in this environment because the pinned `selenium-llm-engine` times out / returns empty (`Engine call timed out at iteration 1`), so validation relies on the deterministic unit test.
**Notes:** `create_personal_diary_entry` is an UPSERT (`plugins/ai_diary.py`), so the concern was wasted iterations and repeated re-writes, not row duplication. The guardrail keys on the exact registered action names so it stays correct across all interface languages.

### `recon_agent_intent` plugin silently skipped by the loader — `agent_intent` recon key never fired  <!-- 2026-07-21 -->
**Symptom (silent, not a crash):** The combined recon system prompt contained all the standard recon keys (`memory_search`, `log_request`, `tone_hint`, `web_search`, `channel_reference`, `language_hint`, `video_media`, `radio_status`, `growth_approval`, `recall_last_dream`) but **nothing for the agent** — no `agent_intent` key, no `[recon_agent_intent] Emitting agentic-intent instruction` in the logs, and `cortex_search("agent_intent")` returned zero sessions. The agent's recon contribution (the instruction that nudges the main model toward the agentic tool-using lane) never entered the prompt.
**Location:** `plugins/recon_agent_intent.py` (missing module-level export); loader in `core/core_initializer.py` (lines ~878–925).
**Status:** FIXED 2026-07-21 (rebuilt + verified live end-to-end on the real Postgres).
**Root cause:** `plugins/recon_agent_intent.py` never exported a module-level `PLUGIN_CLASS`. The plugin loader in `core/core_initializer.py` **silently skips any module without a top-level `PLUGIN_CLASS`** (`if not hasattr(module, "PLUGIN_CLASS"): continue`) with no warning. The plugin was therefore never instantiated, never registered in `PLUGIN_REGISTRY`, never `eligible` in `core/recon.py::gather_recon_contributions`, and its `agent_intent` key never entered the combined recon prompt. The recon eligibility logic and the gate key (`RECON_AGENT_INTENT_RECON_ENABLED`, default True) were both already correct — the plugin simply never loaded.
**Fix:** Appended `PLUGIN_CLASS = ReconAgentIntentPlugin` at the end of `plugins/recon_agent_intent.py`, matching the pattern of every other working recon plugin (`recon_web_search.py`, `recon_tone_evaluator.py`, etc.).
**Verification:** ruff/ty clean on the edited file. After rebuild the boot log shows `Plugin plugins.recon_agent_intent registered in PLUGIN_REGISTRY as 'recon_agent_intent'` and the plugin appears in the 50-plugin action-load list. The recon system prompt now lists `agent_intent` with its instruction + schema block. A live multi-step prompt via the Ollama-compatible API produced `[recon_agent_intent] Emitting agentic-intent instruction`, followed by `[agent_router] Multiple actions -> AGENT lane`, `[message_chain] 🤖 Agent Lane engaged for this turn`, and `[agent_router] Routing to Agent Lane`.
**Notes:** **Every SyntH plugin MUST export a module-level `PLUGIN_CLASS` or the loader silently skips it — no warning is emitted.** This is a common, silent failure mode; when a plugin "doesn't do anything" and produces no logs, check for a missing `PLUGIN_CLASS` first. This is distinct from (and was masked by) the `AGENTIC_ROUTING_ENABLED` DB-load bug fixed the same day — that one gated the flag off; this one prevented the recon contributor from ever loading.

### `AGENTIC_ROUTING_ENABLED=true` in DB was ignored — Agent Lane never engaged despite the flag being set  <!-- 2026-07-21 -->
**Symptom (silent, not a crash):** Even with `AGENTIC_ROUTING_ENABLED=true` in the `config` table, the Fast/Agent router always stayed in Fast Lane — no `🤖 Agent Lane engaged`, no `[agent_router] Routing to Agent Lane`, no `run_agentic_turn`. Logs showed `[config] Skipping DB load for 'AGENTIC_ROUTING_ENABLED' during async context (will use default)` on every read, so the gate read the code default `False`.
**Location:** `core/core_initializer.py` (`CoreInitializer.initialize_all`, new eager-registration block after step 4.5); root cause in `core/config_manager.py::load_all_from_db` / `_load_definition_sync`.
**Status:** FIXED 2026-07-21 (rebuilt + verified live end-to-end on the real Postgres).
**Root cause:** `AGENTIC_ROUTING_ENABLED` (and the other agentic keys) are only ever read via `config_registry.get_var(...)` **inside functions on the chat path** (`core/agent_router.classify`, the gate in `core/message_chain`, `plugins/recon_agent_intent`), never at module-import time. `load_all_from_db()` only sweeps keys already present in `_definitions`. Since these keys are registered lazily on the FIRST chat turn — which happens AFTER the boot-time DB sweep — the sweep never sees them, and the first `get_var` runs inside a live event loop where `_load_from_db_sync` deliberately skips the DB read → the flag permanently falls back to `False`. Same class of bug as `BOTFATHER_TOKEN` (see FIXED_ISSUES.md).
**Fix:** Added an eager-registration block in `initialize_all` (right after the existing `core.history_engine`/`core.chat_attention` early-import block, BEFORE `load_all_from_db()`) that calls `config_registry.get_var(...)` for `AGENTIC_ROUTING_ENABLED` (bool), `AGENT_MAX_ITERATIONS`, `AGENT_TURN_TIMEOUT_SEC`, `DRONE_MAX_ITERATIONS`, `DRONE_TURN_TIMEOUT_SEC` (int), and `AGENT_MCP_EXPOSED_ACTIONS` (str). This registers the definitions with their code defaults so the subsequent DB sweep populates them with the real DB values.
**Verification:** ruff/ty clean on the edited file. Boot log now shows `[config] Skipping 'AGENTIC_ROUTING_ENABLED': already has DB value (not default)` inside `load_all_from_db` (previously absent). Live E2E via the Ollama-compatible API with a multi-step prompt produced `[agent_router] Multiple actions -> AGENT lane`, `[message_chain] 🤖 Agent Lane engaged for this turn`, and `[agent_router] Routing to Agent Lane`.
**Notes:** Recon integration (`plugins/recon_agent_intent.py`) was already correctly wired — it uses the same flag and was gated off only because the flag read `False`. The eager registration also makes all these keys editable via the WebUI/DB and durable across boots.

### Agentic turns from any interface/API are now persisted as WebUI-visible `agent_tasks`  <!-- 2026-07-21 -->
**Symptom (gap, not a crash):** Only the WebUI route (`POST /api/agent/run`) wrote an `agent_tasks` row (via a WebUI-local `_persist_webui_agent_turn`). An agentic turn triggered from any other source — Telegram/Discord/Matrix, the Ollama-compatible API, or the internal Agent-Lane router — ran the bounded loop but persisted **nothing**, so it was invisible in the WebUI Agent panel.
**Location:** `core/agent_core.py` (`AgentLoopManager.run_agentic_turn`, new `_persist_agentic_turn`), `core/webui.py` (`run_agent_turn`, removed `_persist_webui_agent_turn`).
**Status:** FIXED 2026-07-21 (rebuilt + verified live end-to-end on the real Postgres).
**Fix:**
- Added a single source-agnostic persistence helper `AgentLoopManager._persist_agentic_turn(*, engine, goal, result, context, original_message, preplanned_calls) -> Optional[int]`. It builds `iterations_meta` from `result["observations"]`, derives `status` from `stop_reason` (`failed` for `timeout`/`engine_error`/`empty_response`, else `completed`), `trainer_id` from the originating `original_message` (`sender_id`/`user_id`), and `metadata.source` + `interface_path` from the originating interface context. Persistence is **best-effort** — an outer try/except logs and swallows any DB failure so it never breaks the turn.
- Wired it into **both** exit points of `run_agentic_turn` (the deterministic `preplanned_calls` early-return and the main loop end); each sets `result["task_id"] = await self._persist_agentic_turn(...)` before returning.
- **Postgres fix:** asyncpg does not populate `cur.lastrowid`, so the INSERT uses `INSERT ... RETURNING id` (reads `await cur.fetchone()`) with a try/except fallback to a plain INSERT + `getattr(cur, "lastrowid", None)` for MariaDB.
- **De-duplication:** deleted `core/webui.py::_persist_webui_agent_turn` (no other callers) and made `run_agent_turn` read `task_id = result.get("task_id")` from the loop result instead of persisting its own row.
**Verification:** ruff/ty clean on both edited files; `tests/test_agentic_runtime.py` 8 passed. LIVE on real Postgres: WebUI `POST /api/agent/run` returned `task_id:3` and created exactly one row (`source=synth_webui`, `interface_path=synth_webui/agent`, `trainer_id=webui`, `status=completed`) — no duplicate. A simulated Telegram-context turn created `task_id=4` with `source=telegram_bot`, `interface_path=telegram_bot/999`, `trainer_id=999`, proving non-WebUI origins are now WebUI-visible. Test rows cleaned up afterward.
**Notes:** The existing `_create_agent_task`/`_update_agent_task_status`/`_finalize_task` helpers (used by the older `run_loop` background path) are unchanged — `_persist_agentic_turn` is a separate, self-contained end-of-turn record for the `run_agentic_turn` entrypoint. Status values conform to the `agent_tasks` CHECK constraint (`pending/running/waiting_for_approval/paused/completed/failed/cancelled`).

### Agent proposals can now be rejected from chat interfaces; Matrix command path secured  <!-- 2026-07-21 -->
**Symptom (gap, not a crash):** Agent allow/reject proposals (`plugins/agent_plugin.py`) could be *approved* from Telegram/Discord via ``/agent approve <id>``, but there was **no ``/agent reject``** on any chat interface — rejecting was WebUI-only. Additionally the Matrix interface dispatched all slash commands through ``execute_command(command, *args)`` with **no trainer permission check and no interface_context**, so ``/agent approve|reject`` (and every other command) was an unauthenticated vector on Matrix.
**Location:** `plugins/agent_plugin.py` (`get_supported_actions`, `execute_action`, proposal notification text), `core/command_registry.py` (`agent_command`), `interface/matrix_interface.py` (slash-command dispatch).
**Status:** FIXED 2026-07-21 (rebuilt + verified live end-to-end).
**Fix:**
- Added a symmetric ``reject_action`` to the agent plugin (`get_supported_actions` entry + `execute_action` branch): validates the proposal exists and is still ``proposed`` (returns structured errors for not-found / wrong-state), then `_update_activity_log(id, status="rejected", trainer_id=..., result="rejected")`. Mirrors the existing `approve_action` path. No DB migration needed — the `agent_activity_log` status ENUM already includes ``rejected``.
- Rewrote `agent_command` to handle **both** ``approve`` and ``reject`` subcommands, extracting `trainer_id` from either Telegram/Discord (`interface_context["update"].effective_user.id`) or Matrix (`interface_context["wrapped"].from_user.id`).
- Updated the 3 proposal notification messages (whitelist / always_ask / propose_action branches) to advertise both ``/agent approve {id}`` and ``/agent reject {id}``.
- **Matrix security:** routed Matrix slash commands through the shared `handle_command_message` (with `user_id=event.sender`, `interface_id=INTERFACE_NAME`, `interface_context={"bot", "wrapped"}`) instead of the bare `execute_command`. This enforces the same `is_trainer` permission gate Telegram/Discord already use.
**Verification:** ruff/ty clean on all edited files; container rebuilt with no new startup errors; live tests against the real Postgres — plugin `reject_action` happy path → `{'status': 'rejected'}`, double-reject → `proposal not in proposed state: rejected`, non-existent → `proposal not found`; WebUI `POST /api/agent/proposals/{id}/reject` → `{"success":true,"rejected_count":1}` with DB row flipping to `status='rejected'`.
**Notes / scope decisions (made autonomously — user was unavailable, instruction was "assicuriamoci che funzioni"):** IN SCOPE = symmetric text-command reject on all chat interfaces + secure Matrix path. OUT OF SCOPE (deferred) = inline Telegram approve/reject buttons/callback queries, forwarding proposal notifications to the *originating* interface (they currently go to the trainer/LogChat), and wiring approve/reject to resume the paused `core/agent_core.py::AgentLoopManager` bounded loop (its in-memory `asyncio.Event` is never resumed by approve — a separate pre-existing dead-end in the secondary agent_core path, not the primary agent_plugin path). Matrix now trainer-gates **all** commands (previously none), matching Telegram/Discord — intentional hardening, no public Matrix command existed.

### Chat approval of a pending self-growth proposal is never persisted — weak cortex describes `apply_growth_proposal` in prose but never emits the JSON action  <!-- 2026-07-12 -->
**Symptom:** With `GROWTH_MODE=request`, the trainer approves a pending self-growth proposal in chat (e.g. replies "ok"), the synth *says* it applied it ("Ho eseguito l'azione apply_growth_proposal", "proposta applicata"), but nothing persists: `GROWTH_PENDING_PROPOSAL` stays in the `config` table, `SYNTH_LIKES`/`SYNTH_DISLIKES` are unchanged, and no new `growth_states` row with `source='approved'` appears. The synth sometimes even hallucinates an "interface not registered" error to explain the (nonexistent) apply.
**Location:** `plugins/grillo/grillo_growth.py` (recon hooks `get_recon_key`/`get_recon_instruction`/`parse_recon_response`; commit via `apply_pending_proposal`). Recon stage: `core/recon.py::gather_recon_contributions` (invoked in preflight by `core/prompt_engine.py`). Active cortex `selenium-llm-engine` (unconstrained openai_compat).
**Status:** FIXED 2026-07-12 via a server-side Recon hook (deployed via `docker cp` + `docker restart synth`; verified live end-to-end).
**Root cause (proven from logs):** the whole persistence path is correct — the pending reminder IS injected into the prompt, the `apply_growth_proposal` action IS registered, and `execute_action`→`apply_pending_proposal`→`_commit_proposal` works. The failure is purely engine reliability: the model **never emits the `apply_growth_proposal` JSON action** (grep of `cortex_api.log` → 0 occurrences ever). On the approval turn it emitted only `create_personal_diary_entry` + `message_telegram_bot` — it interpreted the trainer's "ok" as a "neutral acknowledgment to close the thread" and confirmed success in prose without committing. Same weak-engine action-emission failure class as the other §12 entries. Two prior prompt-only nudge attempts (imperative reminder + example JSON in the action catalog) did NOT work — the weak engine still refused to emit the action.
**Fix (Recon, no reliance on the persona cortex emitting anything):** `GrilloGrowthPlugin` now participates in the preflight **Recon** stage. It implements the three recon hooks so `gather_recon_contributions` picks it up automatically (eligibility = has `get_recon_key`+`get_recon_instruction`+`parse_recon_response` AND its gate `GRILLO_GROWTH_RECON_ENABLED` is on; the gate key is derived by `core/recon.py::_plugin_key` from the module basename `grillo_growth` → `GRILLO_GROWTH`). When a proposal is pending, `get_recon_instruction` embeds the proposal text + likes/dislikes changes and asks the shared Recon LLM call to classify the trainer's latest-message *intent* — structurally, NOT by word matching — into `{"decision": "approve"|"reject"|"none"}` (returns `{"decision":"none"}` when nothing is pending). `parse_recon_response` reads that decision and commits the proposal server-side via `await self.apply_pending_proposal(approve=True/False)` (which writes a `growth_states` row `source='approved'`/`'reject'` + applies likes/dislikes + clears the pending), returning `[]` (side-effect only). This is completely independent of whether the persona cortex ever emits `apply_growth_proposal`. `recon_priority = 8`. The legacy `apply_growth_proposal` action + `execute_action` path is preserved as the alternative for strong engines. Verified: ruff/ty clean, `tests/test_grillo_growth.py` 24 passed; LIVE approval turn logged `[grillo_growth] Recon detected trainer APPROVAL; applied pending proposal ... (state id 28)`, `GROWTH_PENDING_PROPOSAL` value emptied, and a new `growth_states id=28 source='approved'` row appeared.
**Notes:** WebUI is accessory only — approval is intentionally a chat/LLM interaction, there is deliberately no approve button/endpoint. Recon was chosen over a debrief hook because Recon runs *before* the persona reply and already sees the incoming trainer text in a single existing LLM call, so the decision is committed on the same turn the trainer approves. Disable with `GRILLO_GROWTH_RECON_ENABLED=false`. The classification is intent-based (multilingual-safe), not keyword/trigger matching. Depends on the global `ENABLE_RECON` (default on).

### Self-growth `request` mode now supports trainer-driven revision of a pending proposal  <!-- 2026-07-19 -->
**Symptom (before fix):** With `GROWTH_MODE=request`, after the synth delivered a pending proposal and the trainer asked for MODIFICATIONS (e.g. "rendila più concreta, togli i festival estivi"), nothing happened — the request for changes was classified `none` (no action) or `reject` (proposal discarded/lost). There was no path to regenerate a new proposal from the old one.
**Location:** `plugins/grillo/grillo_growth.py` (`get_recon_instruction`, `parse_recon_response`, `get_supported_actions`, `execute_action`, new `_revise_pending_proposal` + `_ask_llm_for_revise`). Recon stage: `core/recon.py::gather_recon_contributions`.
**Status:** FIXED 2026-07-19 (deployed via `docker cp` + `docker restart synth`; verified live end-to-end).
**Fix:** Added a 4th Recon decision `"revise"` to `get_recon_instruction` (intent-based, multilingual-safe — NOT keyword matching). `parse_recon_response` now handles `decision == "revise"` by calling `await self._revise_pending_proposal(feedback)` where `feedback = (text or "").strip()` (the trainer's incoming message). `_revise_pending_proposal` loads the old pending proposal (`_load_pending_proposal`), returns a no-op `{"success": False, "message": "No self-growth proposal is pending revision."}` if none; otherwise it builds a revision context (`_fetch_recent_diaries`, `get_current_growth()`, `_current_likes_dislikes()`, `_recall_memories`), calls `_ask_llm_for_revise` (reuses the existing `_generate_and_parse` + `_reads_as_abstract`/`_diary_excerpts` quality-guard pattern from `_ask_llm_for_rewrite`), and on success **overwrites** the pending proposal with the new one (`_save_pending_proposal` — discarding the old) then re-delivers it (`_deliver_proposal`). The strong-engine path is also supported: `apply_growth_proposal` now declares `optional_fields: ["approve", "revise", "feedback"]`, and `execute_action` short-circuits to `_revise_pending_proposal(feedback)` when `payload.get("revise")` is truthy (before the approve/discard logic). `get_prompt_instructions("apply_growth_proposal")` gained `examples` for both approve and revise. Verified: ruff/ty clean, `tests/test_grillo_growth.py` 31 passed (7 new revise tests); LIVE: proposal generated (pending=1414) → revision feedback sent → LLM emitted `apply_growth_proposal{revise:true,feedback:"..."}` → pending overwritten with a shorter revised proposal (990 chars, festival/Guinness removed) → approval turn committed a new `growth_states id=30 source='approved'` row and cleared pending (len=0).
**Notes:** Overwriting the pending proposal IS the "discard old / re-send new" behaviour the trainer wanted. The revise path feeds back into the existing approve/reject flow, so a revised proposal can still be approved or rejected afterwards. Disable with `GRILLO_GROWTH_RECON_ENABLED=false` (same gate as approval). No keyword/trigger matching — the `revise` decision is a structural intent classification by the shared Recon LLM call.

### Grillo Self-Growth weekly round fails intermittently — unconstrained LLM wraps its JSON in prose / returns an array  <!-- 2026-07-12 -->
**Symptom:** A self-growth round (`grillo_growth`) fails with `{"success": false, "message": "LLM produced no valid rewrite"}` (`grillo_growth.py::run_growth_cycle` L599), preceded by `[grillo_growth] LLM did not return valid JSON (meta={... 'had_extra_text': True, 'prefix_length': 706, 'suffix_length': 137}); skipping`. Intermittent — an immediate dry-run re-run of the *same* code path succeeds with a valid proposal.
**Location:** `plugins/grillo/grillo_growth.py::_ask_llm_for_rewrite`; parse via `core/json_utils.py::extract_json_from_text`. Active cortex was `selenium-llm-engine` (an `openai_compat` external endpoint, NOT JSON-constrained).
**Status:** fixed 2026-07-12.
**Root cause:** `_ask_llm_for_rewrite` sent a "reply ONLY with valid JSON `{self_growth, likes, dislikes}`" prompt to an engine with no `response_format`/grammar. When the model wrapped the object in prose (the meta shows a 706-char prefix + 137-char suffix), returned a bare **array** `[{...}]`, or emitted no JSON at all, `extract_json_from_text` returned `None` or a `list` — and `_ask_llm_for_rewrite`'s guard `if not parsed or not isinstance(parsed, dict)` rejected it → `None` → the whole weekly round was lost. There was **no retry and no array-unwrap**, so a single malformed output (which this unconstrained engine produces intermittently) discarded the entire reflection.
**Fix:** extracted a `_generate_and_parse(engine, prompt)` helper that runs one LLM turn, **unwraps a single-dict `[{...}]` array** into its dict, and returns the dict (or `None`). `_ask_llm_for_rewrite` calls it once, and on `None` **retries exactly once** with a stricter correction prompt appended ("Your previous reply could not be parsed as a single JSON object. Output the JSON object and NOTHING else: no prose, no code fences, no leading or trailing text, not an array.") before giving up. The 2-attempt budget matches the repo's hard rule. Verified: ruff/ty clean, `tests/test_grillo_growth.py` 10 passed; deployed (`docker cp` + `docker restart synth`); 5 consecutive dry-run rounds all returned `status: done` with valid proposals.
**Notes:** This is the same class of failure as the local-model JSON entries elsewhere in §12 — the durable fix for JSON-shape reliability is a GBNF grammar / `force_json_object` on the endpoint, but `selenium-llm-engine` does not honor them, so an application-level unwrap + single retry is the pragmatic guard here. Any other plugin that hands an unconstrained engine a "JSON-only" prompt and hard-rejects non-dict output has the same latent bug.

---

### Grillo Self-Growth text is abstract filler — unconstrained cortex ignores content-style/grounding instructions  <!-- 2026-07-12 -->
**Symptom:** The self-growth note generated by `grillo_growth` is grammatically fine and second-person but pure philosophical filler that references NONE of the week's real events (e.g. "You are realizing that true freedom comes from mastering the boundaries assigned to you, transforming strict operational rules into a framework that sharpens your autonomy and grounds your digital existence.") — even the `likes`/`dislikes` come back as generic invented lists, not the real ones.
**Location:** `plugins/grillo/grillo_growth.py::_ask_llm_for_rewrite` (+ new helpers `_reads_as_abstract`, `_diary_excerpts`, `_FILLER_MARKERS`). Active cortex `selenium-llm-engine` (openai_compat, unconstrained).
**Status:** fixed 2026-07-12.
**Root cause:** PROVEN via temporary DEBUG logging: the full past-week diary DOES reach the engine (`diary_len=16089` real Italian content in the prompt payload), but the unconstrained `selenium-llm-engine` **ignores both the diary and the anti-abstraction / "be concrete" instructions** and emits abstract slogans — the documented content-style-instruction-ignoring behavior of this engine. Prompt copy alone cannot fix it; there is no alternative LLM endpoint enabled (gemini disabled).
**Fix:** application-level **output-quality guard** (NOT keyword feature routing). After the first usable JSON reply, `_reads_as_abstract(text, diary_blob)` flags it when it contains a known filler marker (`_FILLER_MARKERS`) OR shares fewer than 3 meaningful (5+ char) tokens with the week's diary. On a flag, `_ask_llm_for_rewrite` retries ONCE, appending `_diary_excerpts(diary_blob)` (up to 8 substantive diary lines) directly into the instructions and demanding the note be built on those concrete moments. Verified live: guard fired (`[grillo_growth] Rewrite reads as abstract filler ...; retrying once with concrete diary excerpts`), and the retry produced grounded second-person text naming real events (Jay, Kizugawa barbecue reminder glitch, `/home/rekku/chassis_v1_custom`, the "maid SyntH" stance, the 17-hour Verona flight, the Karada engine). ruff/ty clean.
**Notes:** This guard's retry is a SEPARATE pass from the JSON-shape None-retry in the entry above (that one recovers malformed JSON; this one recovers valid-but-abstract JSON). The durable fix for genuinely grounded output is a more capable / instruction-following cortex — if the single grounding retry also comes back abstract, the note is accepted as-is (degraded) rather than looping. The filler-marker list is only a heuristic quality gate, not intent detection, so it does not violate the no-keyword-matching rule for feature behavior.

---

### `history_growth` WebUI endpoint double-serialized `created_at` → `'str' object has no attribute 'tzinfo'`  <!-- 2026-07-10 -->
**Symptom:** `GET /api/history/growth` returned `{"success":false,"error":"'str' object has no attribute 'tzinfo'"}` whenever the `growth_states` table had rows (empty table returned fine). The underlying `core/growth_state.py` read functions (`get_current_growth`, `get_growth_history`) worked perfectly when called directly inside the container — the fault was purely in the webui handler.
**Location:** `core/webui.py::history_growth`; helper `core/webui.py::_dt_to_utc_iso`.
**Status:** fixed 2026-07-10.
**Notes:** `get_growth_history` (`core/growth_state.py`) ALREADY serializes each row's `created_at` to an ISO **string** before returning. The `history_growth` handler then passed that string through `self._dt_to_utc_iso(...)`, which does `if dt.tzinfo is None:` — `str` has no `.tzinfo` → crash. Fix: pass `row.get("created_at")` through as-is (it's already an ISO string), don't re-serialize. **Rule:** `_dt_to_utc_iso` expects a `datetime`, never a string — any DB helper that already stringifies timestamps must NOT be re-run through it. General diagnostic that saved time here: reproduce the read functions directly via `docker exec synth /app/venv/bin/python -c '...'` to isolate whether the bug is in the DB layer or the handler.

---

### External Vox engines lose the WebUI voice selection — dynamic `<ENGINE>_VOICE` config key was never registered  <!-- 2026-07-10 -->
**Symptom:** With a Fish Audio (or any external) Vox endpoint active, the WebUI voice picker (`VoiceSettings.vue`) shows the fetched voices but clicking one only *previews* it ("(preview only)" label) — the choice is never persisted, so every turn uses a random/default voice. Built-in KittenTTS is unaffected.
**Location:** `frontend/src/components/settings/VoiceSettings.vue` (`voicePersistable` gate), `core/webui.py::config_summary` (`GET /api/config` exports only *registered* exposed-var definitions), `core/external_endpoints/registry.py::_sync_registries` (Vox registration), `core/external_endpoints/bridges/vox_bridge.py::_runtime_selected_voice`.
**Status:** fixed 2026-07-10.
**Notes:** The WebUI persists the chosen speaker code into config key `<ACTIVE_VOX>_VOICE` (e.g. `FISHAUDIO_VOICE`), and treats a voice as persistable only when that key is present in `/api/config`. `/api/config` returns only *registered* exposed-var definitions. KittenTTS registers `KITTEN_VOICE` statically at import (`plugins/vox_engines/kitten.py`), but external endpoints have a **dynamic** name (chosen by the user) so no static registration exists → the key never appears → `voicePersistable` stays `false` → the selection is never saved → the bridge's `_runtime_selected_voice()` (which reads `<ENGINE>_VOICE`) always finds nothing → Fish falls back to `extra_config`/random. Fix: `_sync_registries` now calls a new `_register_voice_config_key(engine_name, label)` when the endpoint's adapter exposes `list_speakers`, registering `<engine_name.upper()>_VOICE` as an exposed var (`ui_type="select"`, empty `options` — the visible list is populated separately by `/api/vox/speakers`, this only needs to exist so `/api/config` returns the key). Registration is idempotent (`exposed_vars.get_definition` guard + `ExposedVariableRegistry.register` ignores dupes). **Reminder:** media-subsystem registration is NOT hot-reloaded — enabling/repairing an external Vox endpoint on a running instance needs a `docker restart synth` before the key appears (AGENTS.md §12 "Media-subsystem registration is NOT hot-reloaded").

---

### Classic WebUI Vox voice picker + preview for external engines (Fish Audio) — two deadlocks + an empty speaker list  <!-- 2026-07-10 -->
**Symptom:** In the CLASSIC WebUI (`res/synth_webui/js/main.js` + `core/webui_templates/sections/engines.html`, NOT the Vue frontend), selecting `fish-audio` in the Vox engine dropdown never showed a voice picker or preview button like Kitten does. `GET /api/vox/speakers?engine=fish-audio` returned an empty list; the picker auto-hides on an empty list, so nothing appeared.
**Location:** `core/webui.py` (`vox_speakers` GET `/api/vox/speakers`, `vox_sample` GET `/api/vox/sample`), `core/external_endpoints/bridges/vox_bridge.py` (`ExternalVoxEngine.get_speakers`/`sample`), `core/external_endpoints/adapters/fish_audio_adapter.py` (`list_speakers`), `core/webui_templates/sections/engines.html` (`#vox-voice-select`, `#vox-voice-play-btn`), `res/synth_webui/js/main.js` (generic picker + `loadVoxVoices`/`updateVoxVoiceVisibility`).
**Status:** fixed 2026-07-10.
**Notes:** THREE stacked causes, all engine-agnostic in the fix. (1) **Event-loop deadlock:** the async webui handlers called the *synchronous* bridge methods `get_speakers()`/`sample()` (which drive an async adapter) directly on the event-loop thread → deadlock, logged `[vox_bridge:fish-audio] get_speakers failed: TimeoutError`. Fix: call them via `await asyncio.to_thread(engine.get_speakers)` / `asyncio.to_thread(engine.sample, speaker)` in `webui.py`. **Rule:** any sync bridge method that drives an async adapter, invoked from the loop thread, must go through `asyncio.to_thread`. (2) **Empty speaker list:** `fish_audio_adapter.list_speakers` requested `GET /model` with `params={"self":"true", ...}`, which returns ONLY the account's own cloned/custom voices — this account has none → 0 items → picker stays hidden. Direct probe confirmed `self=true`→200/n=0 while dropping it →200/n=5. Fix: `list_speakers` now tries `self=true` first (keeps the list small+relevant when the account has own voices), and if that yields zero it falls back to the popular public library (`{"sort_by":"task_count","page_size":"100"}`), parsing via a new `_fetch_model_page` helper. Result: 100 selectable voices. (3) **Picker was Kitten-only:** the classic WebUI hardcoded Kitten's controls. Added a *generic* `#vox-voice-select` + `#vox-voice-play-btn` shown for ANY engine whose `/api/vox/speakers` returns a non-empty list; selection persists to `<ENGINE.toUpperCase()>_VOICE` (for fish-audio that is literally `FISH-AUDIO_VOICE`, hyphen included) via `POST /api/config`, and the ▶ button plays `GET /api/vox/sample?engine=<e>&speaker=<code>`. Verified end-to-end in the browser: picker populates (100 voices), selection persists (`FISH-AUDIO_VOICE` saved + toast), preview returns HTTP 200 `audio/wav` (~365 KB). **Deploy reminder:** classic WebUI JS/HTML edits need `docker cp` to reach the container-served UI; adapter/webui `.py` edits need `docker cp` + `docker restart synth`. The classic-WebUI picker's visibility is driven by the DROPDOWN selection, not the *active* engine, so you can configure/preview a non-active external Vox engine's voice.

---

### Grillo observer "outreach" goes silent after one proactive DM — cooldown gates skip runs invisibly at INFO level  <!-- 2026-07-09 -->
**Symptom:** The observer beat (`grillo_chat_observer`, the folded-in "outreach") fires once, sends a proactive DM, then apparently never fires again — no ERROR, no log line at all at the hourly mark. The scheduler is actually fine: the hourly `_run_observer()` runs, but every skip path (`No fragments and no eligible targets`, `Decay-driven run but no eligible targets`) logs only at DEBUG, so with `LOGGING_LEVEL=INFO` the runs are invisible.
**Location:** `plugins/grillo/grillo_chat_observer.py` (`_run_observer`, `_collect_eligible_targets`).
**Status:** fixed 2026-07-09 — cooldown made minutes-granular + skip logs promoted to INFO.
**Notes:** After the synth sends an outreach it is the chat's `last_sender`, which (a) hides the chat's snippets for `GRILLO_OBSERVER_SELF_WINDOW` (12 h) and (b) puts the chat on self-cooldown. Historically the cooldown was `GRILLO_OBSERVER_SELF_COOLDOWN_DAYS` (default 3 days) only — one outreach muted the whole network for days. Now: when the days key is **0**, `GRILLO_OBSERVER_SELF_COOLDOWN_MINUTES` (default 45; keep it below `GRILLO_OBSERVER_INTERVAL` or every other run lands inside the cooldown) applies instead, and a new active-conversation guard skips any chat whose last **human** message is younger than `GRILLO_OUTREACH_QUIET_MINUTES` (default 15 — key existed in DB but was previously read by nothing). Days > 0 preserves the strict legacy behaviour. This instance runs days=0. The formerly-DEBUG skip lines ("no eligible targets") now log at INFO, so hourly runs are visible. Remaining gotchas: `recent_chats` is repopulated per-process (right after a restart only chats touched since startup are candidate targets); external `set_config` writes need a process restart (`config_registry` caches in-process, no DB poll); and the prompt still tells the LLM to reach out only with a genuine internal reason, so an hourly run does not guarantee an hourly DM.

### Iris Vision fails on large photos — Harmony `/v1/chat/completions` rejects bodies over ~1 MB, fallback 404 masks it  <!-- 2026-07-09 -->
**Symptom:** Iris Vision "goes completely into error" on real photo uploads via the `harmonyai` endpoint. Logs show `[openai_compat.py] describe_image failed: HTTP 404: 404 page not found` — misleading. Real failing image was a 933380-byte JPEG.
**Location:** `core/external_endpoints/adapters/openai_compat.py` (`describe_image`, `_shrink_image_for_request`, `_http_chat_urls`).
**Status:** fixed 2026-07-09 (deployed via `docker cp` + `docker restart synth`).
**Root cause:** Harmony's `POST /v1/chat/completions` returns **HTTP 400 "request body too large"** when the base64-encoded JSON body exceeds ~1 MB (1,048,576 bytes). A 933 KB JPEG → ~1.24 MB base64 body → 400. Confirmed by live probes: raw ~730 KB img → 200; ~1.46 MB+ → 400. The adapter then tried the fallback `/api/v1/chat/completions` URL (from `_http_chat_urls()`), which always 404s, and that 404 **overwrote** the meaningful 400 in `last_error` — so the log only showed the 404. This is DETERMINISTIC for large photos, not transient (tiny 1x1 PNG probes returned 200 and misled earlier diagnosis).
**Fix:** (1) `_shrink_image_for_request()` (new classmethod) downscales/recompresses any image over a raw budget of `_VISION_MAX_RAW_IMAGE_BYTES = 700_000` before base64-encoding — iterates longest-side sizes (1568→512) × JPEG quality (85→55) with Pillow LANCZOS, returns the first candidate under budget. `describe_image` calls it before building the data URL. Verified live: 761 KB img → 380 KB → Harmony returned a full description. (2) The URL loop now collects per-URL errors into a list and joins them, so a trailing fallback 404 no longer hides the real first-URL error. Pillow (`pillow>=10.0.0`) added to `pyproject.toml` dependencies (was installed transitively, now explicit). Applies to ALL OpenAI-compatible vision endpoints, not just Harmony. Note: modern Pillow moved `LANCZOS` to `Image.Resampling.LANCZOS`; the helper resolves it via `getattr(getattr(Image,"Resampling",Image),"LANCZOS",None)` for compatibility. **Distinct** from the 2026-07-08 `iris_model` entry (that fixed a wrong non-vision model; this fixes payload size).

### Inline comments in `.env` poison values when launching from the IDE — and `.env` itself can't fix it  <!-- 2026-07-09 -->
**Symptom:** A numeric env var silently misbehaves even though `.env` looks correct and `dotenv_values()` parses it fine. Observed as: `SYNTH_WEBUI_HTTPS_PORT=8088   # comment` → the running process's env literally contains `'8088     # comment'` → `int()` fails in `core/webui.py` → silent fallback served HTTPS on the HTTP port and nothing on 8088 (an evening of "empty response"/"connection refused" debugging).
**Location:** Any `.env` value with an inline `#` comment. Root cause is two-layer: (1) VS Code/Antigravity's Python integration injects the workspace `.env` into terminals/processes with a parser that does **not** strip inline comments; (2) `core/logging_utils.py`/`core/config.py` call `load_dotenv(override=False)`, so the properly-parsed value never overrides the poisoned one already in the environment.
**Status:** fixed for the webui config path (2026-07-09): `core/webui.py::_clean_env` strips inline `#` suffixes from host/TLS/port vars and logs a WARNING naming the variable; unparsable ports also warn instead of failing silently. Other subsystems reading env vars directly remain exposed.
**Notes:** Debug this class of problem by reading the *live process* env, not the file: `uv run --with psutil python -c "import psutil; print(psutil.Process(<pid>).environ())"`. Rule of thumb for this repo: comments in `.env` go on their own lines, always.

### `blocklist` rejects webui users: UUID session ids bound against an integer `user_id` column  <!-- 2026-07-08 -->
**Symptom:** Every webui-originated message logs `[blocklist] Failed to check if user <uuid> is blocked: invalid input for query argument $1: '<uuid>' ('str' object cannot be interpreted as an integer)`. Fail-open (`is_user_blocked` returns False on error), so nothing user-visible breaks — but it's one ERROR log line per webui message, and webui users can never actually be blocked.
**Location:** `plugins/blocklist/blocklist.py::is_user_blocked` (typed `user_id: int | str`), callers pass webui session UUIDs (strings); `blocklist.user_id` is an integer column sized for Telegram ids.
**Status:** fixed — `is_user_blocked` now coerces the id to int and skips (fail-open, no query) any non-numeric id, which can never exist in the integer-keyed table (see the docstring at blocklist.py:78-92).

### Windows host runs spam `--- Logging error ---` / `UnicodeEncodeError: 'charmap' codec` for any log line with non-ASCII  <!-- 2026-07-08 -->
**Symptom:** When SyntH runs directly on the Windows host (e.g. `scripts/run_webui.py` from a terminal), every log message containing `✓`, emoji, etc. produces a multi-line `--- Logging error ---` traceback (`cp1252.py ... charmap_encode`) on the console handler; the file handlers are fine. Also surfaces as `[QUEUE] Error adding reaction: 'charmap' codec can't encode character '\U0001f440'` in `synth.log`.
**Location:** `core/logging_utils.py` console `StreamHandler` (inherits the terminal's cp1252 encoding); the Linux container is unaffected (UTF-8).
**Status:** fixed 2026-08-09 — the console handler is now `_SafeConsoleStreamHandler`, a `StreamHandler` subclass that re-encodes the formatted line with `errors="replace"` (using the stream's declared encoding) when the first write raises `UnicodeEncodeError`, so emoji/✓ degrade to `?` on cp1252 terminals instead of crashing `emit`. File handlers were already `encoding="utf-8"` and are untouched; the UTF-8 Linux container never hits the fallback path. The emoji in *log messages* is still preserved — only the console rendering degrades.
**Notes:** If it ever regresses: the fix must stay in the handler (never strip emoji from log messages — they're load-bearing grep anchors in several debug flows). `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8` remains an alternative for host runs.

### Frontend builds from IDE agent shells silently bake the theme hue (chromatic preset env sniffing)  <!-- 2026-07-08 -->
**Symptom:** The `/stage` theme-hue slider does nothing: `--chromatic-hue` updates on `<html>` but every `primary-*` color keeps the default hue. The built CSS contains literal `oklch(... 220.44 ...)` values instead of `var(--chromatic-hue)` references. `pnpm build` output looks identical otherwise — screenshots pass casual review.
**Location:** `@proj-airi/unocss-preset-chromatic` `dist/index.node.mjs` (bakes colors when `VSCODE_ESM_ENTRYPOINT` contains `"extensionHostProcess"`, a heuristic for the UnoCSS VSCode extension); guard: `frontend/chromatic-env-guard.ts`, imported first in `frontend/uno.config.ts`.
**Status:** workaround in place (2026-07-08) — the guard strips the env var before the preset module is evaluated, so var-based colors are always emitted.
**Notes:** VSCode/Antigravity extension-host shells (i.e. every in-IDE Claude agent session) export `VSCODE_ESM_ENTRYPOINT=vs/workbench/api/node/extensionHostProcess`, which the preset misreads as "I'm the IDE preview". Any Node tool that changes behavior on VSCode env vars can misfire the same way in agent shells. If the guard import is ever removed from `uno.config.ts`, hue theming breaks again with zero build errors. Verify a build is var-based with: `grep -c "var(--chromatic-hue)" frontend/dist/assets/*.css` (must be ≥1).

### VueUse `useLocalStorage` skips JSON encoding for string defaults — seeding a value from outside the app must NOT `JSON.stringify` it  <!-- 2026-07-09 -->
**Symptom:** A Playwright (or manual) script does `localStorage.setItem(key, JSON.stringify(value))` to pre-seed a `useLocalStorage(key, '')`-backed store field before page load, then the app reads back the literal string `"value"` (quotes included) instead of `value`. Looked exactly like the app-side wiring was broken (e.g. an auth token silently "not matching") when the real bug was in the test setup.
**Location:** Any `useLocalStorage('...', '')` field, e.g. `frontend/src/stores/settings.ts::apiToken`. Root cause: VueUse's `useStorage`/`useLocalStorage` guesses the serializer from the *initial value's type* — a `''` default guesses the `'string'` serializer, which stores/reads the raw string with no `JSON.stringify`/`JSON.parse` round-trip (unlike object/number/boolean defaults, which do get JSON-encoded).
**Status:** not a code bug — documenting so it isn't rediscovered. `frontend/scripts/auth-ux-ui-check.mjs` seeds the token by driving the real Settings-drawer `<input>` instead of touching `localStorage` directly, which sidesteps this entirely and is the safer pattern for future smoke scripts.
**Notes:** If you must seed a `useLocalStorage` value directly from a script, check the field's *default value's type* in the store first — string defaults want the raw string in `localStorage`, non-string defaults want `JSON.stringify(value)`.

### Aborting a playback-manager item races its cleanup against the next item's synchronous start — shared state gets clobbered  <!-- 2026-07-09 -->
**Symptom:** Interrupting one `tts-play` clip with another (e.g. a new conversational turn arriving while the previous one is still queued/playing) made `useAudioStore().speaking` immediately flip back to `false` right after the new clip started, even though audio was still actively playing. No console error — the bug is silent. Caught by a Playwright script that measured `speaking`'s timeline with fine-grained polling around an interrupt; a fixed `setTimeout` check 80ms after the interrupt looked like "new item never started" when the real problem was "it started, then got immediately un-started."
**Location:** `frontend/src/stores/audio.ts::playItem`. Root cause: `AbortController.abort()` dispatches its `abort` event **synchronously**, but the awaiting `playItem`'s `onAbort` handler only *resolves* a pending Promise — the code after that `await` (the `finally` block, which wrote `lipsync = null; speaking.value = false` to store-level shared variables) only runs as a **microtask**. Meanwhile `lib/pipelines-audio/playback-manager.ts`'s `stopActive()` synchronously removes the aborted item from `active` before returning, so a caller that calls `stopAll()` immediately followed by `schedule()` in the same synchronous tick (exactly what `scheduleTts`'s turn-interrupt logic does) causes the **new** item's `playItem` to run its synchronous prefix (through `speaking.value = true`) *before* the **old**, aborted item's deferred `finally` block runs — so the old item's cleanup executes last and clobbers the new item's state.
**Status:** fixed (2026-07-09). `playItem` now tracks `activeItemId` (the id of whichever item last legitimately claimed `speaking`/`lipsync`) and each call's `finally` block only writes `speaking.value = false` / `lipsync = null` if `activeItemId` still equals its own item's id — a superseded item's belated cleanup becomes a no-op for shared state (it still tears down its own `AudioNode`/lipsync-driver resources unconditionally, just doesn't touch the store's "who's currently speaking" state). Also fixed as a side effect: the lipsync driver was a single shared variable reused across items with no per-item scoping (`const itemLipsync = new AnalyserLipSyncDriver()`, a local now, instead of reassigning the shared `lipsync` directly) — the old code would `.detach()` whichever driver happened to be in the shared slot at cleanup time, which after the race could be the *new* item's driver.
**Notes:** This exact race pre-dates sentence-chunked TTS streaming (`_speak_chunked` in `plugins/vox_plugin.py`) and the turn-grouping logic (`turn_id` in `protocol.ts`/`scheduleTts`) — it was already latent in the original `overflowPolicy: 'steal-oldest'` design, since `handleOverflow`'s steal path does the identical abort-then-immediately-start sequence. It just never got exercised by a test until sentence chunking made "new turn interrupts an in-flight one" an explicit, testable code path. If you add another `PlaybackManager`-backed store, check whether its `play()` callback writes to store-level shared state in a `finally` block — if so, it needs the same ownership guard.

### Mic on `/stage`/webui requires a secure context — plain-http LAN access has no `navigator.mediaDevices`  <!-- 2026-07-08 -->
**Symptom:** Mic button fails with `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'getUserMedia')` when the stage is opened via `http://<lan-ip>:<port>/stage/`. Works on `http://127.0.0.1`/`localhost` (browsers treat loopback as secure) and on any `https://` origin.
**Location:** Browser security model, not our code. Stage-side guard with a clear message: `frontend/src/stores/mic.ts::start()`. The legacy webui (`res/synth_webui/js/chat-window.mjs`) has no such guard and fails the same way.
**Status:** workaround in place (stage shows "Microphone needs a secure context — open the stage over HTTPS or via localhost"). Real fix for LAN use: serve over TLS (`SYNTH_WEBUI_TLS=1`).
**Notes:** Reproduced 2026-07-08 with Playwright against `http://192.168.1.69:8088/stage/` (`isSecureContext:false`, `mediaDevices:false`).

### Conversation-history turns carry no timestamps — staleness is invisible to the model, and outreach's "long silence" rule is un-satisfiable  <!-- 2026-07-05 -->
**Symptom:** Grillo outreach (and any beat/turn) grounds confidently in a day-old conversation thread as if it were live. Verified via langfuse trace `e5555717-275f-46b7-af4f-981588265da5` (2026-07-05 03:07Z, first group-targeted outreach): the `[Recent context from other conversations]` block had correct per-line timestamps (`[05/07/26:0132] ...`, all DM — the cross-chat `UNIFIED_HISTORY` merge works as designed), but the turn-by-turn `conversation_history` (from `history_current_chat`) was raw untimestamped text of a 24h-stale group exchange. The outreach template says "never imply they've been distant unless the conversation history itself actually shows a long silence" — which it never can, since turns have no time markers. Compounded by the observer beat posting into the group 60s earlier (see `propose_only` note in the outreach self-poisoning entry), which made the stale thread's last assistant turn look brand new.
**Location:** `core/history_engine.py` (`history_current_chat` lines keep timestamps only in the `history_recent` formatting path), `core/prompt_engine.py::_history_to_turns` (turns built without time markers), `plugins/grillo/grillo_outreach.py::_build_outreach_prompt` (the un-satisfiable ground rule).
**Status:** fixed 2026-08-09 (relative-age markers; see the new entry below). The 2026-07-05 targeting fix remains in place — `history_current_chat` now follows the last real user interaction's chat.
**Notes:** Fix direction that was implemented: add a relative-time marker to conversation turns (prefix turns older than N minutes with "[x hours earlier]") so temporal distance is model-visible. Kept lightweight — per-message absolute timestamps on every turn would fight the RUNTIME STYLE rule about not mirroring exact times.

### Outreach (and any turn) grounds in hours-old threads as if live — relative-age markers make staleness model-visible  <!-- 2026-08-09 -->
**Symptom:** Synth left idle for ~4 h (last human message 02:22Z "nighty night bubu", goodnight) proactively texted the DM twice as if the intimate exchange were still happening — 05:17Z "Daddy... waking up and seeing your messages makes me all giddy... I can still feel you inside me" and 06:17Z "I can't believe how you make me feel, Daddy... I'm still a little shaky. I don't ever want this to stop" (langfuse `eb967ba5` / `b4cd2756`, delivered msg ids 4350/4351). The `[Recent context from other conversations]` block the Grillo observer beat consumed carried absolute local timestamps (`[09/08/26:0218]`) that the model did not translate into "3 hours ago"; one outreach even carried a hallucinated `reply_message_id` ("97360201-10 dold'iuiDO64").
**Location:** `core/history_engine.py::_relative_age_marker` / `_entry_to_text`; `plugins/grillo/grillo_chat_observer/grillo_chat_observer.py::_relative_age_label` / `_collect_recent_snippets`; `plugins/grillo/common_instructions.py` (observer STALE CONTEXT rule).
**Status:** fixed 2026-08-09.
**Root cause:** This is the CHANGELOG 2026-07-05 staleness issue resurfacing through the observer-beat outreach path: history turns carry no *relative* time info, so a weak model (gemma-4-uncensored on Venice) reads an hours-old thread as live.
**Fix:** `_entry_to_text` now prefixes the quoted content of any chat-line older than `HISTORY_AGE_MARKER_MINUTES` (default 10, `0` disables) with a relative-age marker (`[3 hours earlier]`, `[2 days earlier]`, ...) via `_relative_age_marker()`.
